# Changelog

All notable changes to Forge will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **A set-up page for contributors, a page on making a release, a page on embedding Forge, and the library's own front page** (roadmap A9-ROLES). [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](CONTRIBUTING.md) now says what to install (Rust and its MSRV, the `wasm32-unknown-unknown` target and `wasm-bindgen-cli`, Node 22, `markdownlint-cli2`, Gnumeric, LibreOffice 24.8, R, Python for the games) and which `make` target uses each; [`docs/MAINTAINING.md`](docs/MAINTAINING.md) says what happens when a version reaches `main` (the tag, the five builds, the checksums and attestations, crates.io, Homebrew, Studio) and what a release changes; [`docs/EMBEDDING.md`](docs/EMBEDDING.md) shows the four ways in (the library, `forge serve`, `forge-wasm`, `forge mcp`); and `src/lib.rs`, the docs.rs page, no longer opens "YAML formula calculator with Excel-style arrays": it says what Forge is and runs a model from a string as a doctest. `validation/repo/src/roles.rs` holds each page to the files that state its claims, and sends EMBEDDING.md's REST request to a running `forge serve`. It found a link in CONTRIBUTING.md to a file that had become a folder (`src/cli/commands/examples.rs`). `src/lib.rs` is now among the places `doc_counts` checks for the function count.
- **A Studio link that carries a model after the #** (ADR-080, roadmap A7-STUDIO-LINK). `https://forge.mollendorff.ai/#name=bagel.yaml&gzip=…&name=costs.yaml&yaml=…` opens the files in Studio: `yaml=` is a file's text percent-encoded, as an AI or a person writes it, and `gzip=` its text gzipped in base64url, a third as long, as Files' new Copy writes it for the open model. The part of an address after `#` is never sent to a server, so a linked model reaches only the tab that opens it. Studio removes the link from the address bar once read, asks before it replaces files of your own or an edited example, and names what is wrong with a link it cannot read, keeping what was open. The page now has a Content Security Policy: only its own scripts run and it can reach only its own address, so a model's text, drawn as text, could not send anything elsewhere even if it got past an escape. An address with `?example=` over files of your own, or an example you edited, now asks before it replaces them, as a link does; it used to replace them without a word. `studio/tests/link.mjs` opens the mortgage, the bagel shop and the starter model from links and finds every result equal to `forge calculate`'s, and fails if any request to the server carries part of a model.
- **Five Gnumeric cases for `DDB` with a period or a life under 1** (`validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_date_finance_sweep.yaml`). Forge reads a period after 0 and before 1 as the first period, and a life under 1 as taking all down to the salvage; Gnumeric 1.12.56 gives the same on each case (200, 33,485.964492, 900, 0 and 20.2425109464), so CI now holds it, where only a unit test did. Excel for iOS gives the same on all five (2026-11-24, to 0.000001; `DDB(1000, 100, 10, 0.5)` is 200), so the cases are Excel's as well as Gnumeric's. LibreOffice 24.2 answers Err:502 for a period under 1, and is the engine that differs.
- **What moves a game, on its card, and an included file folded** (ADR-076 decision 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). `game` answers each game's `inputs`: every scalar without a formula, or with a distribution, that its payoffs, dynamics or tournament read, followed through formulas, a tournament's round, the Bayesian network and into included files (`src/core/array_calculator/inputs.rs`). The bagel's price war is moved by 13: 5 of `price_war.yaml` and 8 of `bagel.yaml` and `costs.yaml`, such as `@plan.assumptions.price_per_dozen`. Studio draws them as input cards under "what moves it" on the game card, the same cards as in their groups, and an included file's groups are folded under one line (`from bagel.yaml: 4 groups, 1 table`), so the price war and its inputs fit on two screens. `is_distribution` moved from `edit` to `monte_carlo`, where the calculator reads it too.
- **A game of players alike has its payoffs changed on its card in Studio** (ADR-076 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). The count form writes one payoff for each strategy (`pg.payoffs.keep`) over every column of its table; under the table each strategy has a button, shown while "change this game" is open, that changes it with `set_formula` or `set_value` (the engine already took these paths). The row changes in every column, and Forge's refusal is shown as it words it (`studio/tests/gameedit.mjs`, on the public goods game).
- **`set_habit`: the habit a tournament's player plays is changed with `edit`** (ADR-075, ADR-076 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). `forge edit --set-habit rivalry.players.copycat=random`, `forge_edit`'s `set_habit` with `plays`, and forge-wasm's `edit` write the habit where the file has it (`copycat: tit_for_tat`, or the `plays:` of `{plays: random, cooperate: 0.5}`), in the file's quotes, and read the model back with exactly that habit changed. A chance the player has stays, so Forge refuses a habit that does not take it (`random` takes `cooperate`, `generous_tit_for_tat` `forgive`) and says so; so does a roster two tournaments share. Every habit of every player of every example's tournaments was changed and back: 112 byte for byte, 95 refused for those reasons.
- **A workflow that checks a repository of models on every pull request, and the guide's page about it** (roadmap A6-GUIDE-PIPELINE). [`docs/guide/pipeline.md`](docs/guide/pipeline.md) shows `forge validate` as the gate, the exit codes (0, 1 and 2), `forge report --format json` and its `uncalculated` list, and what a pull request's diff shows. [`examples/pipeline/forge.yml`](examples/pipeline/forge.yml) fails a pull request whose stored results are out of date or that adds a formula without its result, and shows the results on the run's page; it has run, green and red, in a repository of its own, and the page links the runs.
- **`AGENTS.md` holds the rules for every coding agent; `CLAUDE.md` imports it** (roadmap A11-AGENTS-MD). The rules that were in `CLAUDE.md` (100% coverage, nothing switched off, tests inline) are now where Codex, Cursor, Gemini CLI and the others look for them, and `CLAUDE.md` is the one line `@AGENTS.md`. A check in `validation/repo` fails if the import goes or a rule is in both files.
- **A tournament's length, noise and players' chances, and a game's dynamics, are changed with `edit` and on their cards in Studio** (ADR-075, ADR-076 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). `set_value` and `set_formula` take `rivalry.tournament.noise`, `.rounds` or `.continuation`, `axelrod.tournament.players.rand.cooperate`, `price_war.dynamics.generations`, `.baseline` and `.start.hold`, as they take a payoff. A `noise` or a `baseline` the file leaves at its default is added, after the last key of its block (or in its braces). `forge game` answers each game's `settings`: the name of each of these numbers as `edit` takes it, with its formula or its number. In Studio, "change this game" shows each of them under the game, with a tap to change it.
- **A game's payoff is changed with `edit`, and on its card in Studio** (ADR-075, ADR-076 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). `set_formula` and `set_value` take a payoff cell: `stag_hunt.payoffs.hare.stag` (and, when a cell is a list of payoffs, its player last: `inspection.payoffs.cheat.audit.human`; in a game of players alike, its strategy: `public_goods.payoffs.keep`). The payoff is written where it is in the file's own style, and Forge keeps the change only when it reads the model back valid with exactly the cells written there changed (a symmetric game's second player reads the first's). `forge game` answers, in each cell, `written`: where each payoff is written as `edit` names it, and its formula when it is one. In Studio, "change this game" shows each payoff's formula under its value; a tap opens it, to be changed to another formula or a number, with Forge's message when it refuses.

### Changed

- **`make check` lints the Markdown files a change touches, not every page** (roadmap A9-ROLES). Its last step, `make validate-docs`, failed on 82 pages that were written before the lint existed (CHANGELOG.md alone has 296 errors), so no clone got `make check` green. It now lints the files that differ from `origin/main` and the new ones (not CHANGELOG.md, which every change touches), so a page you write must lint clean and a clone with no changes passes. `make validate-docs-all` lints every page, as `validate-docs` did.
- **`forge import` reads `COLUMN` back as Forge's, where Excel counts as Forge does.** `COLUMN()` in a table's row and `COLUMN` of a table column's header cell, which `forge export` writes, come back as `COLUMN()`, `COLUMN(z)` (the row's own table) and `COLUMN(t.z)`, and calculate the same: `=COLUMN('u'!$B$1)` in a row is `=COLUMN(u.q)`, in a scalar too. Excel's number is Forge's only on a sheet whose table starts in column A and whose every column has a header and a name of its own (a column with neither is left out of the model, and every column after it would count one less), so on any other sheet, and for `COLUMN` of another cell, of a range, in a scalar's or a total row's cell, or of an included file's table, the formula is still imported as its value, with a warning. The Gnumeric cases of `COLUMN` pass after an export and import.
- **`forge import` reads a sheet of inputs headed in French, Chinese or any language as scalars** (roadmap T1-IMPORT-OTHER-LANGUAGES). A sheet of labels and values was a sheet of scalars only with `Value` over its values, so a French sheet headed `Hypothèse`, `Valeur`, `Unité` came in as a table, and every formula reading it came in as a value, with a warning. Now a sheet of labels is a sheet of scalars, whatever its header says, when the other sheets read its values one cell at a time: at least once, never as part of a range, and never row by row beside their own rows unless the row is fixed (`$B$2`). `Prix unitaire` on `Hypothèses` is `hypothèses.prix_unitaire`, `单价` on `假设` is `假设.单价`, and `=B2*Hypothèses!$B$2` in a table is `=volume * hypothèses.prix_unitaire`. A sheet read as a range (a lookup), or row by row as a table reads another, is a table as before.
- **`forge validate` fails a formula with no stored result, and names it.** A file passes only when every formula has its result stored, each is what its formula gives, and every `expect:` is met. Until now a formula never calculated passed, with a line counting it, so a model that `forge calculate` had never run on passed the gate. Now it exits with 1: `Error: Validation error: 4 formula(s) have no stored result (year.revenue, year.profit, year.margin, year.worst_month): run forge calculate`. The verdict is the engine's, so every surface reads the same one. The command line exits by it. `forge_validate` (MCP), forge-wasm and `POST /api/v1/validate` (whose `valid` it is) answer `passed`, and `uncalculated` with the names in file order, as `forge report` gives them. `uncalculated` replaces `unstored`, which was only a count. `tables_valid` is gone: it was `true` on every answer and never checked anything (a table whose columns differ in length was, and is, refused with an error). `scalars_valid` still means that every stored result matches its formula. The examples are committed calculated, except lesson 2's two files, which are as the lesson types them. A test calculates a copy of every example and checks that each then passes, so `forge calculate` can always meet the gate. [The guide's workflow](examples/pipeline/forge.yml) keeps its `forge report --format json` step while it installs 10.0.0-rc.34, whose `validate` still passes such a formula. The step can go once it installs the first release with this change.
- **Forge Studio's export says what stays in the YAML file, as `forge export` does.** The analysis sections (`monte_carlo`, `tornado`, `decision_tree`, `real_options`, `bootstrap`, `bayesian_network`), `better:` and comments are named as not exported in Studio's export panel too, under "forge export says:" (it said "Written as values", which was wrong for these and for scenarios). The in-memory export (`export_bytes_core`) now gives the same warnings as the file and buffer exports.
- **A misspelt name in a formula is answered with the name that is close to it.** `=price_per_dozen * dozen_per_day` in a model that has `dozens_per_day` now fails with `Unknown variable: dozen_per_day. Did you mean 'dozens_per_day'?`; before, only a misspelt `group.name` got a suggestion, and a name written alone got none. A name that is close to none is answered as before. Lesson 2 of the guide shows it.
- **`scripts/coverage.sh` refuses a toolchain older than Rust 1.100**, so that a local run measures what CI's Coverage job measures. The oldest supported Rust (1.97, the container's default) counts fewer regions inside macros and read 100% where CI did not. The script says how to pick one (`RUSTUP_TOOLCHAIN=1.100.0 make coverage`); CONTRIBUTING.md and the testing architecture say the same.
- **The guide's AI page says how to add Forge's MCP server to Cursor, VS Code and Codex** (roadmap A5-GUIDE-AI). [`docs/guide/ai.md`](docs/guide/ai.md) now has a row for each, from the client's current documentation (the file, the key, and `"type": "stdio"` where it is required), Claude Desktop's Linux file, and the pages it was read from with their dates. It also says what agents.md says of nested `AGENTS.md` files: the nearest wins.
- **Forge Studio changes a value, a formula, an added or a deleted element with Forge's `edit`** (ADR-075 step 7, roadmap S-EDIT). Studio's own line-by-line YAML editing (`studio/text.js`) is gone: an input typed on a card, a formula, "Add" and "Delete" send `set_value`, `set_formula`, `add` and `delete` to forge-wasm's `edit`, which writes the same bytes (the cases of the old `studio/tests/text.mjs` are held by the tests of `src/edit/text.rs`), keeps a change only when Forge reads the model back with exactly it, and shows its message when it does not. A name added on a card may now be in any alphabet. `studio/text.js` keeps the editor\'s colours. The README's screenshots are made again from the bagel's uncertain model (`studio/screenshots.mjs`).

### Added
- **Coverage is 100% of lines, functions and regions** (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE). The last 67 regions were covered with tests that assert (the import's list of what it changed, an inline model whose folder cannot be made or whose file cannot be written, `Invalid YAML` in inline text, the formula error helper by its message), and most of them were one fault: generic code is counted once for each type it is used with, so a function taking a closure, an iterator or a type to write (`run_from`, `watch_with`, `width_of`, `run_with_evaluator`, a tournament's `work_out`, `serve_until`, `write_result`, `evaluate_in_context`) had its error paths missed in every copy but the one that tested them. Each now has one copy where it can: the closure is `&mut dyn FnMut`, the shutdown a boxed future, the arguments a `Vec<OsString>`, the watcher a `Box<dyn Any>`, and the part of `write_result` and `evaluate_in_context` that does not need the type is a function of its own. `--output` now serialises the result as JSON and as YAML and writes the one the file's name asks for. No output changed. The floors in `ci.yml` are 100 for lines, functions and regions. CI's Coverage job, which read 99.99% where a local run read 100%, now prints the regions no test ran (`scripts/coverage_misses.py`), and what it found is fixed: the server's log lines had a second branch for the `log` crate in every `info!`, which axum's default `tower-log` feature turned on and which only some toolchains count as code (CI's stable does, the oldest supported Rust does not): Forge's `axum` now leaves that feature out, and the server's tests of the log set their own subscriber, on and off, the terminal's width is read before it is needed so that a test of `COLUMNS` does not leave a closure unrun, and a test of the function list collects the broken ones in one pass.
- **The guide's page on Forge with an AI** (`docs/guide/ai.md`, roadmap A5-GUIDE-AI). A chat that can run commands (the README's prompt and PROMPTS.md), the MCP server in Claude Desktop, Claude Code and Gemini CLI (with `--root`, and why), and nine rules to put in a repository's `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` for an agent that works on models: start from the starter, the two forms of a formula, change with `forge_edit`, validate after a change, results written by calculate and never typed, simulate an uncertain model, check a result another way, files only in the server's folder, hand back the whole file. Each rule has a row naming the tools it rests on and a case in `validation/repo`'s `agent_rules`, which starts `forge mcp --root` on a copy of the examples, makes the calls one at a time as an agent does, and asserts the answers.
- **The guide's lessons 4 to 6, on the bagel shop's analyses** (`docs/guide/`, roadmap A4-GUIDE-ANALYSES). [4. What if](docs/guide/04-what-if.md): scenarios and `forge compare`, break-even, goal seek, a table of price against demand, and the tornado, with the price's swing worked out by hand. [5. How likely](docs/guide/05-how-likely.md): lesson 2's model with its demand and staff hours uncertain (`examples/guide/05-bagel-uncertain.yaml`), and how to read `forge simulate`: the chance of a good year, percentiles, the worst years, drivers, the months, and why the mean year is 5,106.49 below the plan, which the page works out by hand. [6. What will they do](docs/guide/06-what-will-they-do.md): the price war, its payoffs from the plan, the dominated price, the equilibrium, a tournament of pricing habits and the city's shops copying the winners. A `yaml` block headed `# examples/….yaml, part of it` is checked as lines of that file.
- **The guide's first three lessons** (`docs/guide/`, roadmap A3-GUIDE-CORE, ADR-078), on one model, the bagel shop. [1. The first ten minutes](docs/guide/01-first-ten-minutes.md): open it in Studio or install Forge with its examples, calculate it, change the price with `forge edit`, follow the profit down with `forge audit`. [2. A first model](docs/guide/02-a-first-model.md): write the shop from an empty file, a group of inputs, a table of twelve months with `PREV`, the year's totals, a second file through `_includes`, and scenarios, with three mistakes and what Forge says to each; the finished model is `examples/guide/02-bagel.yaml`. [3. Keeping a model](docs/guide/03-keeping-a-model.md): `forge calculate` writes the results and keeps a `.bak`, `forge edit` sets, adds and renames, `forge validate` fails when a stored result is out of date, git keeps the history, and `forge upgrade` raises the format. Each lesson ends with a result to work out by hand and a question whose answer is checked. For them the guide's test writes the files a page has the reader write (`# bagel.yaml`), compares the lines a page shows of a file after a command (`# bagel.yaml, after forge calculate`), and accepts a command that fails where the page shows its `Error:` line. Every command of the three lessons prints what the page shows with this build and with the released 10.0.0-rc.34.
- **The guide's pages are checked before there are any** (`validation/repo`'s `guide`, roadmap A2-GUIDE-TESTS, ADR-078). Each `$ forge` command of a page in `docs/guide/` runs in a copy of `examples/` and must print the lines the page shows; a `yaml` block headed by `# examples/….yaml` must be that file; every link and anchor must resolve; and every number in a `<details>` answer must be one a command printed. `docs/cli/README.md` is also checked against what `forge --help` prints: it was equal, and nothing kept it so.
- **Forge adds a game from a template: `insert`, the `templates` tool and "add a game" in Studio** (ADR-075 step 6, ADR-076 step 6, roadmap S-TEMPLATES). Four templates, each a game written as the examples write one, with its numbers to change and a comment on its parts: `game_of_two` (a game of two players, played once), `game_of_many` (many players alike, the count form), `tournament` (the prisoner's dilemma and a tournament of three habits that plays it) and `dynamics` (a population whose strategies spread). `forge edit model.yaml --insert tournament=war`, the `insert` change of `forge_edit` and forge-wasm's `edit` add one after the last game in the indentation the games use, or as a `games:` at the end of a model that has none; a model of an earlier format than 5.5.0 is raised to it (the earlier formats are kept by the later). Without a name the game gets the template's default or the first of `_2`, `_3`, ... that the model does not use; a name the model uses is refused with Forge's message. Forge keeps the change only when it reads the model back valid with the template's games added and nothing else changed. Every template is inserted into every model of the examples. The templates are served by `forge templates` (a name shows its block, `--json` lists them as data), the MCP tool `forge_templates` (the 23rd) and forge-wasm's `templates`. Forge Studio's Model view has "add a game": the templates by Forge's line, a name, Add game; `studio/tests/templates.mjs` checks each against the games `game` answers for the files `edit` wrote. Making inputs uncertain is not a template: it is "Make uncertain" on each input.
- **Forge Studio: change a game on its card** (ADR-076 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). Every game card has "change this game": rename the game, a player or a strategy (a strategy only one player has is named with its player), add a strategy for every player, delete one, add a player (a tournament's with the habit it plays, from the nine the schema allows) or delete one. Each is a change of forge-wasm's `edit`, so the file keeps its layout and comments and every name that reads the one renamed follows; a change Forge refuses, such as a strategy of the game a tournament plays, is shown in the card as Forge words it, with the file as it was. `studio/tests/gameedit.mjs` makes each change on the inspection game and the price war and checks the cards against what `game` answers for the files `edit` wrote.
- **Every function Forge lists has a case that checks it against another engine** (`validation/repo`'s `function_cases`, ADR-045 as it was built). The test reads `forge functions --json` and fails for a function that no Gnumeric case and no formula of the R suite calls, unless it is listed with its reason; the one listed is `MC.Discrete`, which no engine draws here, and an entry whose case is added later fails too, so the list only shrinks. It found `TEXTJOIN`, which had only unit tests: three cases (`test_textjoin_*` in `validation/gnumeric/tests/functions/text.yaml`, the length of the joined text, 5, 4 and 6 by hand) now pass in Forge, in Gnumeric 1.12.56 and after an export and import. 196 of the 203 functions had a case; the six `MC.*` distributions besides `MC.Discrete` are drawn by `forge simulate` and compared with R. The Gnumeric suite has 2,165 cases.
- **The number of MCP tools written in the README, `docs/MCP.md` and `docs/WORKFLOWS.md` is checked against `forge mcp`'s own tool list** (`validation/repo`'s `doc_counts`). `docs/MCP.md` said 21 tools; there are 22.
- **The ADR index gives each ADR the status the ADR states** (`validation/repo`'s `architecture_docs`). Every ADR states its status (`**Status:**`, `**Status**:` or a `## Status` heading), and the index's status must start with the same one (Proposed, Accepted, Implemented, Restored, Partly superseded, Superseded or Not implemented); the index may say something is not yet built only when the ADR does. On main, ten disagreed: the index said ADR-076's step 4 was not yet built (the ADR: built on 2026-11-22), ADR-014 and ADR-015 were partly superseded while they said Accepted (both now say Partly superseded, as their notes describe), and ADR-018 to ADR-024 were Accepted where they say Implemented or Restored.
- **`forge edit` adds and deletes a game's strategy and player** (ADR-075 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). A strategy added to `deal_game.yaml` meant a new item in `strategies:`, a new row of `payoffs:` with a cell for every strategy, and a new cell in every other row, each written by hand in the file's layout. `forge edit deal_game.yaml --add-strategy sealed_bids.strategies.bid_50` writes all of it, its cells 0, as the cells beside it are written (a line of its own under a block, `bid_50: [0, 0]` inside braces, `bid_50: 0` in the count form and in `dynamics.start`); `inspection.strategies.human.ignore` adds one player's. `--add-player inspection.players.board` adds a player last, with the last player's strategies: each cell becomes its level, the cell as it was for each of its strategies with a payoff of 0 for it, so the game is unchanged whatever it plays; a tournament's player takes its habit, `--add-player rivalry.players.copycat=tit_for_tat`. `--delete-strategy` takes a strategy out with its cells, and `--delete-player` a player, leaving the payoffs of its first strategy, so an added strategy or player deleted again gives the file back byte for byte. Forge keeps a change only when it reads the model back valid with the game as asked and nothing else changed. What the model does not allow is refused with Forge's message: a strategy of a game a tournament plays, a third player of a symmetric game of two written out (Forge shows the count form), the players `noisy-tournament.yaml` writes once for two tournaments, a strategy or player that takes away an equilibrium a formula reads. Every game of the examples gets a strategy and a player added and deleted again; Forge keeps 16 of the 31 changes and refuses the other 15 for those reasons, and the text of all but the shared roster comes back byte for byte. `forge_edit` and forge-wasm's `edit` take `add_strategy`, `delete_strategy`, `add_player` (with `plays` for a tournament's) and `delete_player`.
- **Every example in `forge <command> --help` is run or checked** (`validation/repo`'s `help_examples`). Each example names a command and flags Forge has, and the models that the help of `bayesian`, `bootstrap`, `decision-tree` and `real-options` shows run with every example under them. The bayesian example had been refused as shown; this check found the next: `forge real-options --help`'s `--option defer` named an option that the model it shows has not (options are found by name), so it now reads `--option "Sell assets"`.
- **Every Mermaid diagram of the docs is rendered on each pull request** (roadmap T1-MERMAID-CHECK). GitHub shows a diagram that does not parse as an error box and says nothing else. `validation/mermaid/check.mjs` renders every ` ```mermaid ` block of every Markdown file in Chromium with mermaid 12.3.0 and fails naming the file, the line and the block; the `Diagrams` workflow runs it whenever a Markdown file changes, docs-only pull requests included, and keeps a PNG of each diagram as the run's artifact. The 47 diagrams on main render.

### Changed
- **No lint allow is left in any crate (T1-NO-SKIPPING).** The last two are gone. `core/array_calculator/whole_column.rs` answered ADR-058's question (is this argument of this function a whole column in a row formula?) with a `match` grouped as the ADR's table is, under `match_same_arms`, as two groups gave the same rule; it is now that table as data, each group of functions with a named rule (`Alone`, `Every`, `At(&[0, 2])`, the sum range and criteria ranges, the criteria ranges). For each of the 203 functions and an unknown name, with 0 to 9 arguments and every position, it answers as the `match` did (compared before the `match` was deleted); a new test lists the positions read whole for every function with 1 to 7 arguments, and another that each name in the table is a registered function, once. In `monte_carlo/engine.rs`, the Gaussian copula's loop over the draws indexed each column by row under `needless_range_loop` (which only clippy 1.97 raised); it now moves one cursor down each correlated column. A seeded run of four inputs, three of them correlated, gives the same draws to the bit as before: a new test holds their bits. The budget of lint allows in `validation/repo` falls from 2 to 0. No output changes.
- **The MCP server's two lint allows are gone (T1-NO-SKIPPING).** The four tools that describe Forge (`forge_schema`, `forge_functions`, `forge_templates`, `forge_examples`) never used the server, and rmcp 3.10 serves a tool with no `self`, so they are plain functions; the comment that said rmcp required `&self` was out of date. `#[tool_handler]` wrote `list_tools` as an `async fn` that awaits nothing, which clippy 1.100 refuses (`unused_async_trait_impl`, unknown to 1.97, so the allow also needed `unknown_lints`); the server now writes it itself as a future that is ready at once, with what rmcp's would answer. A new test lists the tools in an initialized session and in a protocol 2026-07-28 request, where the answer adds `ttlMs` 0, `cacheScope` public and `resultType` complete; the four answers checked are byte for byte those rmcp's generated `list_tools` gave. The budget of lint allows in `validation/repo` falls from 4 to 2. No output changes.
- **Five more lint allows are gone, all in tests (T1-NO-SKIPPING).** `probability_seek_core`'s `too_many_arguments` had nothing to silence (6 arguments; the lint starts at 8). The Bayesian and sampler tests name what they hold (`evidence_of_a_and_b`, `first_draws`, `second_draws`), and the two bagel simulation tests, which typed the model again as a sum of products under `needless_range_loop` and `suboptimal_flops`, share one `month_profit` that names each line as the model does (`flour`, `sesame`, `other_ingredients`, `labour`, `fixed`, `card_fees`) and sums the months with an iterator; they check the same numbers to the same 1e-6. The budget of lint allows in `validation/repo` falls from 9 to 4. No output changes.
- **Tests of the editor's text, check and rename code (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `edit/mod.rs`, `edit/text.rs`, `edit/check.rs` and `edit/rename/` are covered, with exact messages for: a value an alias shares (Forge would read two changes, so it is refused), text that is not YAML or has no such group (`set_formula`, `add`, `delete`, `sites`, the rename of a file), and the paths `copy` and `remove` leave alone. Two branches that could not happen are gone: an entry without a value (the parser gives every entry one, empty where none is written) and a model whose JSON has no `games` or `network`; a player, strategy or entrant named in a game of another kind, or in none, is asserted to rename nothing. The sweeps over every example now fail with the whole list of what failed. No output changes.
- **Tests of `forge edit` and of making an input uncertain or certain (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Every refusal of `make_uncertain` and `make_certain` is asserted by its exact text: a distribution made uncertain again, an input with an empty `value:`, a group written on one line, a distribution the plan cannot give a figure for, an anchor that would make its alias uncertain too, YAML that cannot be read, a group that is not there, and a last input with no line break after it. `forge edit` reads the model's text and parses it in one step (`parser::parse_model_and_text`, which `parse_model` now calls), so a file that changes between two reads can no longer fail the second; a path with no folder is looked up in one place. No output changes.
- **The unit checker's two `#[allow(clippy::unused_self)]` are gone.** `can_add` and `extract_references` never used `self`, so they are plain associated functions; their tests call them directly. The budget of lint allows in `validation/repo` falls from 27 to 25. `core/unit_validator.rs` was already at 100% lines, functions and regions on main. No output changes.
- **The roadmap's adoption track names what agents and their tools look for** (A11 to A18): AGENTS.md, a Forge Agent Skill, the MCP Registry's `server.json`, `llms.txt` (kept only if a trial shows it helps), what Context7, DeepWiki and GitMCP say about Forge, an SBOM with each release, OpenSSF Scorecard, and `CITATION.cff`, `security.txt`, the crate's keywords and GitHub topics.
- **ADR-078 proposed: a way in for each reader** (roadmap A1-DOCS-MAP). Five readers (a new user, an AI agent or its user, a pipeline builder, a developer who embeds Forge, a contributor or maintainer), the page each starts on, a guide of lessons in `docs/guide/` built on the bagel shop, and a test in `validation/repo` for every page from its first commit. FEATURES.md stays the reference and Studio's help links do not move.
- **Documentation errors fixed (roadmap A0-DOC-ERRORS).** `docs/MCP.md` links the Rust MCP SDK at its real repository, gives Claude Desktop's configuration file for macOS and Windows, and counts 22 tools. The comment in `examples/starter.yaml` (and `docs/EXAMPLES.md`) no longer says a table column cannot reduce a column: `=revenue / SUM(revenue)` works (ADR-058). The README's lint command is the one CI runs, its documentation table lists the glossary, the command-line reference and CONTRIBUTING, and it says to write `forge-examples/` where `install.sh --examples` put the examples. The roadmap has a new `adoption` track.
- **Workflows and dependencies are pinned (T1-SUPPLY-CHAIN).** Every workflow starts with `permissions: contents: read`; the jobs that write (Update Generated Docs, Auto Release, the release, Studio-on-Pages and Homebrew jobs of `release.yml`, `pages.yml`'s deploy) keep their own wider block. Every `uses:` is pinned by full commit SHA with its tag in a comment (a branch or a moving tag like `@stable`, `@master`, `@cargo-llvm-cov` could change what CI runs); `dtolnay/rust-toolchain` keeps `toolchain: stable` and `taiki-e/install-action` gets `tool: cargo-llvm-cov` as inputs, since the branch and tag used to choose them. `studio/package-lock.json` is committed (it was in `.gitignore`) and CI and Pages run `npm ci` in `studio/`. `.cargo/audit.toml` no longer ignores RUSTSEC-2024-0436: Cargo.lock holds `pastey`, not `paste`.
- **Tests of the tool, server, parser and writer errors (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Every error message of the MCP tools (audit, export, import, sensitivity, variance, goal seek, break-even, tornado, decision tree, real options, bootstrap, seek), the REST API's paths outside `--root`, the writer's refusals, includes and the names two documents of a file share is now asserted by its exact text. Three things that could not fail no longer pretend to: `functions_core` returns the catalog, not a `Result`; a date is never in a scenario's `ranges`; and `forge serve` and `forge mcp` expect their runtime to start, where they said "Failed to create runtime". `forge serve` rejects a CORS origin that is not a header value once, when the configuration is checked. No command's output changes.
- **`forge calculate` and `forge validate` print from their cores' results** (roadmap T1-CLI-FROM-CORES). They repeated the steps of `calculate_core` and `validate_core`, so a fix made in a core reached MCP, REST and Forge Studio but not the command line. `forge calculate` now calls `calculate_core` (or `calculate_plan_core` with `--plan`) and prints its result; the result holds the tables and scalars in the model's order (JSON keeps them in name order), how many stored values the run wrote (`values_updated` in JSON, left out when 0) and the file as read for `--verbose`. `forge validate` calls the steps of `validate_core` one by one (`ModelToValidate`: read, check the lengths, check), so it still says what it found before a step fails. What they print is unchanged, byte for byte, for every example in every mode; one difference: when `calculate` fails, the lines it printed before calculating (`--verbose`'s counts, the plan figures, unit warnings) are no longer printed before the error.
- **`forge tornado`, `forge scenarios`, `forge goal-seek`, `forge break-even`, `forge sensitivity`, `forge variance`, `forge export` and `forge import` print from their cores' results** (roadmap T1-CLI-FROM-CORES, now done: every command does). The results gain what the commands printed and lacked: a tornado's `section` as the file writes it (the names before Forge resolves them); the `formats` of a scenario analysis's scalars; an export's `not_checked` (why the workbook was not checked; it is still the first of `warnings`); an import's `tables` (each with its columns and rows), `formulas_kept` and, with `--split-files`, `files_written`. A tornado input's `base` is left out of JSON when it is not set. What they print is unchanged, byte for byte, on every example and in every mode, the reports `forge variance -o` writes and the files `forge import` writes included.
- **`forge bootstrap`, `forge bayesian`, `forge real-options` and `forge decision-tree` print from their cores' results** (roadmap T1-CLI-FROM-CORES). Each called its core's steps again, so a fix in the core could miss the command line. Their results gain what the commands printed from the configuration, so MCP, REST, Forge Studio and `--output` files have it too: a bootstrap's `statistic`, `sample_size`, `confidence_levels` and `seed`; every node of a Bayesian network with its `states` and `parents` (`nodes`); a real options analysis's `method` and `option_order` (the file's order); a decision tree's `root_name` and `node_count`. What they print is unchanged, byte for byte, on every example; when one fails, the configuration it printed before failing is no longer printed before the error.

- **REST's export and import call `export_core` and `import_core`** (roadmap T3-API's note). They ran `forge export`'s and `forge import`'s printing functions on a console and threw away what they printed. Their answers are unchanged. `ApiResponse::err`, which no handler used (a failed operation answers `success: true` with its flag false), is removed, with the `error` field it alone set.

### Fixed
- **`forge export` writes `COLUMN` as an Excel formula**, where it refused it and wrote the value. Forge now numbers a table's columns as the export lays them out, so Excel's number is Forge's: `COLUMN()` in a row formula is written as it is, and `COLUMN(z)` or `COLUMN(t.z)` as `COLUMN` of the column's header cell (`COLUMN('t'!$C$1)`). `COLUMN()` in a scalar formula, and `COLUMN` of a scalar, an expression or an included file's table, have no number in Forge and are still written as values. `forge import` reads it back (below).
- **A simulation of huge values prints numbers, not `inf`** (roadmap T1-HUGE-STATS, ADR-079). `forge simulate` on `MC.Triangular(-1E+307, 0, 1E+307)` printed `Std Dev: inf`, and an output ten times it a mean of `inf` too: the standard deviation squared each deviation, past the largest number above about 1e154, and the mean summed values near 1e308. So did the expected shortfall, each input's mean (`null` in the JSON) and the histogram's width. When the plain arithmetic overflows although every value is a number, Forge now works the figure out again on the values divided by a power of two, which is exact, and scales it back: the standard deviation of `=x * 2` is 2e307 / √6, as the triangle's, within 2%. A model that summarizes today keeps every digit. The library's `Statistics::variance` is an `Option`, `None` when the variance is past the largest number (that of values near 1e307 is near 1e613). The spreadsheet functions are unchanged: `=STDEV(-1E+307, 1E+307)` is `#NUM!`, as in Excel.
- **`COLUMN` is a table column's number**, as `forge export` lays the table out: the table's columns in model order, the first (in column A) being 1. Forge gave 1 whatever the column: in a table of `x`, `y: =COLUMN() * 10` and `z: =COLUMN(x) + COLUMN(t.y) * x`, `y` was 10 where it is 20, and `z` with `x` = 1, 2, 3 was 2, 3, 4 where it is 3, 5, 7. `COLUMN()` is the row formula's own column, `COLUMN(t.y)` or a bare `COLUMN(x)` the column named. A scalar formula has no column of its own, so its `COLUMN()` is now `#VALUE!` (it was 1), and so is `COLUMN` of anything that is not a table column. `forge export` still refuses `COLUMN`.
- **`forge export` refuses an array constant with a separator and no item**, as Forge's own parser does. `={1; }` was written as `={1;}`, which Excel does not accept (a syntax error); the same for `{1,}`, `{,1}` and `{}`.
- **A model file that cannot be read says which file**, and a variance report that cannot be written is an I/O error, not an "Excel export error". `forge calculate missing.yaml` said `IO error: No such file or directory (os error 2)` and left the person to find the file in a command with several; it now says `IO error: missing.yaml: No such file or directory (os error 2)`, for the model and for a file it includes. A YAML variance report written where there is no folder said `Excel export error: Failed to write file: ...` though no Excel file is made; it is now `IO error: <the report's path>: ...`.
- **`ROW(reference)` is the row the reference is read at**, numbered as `ROW()` numbers a table's rows (the first is 1). Forge gave 1 whatever the reference: `=ROW(x) * x` in a table whose `x` is 3, -2, 5 was 3, -2, 5, where it is 3, -4, 15. Read whole, in a scalar formula or inside `SUM`, a column's rows are 1 to its length, as Excel's `ROW(A2:A4)` is `{2; 3; 4}`: `SUM(ROW(t.x))` is 6. A reference that is not a table column (a scalar, a number, a text) has no row, and is `#VALUE!`, as Excel's `ROW` refuses what is not a reference. `forge export` still refuses `ROW` of a reference.
- **An array `IF` is an error only where an element chooses the branch in error**, as in Excel and LibreOffice, which give each element its own result. Forge evaluated each branch over the whole array, and an error anywhere in it was the IF's error: `SUM(IF(t.a > 0, 10 / t.a, 0))` with `t.a` = 0, 1, 2 failed with division by zero, where it is 15, and `SUM(IF(t.a > 5, 1 / 0, 1))` failed though no element chooses `1 / 0` (it is 3). A branch that is an error as a whole is now evaluated again for each element that chooses it, at that element's row; an element that chooses an error is still the error, as Forge has no error inside an array.
- **SUMIFS, COUNTIFS, AVERAGEIFS, MAXIFS and MINIFS refuse ranges of different sizes** with `#VALUE!`, naming the criteria range and its size, as Excel (whose help asks for the same size and shape), LibreOffice (`IterateParametersIfs`, Err:502) and Gnumeric (`gnm_criteria_ifs_func`) do. Forge counted the rows past a shorter criteria range as matching: `SUMIFS(t.v, t.k, ">0", s.j, ">0")` with `t.v` = 10, 20, 30 and `s.j` = 1 gave 60, where only the first row meets both. The five functions now share one matcher. SUMIF and AVERAGEIF with a shorter sum range are unchanged: Excel and LibreOffice read the empty cells past it, which add nothing and are not averaged, so they give Forge's answer.
- **`DDB` reads a period after 0 and before 1 as the first period**, as Gnumeric does (`gnumeric_ddb`); LibreOffice's `ScDDB` gives an error. Forge took the fraction as it was, and gave 63,053.12 for `=DDB(944326.664492, 910840.7, 21, 0.8, 3)`, a case of the scheduled Validation run, where Gnumeric gives 33,485.96: the first period's 944,326.66 × 3 / 21, at most down to the salvage. With a life under 1, that first period takes all down to the salvage, as in Gnumeric. A period of 0 or less, or after the life, is still `#NUM!`.
- **PMT, PV, IPMT and PPMT answer over any number of periods, and DDB at any rate** (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING, the follow-up to the closed forms of CUMIPMT, CUMPRINC and VDB). A growth (1 + rate)^nper past the largest double made the payment infinity over infinity, so `=PMT(0.05, 20000, 1000)` was #NUM! (1.05^20,000 is about 1E+423), and so were `PV`, `IPMT` and `PPMT` over that many periods. Where the growth is above 1, PMT and PV now divide above and below by it, so the payment over 20,000 periods or 1E+20 is -50, the interest, and `=PV(0.05, 1E+20, -100)` is 2,000, a perpetuity's worth (mpmath, 60 digits: -50.0000000000000028 and 1999.99999999999989). On 20,000 random loans the new form is no less exact than the old (its worst error is the same) and closer in more of them than not; below 1 nothing changes. DDB took a period's loss as the worth before it less the worth after, two numbers that agree in most of their digits when the rate is small: at a rate below about 1E-16 a period `1 - rate` rounded to 1 and `=DDB(1000, 100, 1E+17, 1)` was 0, not 2E-14, and `=DDB(1000, 0, 2E+10, 1)` was 9.999996564E-08, not 1E-07. A period now loses the worth before it times the rate. The Gnumeric suite gives the same results before and after (1,989 passed; the 175 that fail here need LibreOffice 24.8, as CI has). One printed number moves, in its last digits: the mortgage example's `paid_off.last_payment`, what is left after 246 months of its schedule, is 42.2487749021918 (it was 42.2487749021881; exact, 42.2487748993687, both off by the schedule's rounding), so `docs/posts/mortgage.md` says so.
- **CUMIPMT, CUMPRINC and VDB no longer hang, and are exact late in a long loan and at tiny rates** (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING). They added a run of periods one at a time in a floating-point counter, which stops moving past 2^53: `=CUMIPMT(0.01, 1E+20, 1000, 1E+17, 1E+17, 0)` or `=VDB(1000, 0, 1E+17, 0, 1E+17)` ran until stopped, and a model holding one hung `forge calculate`, the MCP server and the REST server. Each sum is now a closed form, so any run costs what one period does: a loan's balance and an asset's worth are e^(j ln(1 + rate)), and what they lose between two periods is taken with `expm1`, so no sum is a difference of large numbers. That fixes two wrong answers on the way. The interest of the last payment of a loan over 1,000 periods at 5% (`=CUMIPMT(0.05, 1000, 100000, 1000, 1000, 0)`) is -238.095238095238 (exact, 60 digits); it was 744,908,390.4, and Gnumeric 1.12.56 gives 0 and LibreOffice 24.2 858,993,459.2. A rate below about 1E-16 a period declined by nothing, as `1 - rate` rounded to 1: `=VDB(1000, 100, 1E+17, 0, 5E+16, 2, TRUE)` is 632.120558828558, 1,000 times 1 - e^-1. The closed form of VDB agrees with the period-by-period walk it replaces (LibreOffice's ScVDB) to 1E-10 on 20,000 random assets, and is closer to it run with 60 digits (1.4E-12 against 6.8E-12 at worst); CUMIPMT and CUMPRINC agree with exact values to 5E-16 for interest and 6E-14 for principal (the rounding of ln(1 + rate) times the periods). The principal of a first payment at the start (`CUMPRINC(..., 1, 1, 1)`) can differ from `PMT`'s in the last bit. The Gnumeric suite's 32 cases of these functions pass.
- **ADR-041, ADR-043 and ADR-045 are Superseded, not "Not implemented".** They proposed scripts of the old `forge-e2e` repository that were never built here, and the validation that exists does what each wanted: ADR-057 and ADR-062 (expected values that every engine must agree with, and cases drawn at random with none), ADR-057 (every case must pass, so there is no baseline of failures to compare with) and the test above (a function without a case). Each says why its design would not do, and the index says the same. Page 10 of the architecture no longer says that no test compares the MCP tool count with `docs/MCP.md`: `doc_counts` does.
- **Architecture pages 00, 03, 05, 08 and 10 say what the code does** (as #377 did for 01, 02, 04, 06 and 09). 03: a row formula's column is Text, Boolean or Number (a date result is its text; there is no Date column from a formula); the calculation can also hold a Bayesian network cell; the calculation order does not read `whole_column.rs` (the evaluator, the parser, `PREV` groups and the Excel translation do); the Excel export does not read the function registry; only `forge simulate` and `--seek` share a `CalcPlan` (goal seek, sensitivity and tornado start afresh each step); `format:` is checked only when no cell failed. 05: the MCP tool's inline workbook comes from `export_buffer_core` (base64), `export_bytes_core` is forge-wasm's, and games are listed as not exported. 08: `ServeArgs` is in `src/cli/args/options.rs`, each answer's fields, and the statuses outside the envelope (403, 401, 415, 400, 422, 404, 405), checked on a running server. 10: the function count is also in `src/main.rs` and `docs/FUNCTIONS.md`, whose category headings `doc_counts` checks in the pull request; the `*_core` exports, `help_examples`, `make docs-cli`, `make test-repo`, and `carried.rs` for a new analysis section. 00: `validation/mermaid` and every workflow. docs/MCP.md said 21 tools; there are 22.
- **A game whose players do not have the same number of strategies no longer stops Forge.** A game that is not symmetric, whose first player has two strategies and another has one, panicked when its results were worked out (`forge game`, `forge calculate`, the MCP tools and Forge Studio): the population of a symmetric game, which reads the first player's payoffs for everyone, was worked out before the game was found not to be symmetric. It is now worked out only for a symmetric game. Found by deleting a strategy with `forge edit`.
- **The command line's help says what the commands do today** (and docs/cli/README.md, made from it). `forge bayesian`'s example ran with nodes that do not exist (`default_prob`, `economy`) and a model without `_forge_version`, which is refused since rc.34; it now runs as shown and mentions formulas, queries and `--scenario`. `forge upgrade` said the latest format was 5.1.0 (it is 5.6.0; 5.1.0 is the default of `--to`); `forge import` said it writes 5.1.0 (5.3.0 or 5.4.0 when there are empty cells, dates or formats); `forge serve` promised every operation (it has five). `forge examples --help` lists expected-loss and the ten games, `forge bootstrap --help` the interval methods and every statistic, `forge simulate --help` correlations and expected shortfall. README.md lists `forge edit` and `forge validate`'s `expect:`, and says `forge calculate --plan` calculates an uncertain model at plan.
- **`forge calculate --verbose` lists the scenarios in name order.** "Found N scenarios" printed them in the order of a `HashMap`, which changed from run to run; `ParsedModel::scenario_names` now sorts them, so the list is the same every time.
- **VALIDATION.md has its section 5 and its known limits again** (roadmap T1-LEFTOVERS-2026-11-22). The commit that added the dynamics of a symmetric game (2026-11-21) cut the end of the file: section 5, the six rounds of checks in real Excel that section 2 refers to, and the Known limits. Both are restored, section 5 before section 6, and the summary and docs/STABILITY.md count six rounds, the sixth (`XNPV` and `XIRR` with a date before the first, 2026-11-19) included.

### Removed
- **`FormulaTranslator`, from the library.** `forge export` translates formulas with `workbook_refs`, token by token, resolving names as the calculator does; the old regex translator matched ASCII names only (Forge's names are Unicode: `hypothèses.coût_unitaire`, `假设.单价`) and was exported but called by nothing in Forge, its WebAssembly module or its validation crates. What the export did use of it, the names Excel knows as functions, is `src/excel/excel_functions.rs`, with its tests: the list is sorted, and every listed Excel function is in it. No output changes.
- **The `cfg(coverage)` check-cfg in Cargo.toml and forge-wasm/Cargo.toml** (roadmap T1-LEFTOVERS-2026-11-22). No code has been compiled only for or only without the coverage run since ADR-006 was retired, so nothing used it.

## [10.0.0-rc.34] - 2026-11-22

### Added
- **`forge edit --rename` renames a game, a player and a strategy, and every result name that reads them** (ADR-075 step 5, roadmap S-GAMES-EDIT). Renaming the bagel's strategy `hold` meant changing it in `strategies:`, at both levels of `payoffs:`, in `dynamics.start`, and in every formula that reads a result such as `price_war.dominated.lune.hold`, and a missed one was found only when the model failed. `forge edit price_war.yaml --rename price_war.strategies.hold=keep` does all of it; `--rename price_war=war` renames the game (its key, a tournament's `round:`, its results), `--rename price_war.players.lune=shop` a player, `--rename rivalry.players.steady=calm` a tournament's player (`rivalry.score.steady`, `rivalry.match.steady.matcher`), and `--rename inspection.strategies.human.audit=check` one player's strategy in a game that is not symmetric, at that player's level of the payoffs only. Result names are respelled part by part by what each part is, never by searching the text, and comments stay as they were. Forge keeps the change only when it reads the model back as it was but for the name. Every game, player, strategy and tournament player of the examples is renamed and back byte for byte; the players `noisy-tournament.yaml` writes once for two tournaments (`players: *roster`) are refused, since one key would rename a player of both. `forge_edit` and forge-wasm's `edit` take the same paths.
- **Forge Studio: "Make uncertain" on a number input, and "fixed" on a distribution** (ADR-076 step 4, roadmap S-UNCERTAIN). A distribution card had Forge's kinds and a field for each parameter, but no way back to a plain input, and a plain input had no way to become a range except typing the formula in the YAML view. "Make uncertain" on an input's card turns it into a triangle from 80% to 120% of its value, the value its mode and its plan, and adds a simulation to a model that has none; the new card is a distribution card to edit. "fixed", the first chip on a distribution card, makes it an input again with its value kept. Both are written by Forge itself (forge-wasm's `edit`, the `make_uncertain` and `make_certain` of `forge edit`), so the file keeps its layout and comments, and a change Forge cannot make (an input of 0, which has no 80% to 120%; an input of a file that another included file includes too) is shown as Forge words it, with the file as it was. `studio/tests/uncertain.mjs` makes the bagel's demand fixed and its price uncertain and fixed again, checks the file's text, and checks the simulation's numbers after each against `forge simulate`.
- **`forge edit --uncertain` and `--certain`: an input made a distribution, and back** (ADR-075 step 4, roadmap S-EDIT). Making an input uncertain meant writing `value:` and `formula: "=MC.…"` by hand under its name, and a `monte_carlo:` section if the model had none. `forge edit model.yaml --uncertain year.price` does it in the style the file uses (`price: 16   # dollars` becomes a block with the comment kept on the value; a block gets a `formula:` line; a flow mapping gets `formula:` inside): a triangle from 80% to 120% of the value, the value its mode and its plan, or `--uncertain 'year.price==MC.PERT(12, 16, 22)'` for another; a value of 0 needs one. A model with no `monte_carlo:` gets one at the end of the main file (5,000 runs, seed 7, Latin hypercube, the model's results as outputs); an included file's input is changed in that file. `--certain year.price` takes the formula out and keeps the value (`--certain year.price=90` sets another; with none, the distribution's plan figure), and writes a name left with `value:` alone short again, so every input of every example (479, in the main files and the files they include) goes through both and comes back byte for byte. Forge keeps the change only when it reads the model back valid at plan with that formula and nothing else changed. An input of a file that another included file includes too (the bagel's `costs.yaml` under `price_war.yaml`) is refused with Forge's message, because the engine does not plan through nested includes yet; the same models do not simulate either. `forge_edit` and forge-wasm's `edit` take `{"op": "make_uncertain", "path": …, "formula": …}` and `{"op": "make_certain", "path": …, "value": …}`.
- **`forge edit --rename`: a name renamed, and every name that reads it** (ADR-075 step 3, roadmap S-EDIT). Renaming `year.price` meant finding by hand each formula that reads it, in each file, short (`price`) or in full, and each scenario, expectation, simulation output and tornado input that names it; one missed broke the model, or, worse, a short name started reading another scalar. `forge edit model.yaml --rename year.price=unit_price` renames a scalar, `--rename year=annual` a group, and a table or a column the same way; `@costs.rent=lease` renames it in the included file and where the model reads it. What each name reads is found by the calculator's own reader, where it is written: a column's bare name reads its table first, a scalar's short name its group first. Each is written again as it was, full or short, and in full when the short name would read something else once renamed. Forge then reads the model back, and keeps the change only when every name reads what it read, renamed, and nothing else changed; a formula folded over several lines, a key a YAML alias gives two names, and a name of `LET`'s that would read the renamed scalar are refused, with the reason. `forge_edit` and forge-wasm's `edit` take `{"op": "rename", "path": "year.price", "new_name": "unit_price"}`. Every scalar, group, table and column of the 62 example models, and each scalar of a file one includes, is renamed and back to its files byte for byte. A game, a player and a strategy are renamed too (the entry above).
- **`forge edit`: Forge writes a model's text, comments kept** (ADR-075, roadmap S-EDIT). Changing a model meant editing its YAML by hand, or Forge Studio's line search in the page, which the command line and an agent did not have. `forge edit model.yaml --set year.price=17` sets an input, `--formula 'year.margin==…'` a formula, `--add year.tax=0.15` (or `PATH==FORMULA`) adds an input, a formula or a table's column at the end of its group in the indentation of the keys there (`--after NAME` at the top level), and `--delete` takes one out with its lines; `@costs.rent` changes the file included as `costs`. Every byte outside the change stays as it was. A change is made only when Forge reads the model back valid with that change and nothing else; when one cannot be made, no file is written and the message names it and says why. Each file written is kept as it was in a `.bak`, and `--dry-run` shows the new text. The MCP tool `forge_edit` (the server has 22 tools) and forge-wasm's `edit` take the same changes as JSON. Names are found in any alphabet (`hypothèses.coût_unitaire`, `假设.单价`). It is held to Forge Studio's own bytes (each case of `studio/tests/text.mjs`) and to the 62 example models: each of their 376 inputs and 723 formulas changes alone, and an input, a formula and a column added to each of their groups and tables is deleted again to the file as it was.
- **Forge reads YAML through `yaml-edit` 0.3.6** (Apache-2.0), a lossless syntax tree, to write a model's text (ADR-075, decision 5). It makes forge_wasm_bg.wasm **154,761 bytes larger gzipped** (2,011,484 to 2,166,245; ADR-070's ceiling is 3,000,000). Its `rowan` brings `hashbrown` 0.14 beside 0.17, a second version the maintainer allowed in `clippy.toml` (no release of `rowan` takes a newer one).
- **An uncertain model on Forge Studio's cards** (ADR-076). `examples/bagel/bagel_simulate.yaml` opened on a red Problem pill, a banner sending you to Analyze, "Not calculated" on every result and its uncertain inputs listed as results. Every card now shows the plan, with each uncertain input at its plan figure, so a slider answers in milliseconds as on any model. Each result `monte_carlo.outputs` asks about has its range under it: P5 to P95, the mean, a mark at the plan and one line per threshold (the bagel's year: a plan of 786.32 over a mean of −15,925.94, above 0 in 34.8% of runs), and a column its rows in a table. The range is `forge simulate` with the model's own runs, seed and sampling, worked out again 400 ms after a change: the previous one stays, dimmed and marked running, and an answer a newer change has overtaken is dropped. The pill says "uncertain: 4 inputs are ranges". An input that is a distribution is a card of its own: its plan, the mean of the runs, Forge's kinds (Normal, Triangular, Uniform, PERT, Lognormal, Discrete, from its function list) and one field per parameter, named as the list names them (`=MC.PERT(min, mode, max)`); a change writes the formula, kept when Forge reads it back. No shape is drawn. `studio/tests/uncertain.mjs` checks every number on screen against `forge calculate --plan` and `forge simulate` run natively, for the seven uncertain models of the example sets.
- **`forge calculate --plan`** (ADR-076). `forge calculate` refuses a model with `MC.*` inputs. With `--plan` it calculates it once with each uncertain input at its plan figure, the rule `forge simulate` uses for each output's value at plan (`value:` beside the formula, or else the distribution's likeliest value), after the scenario, prints those figures and writes nothing. The MCP `forge_calculate` tool, the REST API's calculate and forge-wasm's `calculate` take `plan: true`, and their answer's `at_plan` lists the figures. Every output of every uncertain example equals `forge simulate`'s `at_plan` (`src/cli/commands/plan.rs`). Without it, the refusal now names `--plan`.
- **Help in Forge Studio** (ADR-077). The page linked to no documentation. Each heading now has a "?" (scenarios, inputs, results, tables, games, a tournament, the dynamics, each of Analyze's tools, a decision tree, real options, a bootstrap, a Bayesian network) that opens a few sentences, the terms the section uses, examples to open and the manual on GitHub at the tag of the release that is running, so the text matches the engine on screen. While a formula is typed, the line under the suggestions is Forge's syntax and description of the first function offered; while a key is typed in the YAML editor, the schema's description of it. The manual is linked rather than bundled: at 77 KB gzipped it would fit, but its links to source files, ADRs and examples work only on GitHub. `studio/tests/help.mjs` fails when a link names a heading the docs no longer have.
- **Every model in Forge Studio has a Model view** (ADR-077). A file that is a decision tree, real options, a bootstrap or a Bayesian network (the bagel's second shop, the wait, the first month and the slow week, and their kind in the acquisition and the microgrid) opened on an empty page with no message; its tool ran only on the Tools tab, as JSON. It now opens on a card drawn from its tool's answer: the best choice, its expected value, the plan and what can happen on it; the value with each option against deciding now; the estimate and each interval; each node's chances, with a state tapped as evidence. Checked by `studio/tests/analyses.mjs`, number by number against the tool.
- **Forge Studio shows a set's models as questions** (ADR-077). The bagel opened on its plan, and its simulation, its two games, its decision tree, real option, bootstrap and Bayesian network were reached only through Files, by file name. The welcome card of a set of several models now lists them as the questions they answer ("Cut the price if the rival does?"), each marked with its kind, and a tap opens one; a strip at the top of the Model view opens any of them later, and Files says each one's question. The kind is Forge's: `model` answers `analyses`, the analysis sections a file has (`monte_carlo`, `tornado`, `decision_tree`, `real_options`, `bootstrap`, `bayesian_network`), recorded by the parser as it skips them. Checked by `studio/tests/sets.mjs` on every set.
- **A link opens one model of an example set in Forge Studio.** `?example=` opened a set on its own main model, so the bagel's price war was two taps away from any link (Files, then Open), and a post could not link to it. `https://forge.mollendorff.ai/?example=bagel&model=price_war.yaml` now opens the price war on its games, as Open does; a file the set does not have opens the set's own model, as before. `studio/tests/games.mjs` opens the link, checks every cell of the game against `forge game` with no tap on Files, and checks the fallback.

- **A model states what it should give, and `forge validate` checks it, scenario by scenario (ADR-074, roadmap T2-SCENARIO-CHECKS).** `forge validate` compared each stored `value:` with its formula for the file as written only, and took no scenario, so a model's figures for a scenario, or a game's results, which have no `value:`, could be pinned only by a script beside the model: kinship-protocol's `scripts/check_models.py` pins 332 such figures. Model format 5.6.0 adds `expect:`, at the top of the file for the file as written and in each scenario for that scenario: any name `forge calculate` prints, a game's results included, as a number or a formula over numbers only (`kinship.barrier: =2/11`; a formula that reads a name is refused). `forge validate` calculates the file and each scenario that has an `expect:`, and lists every result that differs, with the scenario, the name and both numbers; a result passes within 10⁻⁶ × max(1, |expected|), a name the model does not give fails, and a scenario that does not calculate fails it, named. The command, the MCP tool `forge_validate`, `POST /api/v1/validate` and Forge Studio now share one function (`src/cli/commands/validate.rs`), so the MCP server and the REST API also refuse a table with columns of different lengths, as the command did. `forge_calculate` reports the expectations of the run it calculates, and Studio shows each on its card under the scenario applied, marked when Forge gives something else; Studio's check of an edit leaves them out, so an edit that moves a result away from its expectation is kept (`studio/tests/expect.mjs`). Before 5.6.0 a scenario's `expect:` is refused with the format that has it, a top-level group named `expect` is a group as before, and `forge upgrade --to 5.6.0` refuses a file with such a group. `examples/games/stag-hunt.yaml` states its mixed equilibrium, (3 − c) / 4, at each of its costs and where its population ends, worked out by hand; breaking one (=5/9 for 5/8) makes `validate` fail with `scenario c1`, `Expected: 0.555556 (=5/9)`, `Forge: 0.625`.

- **A Bayesian network reads the model and feeds it (ADR-074, steps 5 and 6; roadmap T2-BAYESIAN-LINK).** A prior or table entry was a number only (`invalid type: string, expected f64`), and `forge bayesian` took no scenario, so a network could not follow the model's inputs, and its answers could not feed a formula: kinship-protocol checks its network at nine settings by rewriting the file for each. Now:
  - **A probability may be a formula over the model's scalars** (`prior: ["=1 - p", "=p"]`). It is worked out after them, under the scenario applied. Each row must then still add up to 1, and each entry be from 0 to 1, or the calculation fails with the node named.
  - **`forge bayesian --scenario <name>`** works the network out under the scenario, and `forge_bayesian` and forge-wasm's `bayesian` take `scenario`.
  - **Named queries give posteriors as scalars of the model.** `bayesian_network.queries.<q>: {node, evidence}` gives `bayesian_network.<q>.<state>`, which formulas, `expect:`, `forge simulate` and `forge tornado` read. The network is a cell of the calculation, ordered as a game is, and a network that reads its own results is refused as a cycle. A query is checked when the file is read: a node or a state that does not exist is named, with the query.
  - **`examples/expected_loss.yaml`** feeds the probability of default given the economy into an expected loss. Beside it, Bayes' rule works the same figures out in formulas, and `expect:` pins both in the file and two scenarios (0.0975 and 58,500 as written, 0.1254 and 75,240 in a recession). `forge examples expected-loss` lists it.
  - Tests show `forge simulate` moving a prior that reads an `MC.*` input, and `forge tornado` one that reads a scalar, each run equal to Bayes' rule by hand.
  - `forge export` writes the network's results as numbers as of the export, as it does a game's. `forge import` brings them back as group `network_results`, with a warning, and rewrites the formulas that read them. A group named `bayesian_network` would be read as the network.

### Fixed
- **`SORT(values, -1)` no longer sorts its order in with the values.** Every argument was read as values to sort, so `SORT(t.data, -1)` of 3, 1, 4, 1, 5 gave 5, 4, 3, 1, 1, -1, and `SORT(t.data, 1)` had an extra 1. Only the first argument is sorted now.
- **`INDEX` of one value is that value, as in Excel** (`INDEX(5, 1)` is 5, `INDEX(5, 2)` is out of bounds). It was the error "INDEX requires an array".
- **`forge game --help` shows its long help and examples.** It showed one line: the command's doc comment in the list of commands replaced the help written with its arguments. `forge edit` is written the same way, and a test holds both.
- **`forge edit --rename` rewrites the names a Bayesian network's formulas read.** A prior or table entry may be a formula (ADR-074), but a rename left it reading the old name, so Forge refused the rename ("reads the new text as more than this rename") on `examples/expected_loss.yaml`, and `every_name_of_every_example_is_renamed_and_back` failed on main once both changes had merged. `a_scalar_a_networks_prior_reads_is_renamed_in_the_prior` fails on 803523e.
- **`forge calculate --scenario` no longer writes the scenario's results into the file (ADR-074, question 5).** Without `--dry-run` it stored them as the file's own `value:`s: with `w: 0.6` and `twice: =w * 2` stored as 1.2, `calculate --scenario short` (`w: 0.4`) stored 0.8, and `forge validate` then reported the value out of date (measured on rc.33). Under a scenario nothing is written now, and it says so; `forge_calculate` and `POST /api/v1/calculate` follow the same rule, and an inline call under a scenario returns no `calculated_content`. Export never read the stored values (it calculates the model itself), so no reader needed them. The test `calculating_under_a_scenario_writes_nothing` fails on 2f83fa8.

### Changed
- **`forge bayesian` reads its file as a model, so the file needs `_forge_version`** (ADR-074). Before, a bare `bayesian_network:` section was read on its own. A failed formula the network does not read does not fail the command.
- **A network's probabilities are each checked to be from 0 to 1**, as well as each row to add up to 1. A literal entry of −0.1 was accepted before. Evidence that cannot happen in the network (probability 0) is refused with the query named; inference gave every state 0.
- **The architecture documentation is rewritten from the code** (`docs/architecture/`). Pages 00 to 05 and 08 described Forge around v1.2 to v5.0 (`xlformula_engine`, `columns:` keys, per-table graphs, petgraph, an API "for every operation") under notes saying they were superseded, and linked to two pages deleted in 2025; nothing described the code as it is. Eleven pages now do, each with Mermaid diagrams GitHub draws in place: the C4 system context and containers (00), the components and the one-core, four-doors pattern (01), the data model and format versions (02), formula evaluation (03), dependency resolution with `PREV` and games (04), Excel (05), the command line, MCP and the browser (06), the tests and gates (07), the REST API (08), the analysis engines (09) and checklists for extending Forge (10). The index lists every ADR with its status; five statuses were wrong (ADR-004 is enforced, ADR-006 and ADR-042 are superseded, ADR-064 is accepted, ADR-076 has three steps built), and dead paths and links in ten ADRs point where the code is now. A new check, `validation/repo/src/architecture_docs.rs`, fails when a link or an anchor in these pages breaks, a source path they name is gone, an ADR is missing from the index, or a module of `src/lib.rs` is missing from the module map. Every diagram was rendered with Mermaid 11, and the example model, the error report and the workbook formulas the pages show come from running `forge`.
- **The coverage floors in CI are 99.7 / 99.3 / 99.7** (lines / functions / regions), up from 99.0 / 98.1 / 98.7, from CI's run on PR #366 (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).
- **ADR-074 decides how a scenario states its results, and how a Bayesian network reads the model (roadmap T2-SCENARIO-CHECKS, T2-BAYESIAN-LINK). Nothing is built yet.** `forge validate` checks stored values for the base case only, so kinship-protocol pins its figures, most of them results of scenarios, with a Python script. Decided, with that script's 382 checks in front of it: an `expect:` in each scenario and at the top of the file (model format 5.6.0) names any scalar the calculation gives, game results included, as a number or a formula over numbers, and `forge validate` checks every one within 10⁻⁶ (relative above 1), naming the scenario, the name and both numbers. The MCP server, the REST API and Forge Studio get the same report. `forge calculate --scenario` will stop writing: measured on rc.33, it wrote the scenario's results into the file's own stored values, which `validate` then failed. A Bayesian network will take formulas over scalars in its priors and tables, `forge bayesian` will take `--scenario`, and named queries will give each posterior as a scalar of the model, which formulas, `expect:`, `simulate` and `tornado` read; `=BAYES()` is left out. What is left out, and why, is in the ADR.
- **More of the command line is tested (T1-COVERAGE-GATE), and a YAML variance report that cannot be written says "Failed to write file"** where it said "Failed to create file" for a missing folder. The tests cover `forge functions --json`, the in-memory export, `--verbose`, `--seed`, `--scenario` and `--output` of `simulate`, `bootstrap`, `decision-tree` and `scenarios`, `validate`'s errors, and each failure of `export`, `probability seek` and `report`. `bootstrap`, `decision-tree` and `scenarios` share one loader and one result writer instead of copies, and `forge scenarios` reads the model once.
- **The evaluator is tested in full (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Every function of `core/array_calculator` and the function registry has tests for an error in each argument, its defaults and its edges, each asserting a value or a message: the conditional aggregates with a shorter or non-numeric sum range, `IF` over arrays, `VDB` from a part period in the second half of the life (worked by hand through LibreOffice's steps), `RATE` checked by `FV`, holidays from a column, a tilde that escapes nothing, an included file's cycle and its unreadable row mismatches. Code that could not fail no longer returns an error: `TODAY` and `NOW`, `ROW` and `COLUMN`, and the tokenizer's operators (six `#[allow(clippy::unnecessary_wraps)]` gone), a 29 February anniversary and the day 1899-12-30. The check of a short name against the included values, which are all `@namespace.path` and so never a short name, is gone. Tests that passed whatever the result (`if let` with no `else`, `matches!(v, Value::Number(_))`) now compare the result. The test count in README.md and VALIDATION.md is over 3,050.
- **The analytics engines are tested in full (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** The sampler, scenario overrides and outputs, the Monte Carlo workbook, correlations, bootstrap (median, BCa tails), tornado, sensitivity, decision trees and Bayesian networks have tests for their failures and edges, each asserting an exact result or message. Errors that cannot happen are now infallible. One fix: the Monte Carlo workbook's sample sheets had as many rows as whichever column a hash map listed first; they have as many as the longest column.

### Fixed
- **Analyze's simulation lists the outputs in the model's order** (ADR-076). `months.profit` came as row 1, row 10, row 11, row 12, row 2, …: `forge simulate`'s JSON keys `outputs` by name, and a JSON object's keys are written sorted as text. The answer now also gives `output_order`, the order of `monte_carlo.outputs` with a column's rows in order, and Analyze lists them so. The test `the_json_lists_the_outputs_in_the_order_configured` in `src/monte_carlo/engine.rs` failed before.
- **Analyze's scenarios card says what Forge means** (ADR-076). For a model whose scenarios have no probabilities (the acquisition's `deal_risk.yaml`) it showed "Validation error: No scenario has a probability…"; it now says first that they are choices to compare, not chances, and gives Forge's message without the error kind (`studio/tests/scenarios.mjs`).
- **The documentation's dead ends** (ADR-077). docs/GLOSSARY.md, linked from the architecture documents but missing, is written, each term with where to read more. EXAMPLES.md named `MC.Correlated`, which is not a function (correlations are `correlations:` under `monte_carlo:`), and pointed to a `test-data/v1.0/` that does not exist. FEATURES.md showed `_forge_version` 8.4.0 to 8.7.0, which Forge does not read (5.1.0 now), said that strategy shares changing by generation were not in the format (they are `dynamics:`), and had no section for goal seek, break-even and sensitivity; it has one now, and `format:` and `dynamics:` have headings of their own, so a link can land on them. docs/architecture/README.md says ADR-055 and ADR-056 are built.
- **Forge Studio's Tools tab shows a whole answer** (ADR-077). An answer was cut at 6,000 characters, so the bagel price war's `forge_game` answer ended in the middle of a player's list. The answer is now shown whole, in a box that scrolls, with Copy and Download (JSON); `studio/tests/games.mjs` checks that what is on screen is the tool's answer.

### Removed
- **`ReverseFormulaTranslator`, from the library.** `forge import` reads formulas with `workbook_refs::reverse`; the old translator was exported but called by nothing in Forge, its WebAssembly module or its validation crates. The rest of `src/excel/` is now at 100% coverage (T1-COVERAGE-GATE): `text_results.rs` reads back only the workbook the writer saved, so its archive and UTF-8 checks are `expect`s with the reason; `WorkbookRefs::gives_a_date`, called by nothing, is removed; tests cover a sign and an operator in an array constant, a quote in an imported constant, `VARIANCE_STATUS` with a number threshold, a name `irr` that is not a call, a LAMBDA not called in a LET, `PREV` without its `)`, `IFS` and a `SWITCH` without a default over dates, and a formula the parser does not read on import. A text read back from a workbook is written with a quote inside doubled (`dates/print.rs`); import refuses such a text before it gets there, so no result changes.

## [10.0.0-rc.33] - 2026-11-22

Games of many players, and strategies that spread (ADR-073). A symmetric game of any number of players is written as one payoff for each strategy over how many of the others play each, not as its whole table: kinship-protocol's public goods game of five players is two lines, not 32 cells. Forge finds the mixed equilibria in which every player plays alike, for games of two strategies, and says whether a population returns to each; the `dynamics:` key follows the shares of a population generation by generation. Forge Studio draws a game of five players by count and the dynamics as a chart, and the bagel example has a price war that spreads through the city and a festival five shops will not pay for. All of it is checked against Gambit, egttools and R.
