Wrong answers fixed: `forge export` wrote a range `a:b` as an Excel range, `forge real-options` gave Black-Scholes prices off by up to 1.2e-7, correlated draws and BCa intervals used a normal distribution off by up to 9e-11, and a decision tree with a chance node at its root listed no outcomes in its optimal path. Wrong answers fixed, so the two weeks in docs/STABILITY.md start again from rc.33 (no earlier than 2026-12-06).

### Fixed
- **`forge export` no longer writes a range `a:b` as an Excel range (a wrong formula).** Forge reads `revenue:year` as the text of its two ends; the exporter wrote `SUM($A$2:$A$4:$C$2:$C$4)`, which Excel reads as cells. It is now refused like `[ ]`, with the reason "a range a:b has no Excel equivalent", and so is a comma outside any call (`=1, 2` was written as it stood). The tests are `a_range_or_a_comma_outside_a_call_is_not_written` in `src/excel/workbook_refs.rs`.
- **`forge import` refuses an Excel formula with a `(` never closed or a comma outside any call.** `=SUM(1` and `=1, 2` came back unchanged, for Forge to refuse later; they now give "( without )" and "a comma outside a call" (`what_excel_does_not_write_is_refused` in `src/excel/workbook_refs/reverse.rs`).
- **An unknown key in a game is named (a wrong message).** `forge game`, `validate` and every command that reads a model said `'games.hd' is not any of the forms allowed here (a mapping)` for a game with a key it does not have (`spread:` for `dynamics:`), and nothing about the key. A game is a table game or a tournament, an unknown key fails both at the game itself, and the message had nothing to prefer. Now, in a mapping that fails every form at the mapping itself, the form that fails in the fewest places is the one meant, if one does: `'games.hd.spread': there is no key 'spread' here. The keys here are description, dynamics, payoffs, players, strategies, symmetric.`, with `Did you mean 'dynamics'?` for a near miss, and a misspelt `tournment:` says it is `tournament`. Where two forms fail alike the message is the general one, as before. The test `an_unknown_key_in_a_game_is_named` in `src/parser/schema_errors.rs` fails on b47c0f7 with the general message.
- **A chance node at the root of a decision tree lists its outcomes in the optimal path (a wrong output).** `forge decision-tree` printed `Flip → (await outcome)` and then nothing, because the branches were looked up among the named nodes and the root is not one of them; a chance node anywhere else listed its branches. The test `a_terminal_node_ends_the_path_and_adds_no_outcome` in `src/decision_trees/engine.rs` fails on 6e4a729.
- **Correlated draws and BCa bootstrap intervals use a normal distribution function right to the last digits.** Both used the `statrs` crate's, which is off by up to 9e-11 for |z| from 0.9 up (measured against mpmath at 50 digits; its inverse, which Forge also uses, is right to 2e-16). They now use the one `NORM.S.DIST` uses, right to about 3 units in the last place. A correlated simulation moves in the tenth digit: on `examples/vineyard/vineyard_risk.yaml` the median profit is 39,680.667472, not 39,680.667474, and the 95th percentile 153,625.617060, not 153,625.617063, both what Python computes from Forge's draws; the printed numbers, to the cent, are the same.
- **`forge real-options` with `method: black_scholes` gives prices right to the last digits (a wrong answer).** Its normal distribution was a hand-written approximation of `erf` (Numerical Recipes), off by up to 1.2e-7: on `examples/real-options.yaml` with `method: black_scholes` the wait-two-years option was worth 3,237,829.74 and is 3,237,829.65, expanding moves by 0.014 and abandoning by 0.001 (the decision is the same). It now uses the distribution function `NORM.S.DIST` uses, right to about 3 units in the last place; the `statrs` crate's, which the code might have used instead, is right to only 11 digits (its `erf` is off by 4.9e-11 at 0.5). Calls and puts are checked against scipy 1.18.1 and mpmath at 40 digits, to within 1e-12 of the spot.
- **`forge bayesian` lists its evidence in name order.** `-e sales=slow -e weather=rainy` printed the two lines in either order from one run to the next (they were kept in a hash map); the printed probabilities and the written result were not affected. The test that gives the whole output found it, failing on some runs.

### Removed
- **Library functions of the simulation that nothing called (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `forge simulate`, `forge mcp` and Forge Studio draw every value through `Distribution::inverse_cdf`; a second way of drawing, `Distribution::sample` and `sample_n`, was used only by its own tests, and so were `Distribution::theoretical_mean` and `theoretical_variance`, `Statistics::probability_greater_than`, `probability_less_than` and `get_percentiles`, `Histogram::bin_centers`, and `MonteCarloEngine::sampler` and `sampler_mut`. They are gone, and with them the `rand_distr` dependency. `theoretical_variance` was wrong for two distributions: 2.96 for `MC.PERT(0, 3, 10)`, whose variance is 3.32, and -0.83 for `MC.Lognormal(2000, 500)`, whose variance is 250,000. A `Distribution` is built only by its checked constructors now: its `dist_type` is read with `dist_type()`. No number Forge prints changes.

### Changed
- **The Excel translation is covered in full (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `excel/previous_rows.rs`, `workbook_refs.rs`, `workbook_refs/reverse.rs` and `workbook_refs/local_names.rs` are at 100% lines, functions and regions: each refusal of a malformed formula, workbook reference, LET, LAMBDA and COUNTUNIQUE has a test of its message, and a LET name Excel cannot write (`coût`) is `_xlpm.v`. Paths no input reaches are gone: `calls_function`, which nothing called, and checks of a function name the caller had already matched. One `allow` attribute less (`too_many_arguments` on a function that no longer needs it).
- **The documentation says what games can do now (ADR-073).** The README's `forge game` row and its two lines on what is checked named only equilibria and tournaments, Gambit and axelrod: they now name games of many players, the symmetric mixes with their stability, the `dynamics:` key, egttools and the R check. `docs/EXAMPLES.md` has a Games section for the ten models of `examples/games` and the bagel's two. The architecture index calls ADR-068 and ADR-073 built, as they are, and ADR-068's example count says ten with ADR-073's.
- **The coverage floors in `ci.yml` rise to 99.0% lines, 98.1% functions and 98.7% regions (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** They were 98.9, 97.8 and 98.6. CI's Coverage job on main after #341 measured 99.075%, 98.153% and 98.789%.
- **`TEXT` and `format:` number formats are covered in full (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Each code Forge refuses (an open quote, a `\` or `_` or `*` at the end, `E+` without zeros, an open `[`, a colour number outside 1 to 56, six `m`, a date mixed with digits, text between grouped digits, a point after the exponent, a comma that is not between digits, two decimal points) has a test of its exact message, and the numbers that Gnumeric 1.12 and LibreOffice 24.2 both write (grouped percent, scientific with text after it, `E-00`, zero, a mantissa that rounds up to a new power) are asserted. Where the two disagree (a percent sign after an exponent, a lone `.,` section, a lowercase `e`), nothing is pinned as right: the lowercase `e` test says it records Forge's behaviour and that Excel was not checked. The code that no format could reach is gone, and the pieces written as they are share one function. Nothing `TEXT` returns changes.
- **Tests for the printing and writing of `forge simulate` results, the files held in memory and the formula parser (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** A column of rows with its thresholds, each output file and each failure to write one, the nesting limit of every kind of link, and the message of every malformed formula. `SimulationResult::to_json` returns the text, not a `Result` that could never be an error. Nothing else in Forge changes.
- **The coverage floors in `ci.yml` rise to 98.9% lines, 97.8% functions and 98.6% regions (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** They were 98.6, 97.3 and 98.3. CI's Coverage job on main after #337 measured 98.90%, 97.82% and 98.61%; the floors are that, rounded down to a tenth, as before, and not from a local run. Nothing in Forge changes.
- **The coverage floors in `ci.yml` rise to 98.6% lines, 97.3% functions and 98.3% regions (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** They were 98.2, 96.8 and 97.9. CI's Coverage job on main after #333 measured 98.67%, 97.37% and 98.37%; the floors are that, rounded down to a tenth, as before, and not from a local run. Nothing in Forge changes.
- **`forge real-options` is covered in full, and says what its approximations are (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `docs/FEATURES.md` listed Monte Carlo as a third valuation method; `method: monte_carlo` values each option on a binomial tree of 200 steps and does not use `monte_carlo_iterations`, and the page now says so. New tests state the other approximations as they are: a switch option is the larger of expanding and contracting by a fifth, whatever its own factors, and a compound option half an expansion plus waiting a year at half the cost. Theta and rho of a call are checked against mpmath. The decision no longer has branches for an analysis with no options, which `validate` refuses.
- **`forge goal-seek` and `forge break-even` calculate the model fewer times per search (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** The bisection keeps the output at each end of the interval as it goes, so it no longer calculates the model again at the low end after widening the range, or at the answer it has already calculated. The answers, the iteration counts and the printed text are unchanged (checked bit for bit on ten small models and by the examples' tests). New tests use one input and one output, so each answer is known by hand: the search ranges from a positive, a negative and a zero input and their widening, a target out of reach, an output that cannot be calculated at either end, at a widened end or at a midpoint, a step the target falls inside (which never converges, ending at either neighbouring float), and `--verbose`.
- **`forge bayesian` prints no "Query Result" heading above an error.** It printed the heading, then said the variable or the evidence was wrong; the query is now asked first and the heading printed with its answer. The same code reads the network, the evidence and the query for `forge bayesian` and for the library call, and `-o` is written by the shared writer of the prediction commands. Exact tests give each output, error and written file, with the probabilities worked by hand (P(roadwork | rainy, slow) = 3/11).
- **The Bayesian engine, its inference, the decision tree and real options configs and engines, and the model's input overrides are covered by tests that assert a result (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Measured with `cargo llvm-cov --lib`, none of their files has a line, function or region without one. Code that validation already rules out is gone: `BeliefPropagation::query` and `query_with_evidence` were two copies of the same 60 lines (the first is now the second with no evidence), and the decision tree's optimal path, policy and outcome collector no longer re-check what validation guarantees. The overrides of a model's inputs moved to `src/types/overrides.rs` (`types.rs` was 1,462 lines), and an override of a date in an included file now gives the message the scalar's gives when the name has nothing after the include. The floors in `ci.yml` are unchanged until CI measures this.

### Added
- **The bagel's games of many shops (ADR-073).** `examples/bagel/price_war.yaml` follows the price war across the city: with a tenth of Montreal's bagel shops at $14 today, and each month the prices that earn more gaining shops in proportion (`dynamics:`), 99.1% of the shops cut after two years, each then earning 19,190 a month against 21,061 today; with loyal regulars the shops at $14 fall to 2.4%. `examples/bagel/street_fund.yaml` is new: the street's five food shops each asked for 1,000 for a December festival whose lift in sales they all share, a public goods game of five players in the count form; keeping pays 374.85 more whatever the others do, so no one pays, though all paying would leave each 2,125.73 better off, and a borough that matches every dollar makes everyone pay. The numbers are worked out by hand and in Python from the plan's margin, and asserted in `src/cli/commands/game.rs`; both are in Forge Studio's bagel set.
- **Games of many players and the dynamics are checked again in R, and through the MCP server.** `validation/games/many_players.R` works out from the payoffs alone, in R, every generation of the dynamics (and the whole path where its end is known by hand), the symmetric mixed equilibria of games of two strategies as the roots of the population function, with their stability and payoffs, the volunteer's dilemma's closed form and the public goods game's pure equilibria, on the same seeded games as the Python checks and the bagel's price war: 86 games and 3,713 generations on every pull request, 0 failed. The bagel's price war is also stepped against egttools, and its festival compared cell by cell and against Gambit. A test calls `forge_game` in-process on the bagel's price war and festival and on the volunteer's dilemma, by path and as text with their includes.
- **Forge Studio draws a game of many players by count, and the dynamics as a chart (ADR-073).** Studio drew a game of three or more players as the list of its profiles, 32 rows for the public goods game of five, and drew none of the dynamics. A game whose players are alike (each has the same strategies, and gets the same payoff for its own strategy and the others' split, read off Forge's cells) is now one row for a player's own strategy and one column for each way the others split: the public goods game is 10 numbers, a cell marked where a player of a pure equilibrium stands. Its pure equilibria are listed by split, with how many profiles each is: at r = 5, the 32 are 6 lines. A game with `dynamics:` has the shares by generation as a chart, with the last shares and average payoff named beside it, a generation's shares where the chart is pointed at or touched, and the path as a table. Each equilibrium says whether a population returns to it, where Forge says. The Games set adds `public-goods.yaml`, `volunteers-dilemma.yaml` and `hawk-dove.yaml`. `studio/tests/games.mjs` checks every number against Forge's report: each count against every cell with that strategy and split, the splits and their payoffs, the symmetric mixed equilibria, the last shares (hawks at 2/3; the stag hunt all stag from 0.8 and all hare in `few_stags`), and the generation under the pointer.
- **A symmetric game of many players is written in two lines, not as its whole table (ADR-073, roadmap T2-GAME-THEORY-MORE).** `symmetric: true` took exactly two players, so kinship-protocol's public goods game of five players was 32 cells of five payoffs, written by a script. Now a symmetric game of any number of players can give one payoff for each strategy, a formula over how many of the other players play each (`pg.others.contribute`), and Forge expands it into the table every result is worked out from: equilibria, dominance, the even mix, `forge game`, tornado and the rest. `examples/games/public-goods.yaml` has five players: below a multiplier of 5 everyone keeps (10 each, where contributing together would give 16), at 5 all 32 profiles are equilibria, and above it everyone contributes, as by hand (keeping pays 10 (1 - r / 5) more whatever the others do). `validation/games` checks the expansion cell by cell and the pure equilibria against Gambit, on that game and 300 seeded ones of three to five players. A symmetric game of three or more players written out as a table is refused with a message that shows the count form, and a symmetric game's message now says every player needs the same strategies, not two players.
- **The mixed equilibria of a symmetric game of two strategies, for any number of players, and whether a population returns to each (ADR-073, question 4).** A game of three or more players had its pure equilibria only, so in the volunteer's dilemma (one of five must do a chore) Forge listed the five profiles with one volunteer and nothing on what happens when no one knows who: the mix in which each volunteers with 1 − (c / b)^(1 / (n − 1)). Now Forge finds every mix in which all players play alike, as the roots between 0 and 1 of the population function g(x) = Σ C(n−1, k) xᵏ (1−x)ⁿ⁻¹⁻ᵏ dₖ (dₖ: what the first strategy pays more than the second with k of the others playing it), and lists them after the pure ones by increasing share. `<game>.equilibrium_<k>.stable` is 1 when a population returns to that equilibrium after a small change in the shares and 0 when it moves away, for every equilibrium in which all play alike, pure or mixed, two players included (the stag hunt: both pure ones stable, the mix at 3/4 not). Where the two strategies pay the same whatever the others do (the public goods game at r = n) every mix is an equilibrium: `degenerate` is 1, the tie is named, and none is listed. Such a game of three or more players has no `equilibria` count, since mixes in which players differ are not looked for, and both `forge game` and the error for that name say so. `examples/games/volunteers-dilemma.yaml` has five players: each volunteers with 0.204729, and nobody does with 0.318108, against 0.16 for two players, as by hand. The roots come from the `polycool` crate (linebender), not `roots` as ADR-073 first chose: measured, `roots` 0.0.8 missed a root in 41,413 of 119,818 such polynomials of five to eight players; with polycool and three steps from the exact algebra around it (a root at either end divided out, a root where g touches 0 found from g′, two roots with g within the tie between them made one), all of 31,500 polynomials of two to eight players had the exact number of roots (sympy). `validation/games/symmetric.py` checks the volunteer's dilemma of 2 to 8 players against the closed form, and seeded games of three to five players against the exact roots and Gambit's `enumpoly_solve`, every equilibrium Gambit finds being one of Forge's, with each payoff and `stable` worked out with fractions. For two players, the symmetric equilibria are the support enumeration's, as before: a test compares them on 2,000 seeded games. `forge game -o` has `stable` on such an equilibrium and `symmetric_searched` on the game.
- **Strategies that spread: the `dynamics:` key of a symmetric game (ADR-073, question 3).** An equilibrium says which states are stable, not which one a population reaches or from where: in the stag hunt, all-stag and all-hare are both equilibria, and Forge said nothing on which a population ends in. Now a symmetric game, of any number of players and strategies, in the table form or the count form, can have `dynamics: {start: {stag: 0.8, hare: 0.2}, generations: 100, baseline: 0}`, and Forge follows the shares by the discrete replicator equation, x′ = x (f + b) / (f̄ + b), generation by generation, where f is what a strategy pays against the population over every way the other players' strategies can fall. `start`, `generations` and `baseline` can be formulas, so a scenario or a tornado moves them. `<game>.dynamics.share.<strategy>` and `<game>.dynamics.payoff` are the shares and the average payoff after the last generation; `forge game` prints every generation, and `-o` writes them (`dynamics.path`), as Forge Studio's data has them. A strategy still played that pays no more than minus the baseline would take its share below 0, so it is refused with the strategy, the generation and a baseline that is enough. `examples/games/hawk-dove.yaml` (V = 4, C = 6) goes from 10% hawks to 2/3, as by hand (V / C); `examples/games/stag-hunt.yaml` now has dynamics too, all stag from 0.8 and all hare from 0.7. `validation/games/dynamics.py` checks every generation against egttools 0.2.1's replicator gradient, with each average payoff worked out exactly, on those games, the volunteer's dilemma of five players, rock, paper, scissors of three, the examples and 300 seeded games of two to five players. That suite found a fault before release: the shares were divided by f̄ + b, which equals their sum only on the simplex, so where f̄ is below 0 a rounding grew by b / (f̄ + b) each generation, and in a seeded game of three players the share of one strategy passed 1 in generation 23; each step now divides by the sum, the same number on the simplex.
- **The quantiles of each distribution are checked against scipy.** `MC.Normal`, `MC.Triangular` (symmetric and not), `MC.Uniform`, `MC.PERT` and `MC.Lognormal` at five probabilities each, within 1e-9 of scipy 1.18.1. New tests also give each refused distribution, threshold, correlation and engine setting its exact message, and `monte_carlo/distributions.rs`, `statistics.rs` and `engine.rs` are fully covered. Two lint allows went with the removed code, so the budget in `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs` falls from 36 to 34.

## [10.0.0-rc.32] - 2026-11-21

Wrong answers fixed, and a stricter, standard validator. `forge import` read a range over part of a table's column as the whole column, SWITCH with two arguments gave an answer instead of an error, and a NaN or an infinity in a model gave different answers in Forge, in its workbook and after the round trip; all three are fixed. A YAML merge key (`<<`) is refused with one message wherever it is written (ADR-071), and the `jsonschema` crate checks every model again (ADR-072). `forge import` also reads workbooks it did not write, and there are two new examples: `inherited-budget` and the takeover's rival bidder as a game. Wrong answers were fixed, so the two weeks in docs/STABILITY.md start again from rc.32.

### Fixed
- **A YAML merge key (`<<: *base`) is refused with one message, wherever it is written (ADR-071, roadmap T1-YAML-MERGE-KEYS).** Each part of a model met a key named `<<` in its own way: in a tournament's players it was "not any of the forms allowed here", in a payoff row a strategy named `<<`, in a scenario "must be a number, not a mapping", in a `decision_tree:` branch an "unknown field". A scalar written `y: {<<: *x}` was accepted without a word, and no scalar `y` existed: a formula that read it failed with "Unknown variable", and with none its value was lost without a word. Now every model text becomes YAML through one function, `parser::read_yaml`, which refuses a merge key anywhere with its place: `'scenarios.more.<<' is a YAML merge key (<<), which Forge does not read: YAML 1.2 dropped merge keys`. This is in the model, an included file, each document of a multi-document file, `forge upgrade`, the MCP server's inline models and the commands that read their own section (`simulate`, `scenarios`, `tornado`, `bayesian`, `bootstrap`, `real-options`, `decision-tree`). It replaces the scenario message the entry below names for `<<:`. Anchors and aliases (`players: *roster`) work as before; docs/FEATURES.md says so. Found building kinship-protocol's models; the test in `src/parser/merge_keys.rs` (`every_place_gives_the_same_refusal`) fails on caf9097.
- **One bad scenario no longer makes the good ones look wrong (a wrong message).** A model with a scenario that had a misspelt key (`scalar: {c: 2}`) got the right line for it, "a scenario has no key 'scalar'. Did you mean 'scalars'?", and two wrong ones for a scenario that was fine: `'scenarios.good.description' must be a date (YYYY-MM-DD), not the text "fine"` and `'scenarios.good.scalars' must be a number or text, not a mapping`. Each scenario also fails the flat form of `scenarios:` (a name and its value), deeper in, and the deepest form was the one reported. Now the form under which the fewest scenarios fail is reported, so only the bad one is named. A key close to none of a scenario's (`weight:`), a comma inside a flow-style description, or a YAML merge key (`<<:`) is now named as the one unknown key, where before every scenario in the file was reported. Found building kinship-protocol's models (roadmap T1-SCENARIO-FALSE-ERRORS); the test in `src/parser/schema_errors.rs` (`one_bad_scenario_does_not_make_the_good_ones_wrong`) fails on 4077877.
- **`forge import` reads a range over part of a table's column as that part, not the column (a wrong answer).** A formula that summed some rows of a table, `=SUM(sales!A2:A3)` of 10, 20, 30 and 40, was imported as `=SUM(sales.amount)`, 100 where the workbook has 30, without a warning; and a running total down a column, `=SUM($A$2:A2)`, `=SUM($A$2:A3)`, ..., came in as `=SUM(amount)` in every row, 100, 100, 100, 100 where the workbook has 10, 30, 60, 100. A range is the column only when it runs from the table's first data row to the column's last cell; any other range is imported as its values, with a warning that says so. Found reading the importer for T1-EXAMPLE-EXCEL-ROUNDTRIP; the test in `src/excel/importer/mod.rs` (`a_range_over_part_of_a_column_is_not_the_column`) fails on b14fe77f. A wrong answer fixed, so the two weeks before the stable release start again.
- **`forge import` says when it drops a formula column, and no longer reads a third column as a formula unless it is headed `Formula`.** A sheet with rows above its header had every formula column imported as text, with no warning. A column whose first row was typed in and whose other rows held formulas came in as its values silently; it now comes in as one formula when that is possible (Added, below), and otherwise with a warning. On a sheet of scalars, any third column (a unit, a comment) was read as a Forge formula written as text, as in workbooks from before format 5.1.0.
- **SWITCH needs at least three arguments, as in Excel.** `SWITCH(1, 2)` gave 2, reading the 2 as a default with no value to match; it is an error, as its test (`test_switch_insufficient_args`) always meant. No example or validation case calls SWITCH with fewer.
- **No model holds NaN or infinity, so Forge, its workbook and the round trip agree.** YAML reads `.nan`, `.inf` and `-.inf` as numbers, and Forge took them: with `[3, .nan, 2, 1]` in a column, MEDIAN was 2.5 (the NaN counted as a value above the others), MIN, SMALL, QUARTILE and RANK gave answers and MAX, LARGE, PERCENTILE, STDEV and VAR #NUM!; `forge export` wrote the NaN as the text `NAN`, so the workbook's MEDIAN was 2 (LibreOffice), and `forge import` read that cell back as 0, so the model's was 1.5. A formula reached NaN too: `1E309` was read as infinity, `=1E309 - 1E309` gave NaN, `forge calculate` wrote `value: .nan` into the model, `forge export` wrote the formula `inf - inf` with a cached `NaN` (LibreOffice: Err:502), and `forge import` of that workbook wrote `nanres: .nan`. Now each way in is closed: a model with a number that is not finite is refused as it is read, naming the place (`'t.x row 2' is .nan, which is not a number a model can hold`), in every file and from every command, the browser and MCP included; a formula's stored `value:` is not checked, as it is calculated again, so a file an older Forge wrote with `.nan` still opens. A number past the largest double in a formula (`1E309`) is #NUM! as it is read, as Excel refuses it. Arithmetic that is not a number (infinity minus infinity) is #NUM!, as an overflow already was. A cell of a workbook that holds NaN or an infinity, which only a workbook Forge exported can, is imported as Excel's #NUM! (as an error cell, named in the warnings), never as `.nan`. No example, validation case or Studio model holds one.

### Changed
- **The `jsonschema` crate checks every model again, instead of a reader of Forge's own (ADR-072).** ADR-069 had replaced it on 2026-11-20 mostly for its size in the browser. `jsonschema` is the implementation the official JSON Schema Test Suite is run against (Bowtie), with years of use outside Forge; the reader was a day old and only checked against it. Nothing changes in which models are accepted or in what is said: the messages are still Forge's own (`schema_errors.rs`), and while both were in place, every model a test read and 78,838 generated ones got the same lines from both. Forge Studio's download grows by 682 KB gzipped (1,355,065 to 2,037,051 bytes before wasm-bindgen), under ADR-070's 3 MB ceiling: what it buys is a standard validator in place of 2,000 lines of our own. The reader's own tests go with it, so the test count in `README.md` and `VALIDATION.md` is "over 2,700" (it was 2,750). `getrandom` is now allowed in two versions (0.3 through `jsonschema`'s `ahash`, 0.4 for Forge), and `r-efi` with it (5 and 6, for UEFI firmware only), the maintainer's decision that the rule against duplicate crates does not compete with keeping a core library (`clippy.toml`, CLAUDE.md).
- **The coverage floors in `ci.yml` rise to 98.2% lines, 96.8% functions and 97.9% regions (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** From CI's run on main after #326, which measured 98.21%, 96.88% and 97.91% (they were 97.1, 94.8 and 96.3), rounded down to a tenth as before. The Coverage job fails below them.
- **More of the evaluator is covered by tests that assert a result (T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Its functions are checked argument by argument: an error in any argument is passed on as it is, and text where a number is needed is refused with that argument's message (`each_argument`, a helper of the evaluator's tests), for the financial, trigonometric, rounding, logarithm, normal-distribution, statistical, schedule, date, lookup, text and Forge functions. New tests reach what none did: NORM.S.DIST near 0, where it sums a series (to 1e-15 of mpmath's values); IRR finding the root nearest its guess when Newton's method has no NPV to start from; ACCRINT with coupons before the issue date and at the ends of the calendar; text criteria such as `">b"` in COUNTIF, which compare text with text ignoring case (LibreOffice counts the same); WEEKNUM for weeks starting on each day; DATEDIF's anniversaries, from 29 February as Excel takes it; DDB with a factor of at least the life; VALUE's signs, exponents and times; SWITCH comparing arrays; the evaluator's own errors (an index into what is not an array, a call of what is not a LAMBDA, a row past the end of a column). Reference values are from LibreOffice Calc 24.2 where it agrees with Excel. Forty tests that read a column with `if let` or `match` passed or panicked whatever the column held; they now fail unless it holds what they check (`ColumnValue::texts`, beside `numbers`). A test that accepted MEDIAN of an empty column either failing or giving 0 asserts that it fails, and 43 tests that asserted nothing do: 36 ended in `let _ = calculator.calculate_all();` and seven in `assert!(result.is_ok() || result.is_err())`. Each now asserts what its formula gives, checked by hand; the 17 that put a text formula (LEFT, MID, UPPER, SUBSTITUTE and the like) in a scalar, which holds a number, assert that the scalar refuses it and what the function gives, and one that calculated no formula at all concatenates its two columns. Code no test could reach is gone: IRR read its first argument twice (it reads the value it has); the bisection's check for an NPV that is not a number (both ends of a bracket have one, and so does every rate between them); ACCRINT's second build of a coupon it had built; `with_day(1)`'s failure; the normal distribution's check for a z that is not a number (it gives one that is not either) and the error of a standard normal distribution that cannot fail to exist; PERCENTILE's and QUARTILE's own answer for one value (the interpolation gives it); VALUE's failure to read the text it wrote itself; INDIRECT's check that a split at a dot has two parts. PRICEDISC, YIELDDISC and ACCRINT moved to `financial/securities.rs`, so `financial.rs` stays under 1,500 lines.
- **Forge Studio's download has a stated purpose and a ceiling of 3 MB gzipped (ADR-070).** CI failed above 1,362,000 bytes, a number with no reason written down, and it pushed code toward replacing libraries for size alone (ADR-069's schema reader in part, and a hand-written scan instead of `regex` in #323). The target is now Studio usable within 3 seconds on a 10 Mbps connection on a first visit (conference or hotel Wi-Fi in the cities Studio is shown in), which allows 3,000,000 bytes gzipped; the module is about 1.29 MB. Growth over 100 KB in one pull request is said in its description and here, and size alone is no reason to replace a library with code of our own (CLAUDE.md). Two roadmap items follow: deciding again between Forge's schema reader and `jsonschema` (T1-REVISIT-VALIDATOR), and importing a sheet of scalars headed in another language (T1-IMPORT-OTHER-LANGUAGES).
- **`examples/README.md` says Studio opens the games.** It said Studio opened every example but `games/`, which has been wrong since rc.31.

### Added
- **`forge import` reads a workbook it did not write (roadmap T1-EXAMPLE-EXCEL-ROUNDTRIP).** Titles above a table, headings merged over its columns, a blank row inside it and a total row under it are taken out of the table, and every formula that reads the sheet is still translated by the rows it names; the total row's cells come in as scalars (`monthly_total.revenue: =SUM(monthly.revenue)`), and `=Monthly!D17` elsewhere reads `monthly_total.revenue`. A sheet of labels and values, with `Value` or nothing over the values, is a sheet of scalars named from the labels (`Opening volume` is `assumptions.opening_volume`), with its `Unit` and `Notes` or `Comment` columns kept as `unit:` and `notes:`. Headers that are not names become names (`Volume (kg)` is `volume_kg`). A named range is read as the cell or range it names. A column whose formula changes once (from July) or has a number typed over it in a row or three comes in as one formula that picks by row, `=IF(ROW() <= 6, a, b)` or `=IF(ROW() = 10, 21000, a)`, with a warning that names the rows, instead of as values. The heading over the import's warnings says "What the import changed or could not keep" (it said every one was a formula imported as values). docs/FEATURES.md lists the rules and what is not read.
- **A budget inherited as a workbook (`examples/inherited-budget`).** Kestrel Coffee Roasters' FY2027 budget: three sheets laid out by hand (titles, merged headings, a blank row between the half-years, a total row, a named range, number formats, units and comments, and October's staff cost typed in), written with openpyxl by `make_workbook.py` and calculated by LibreOffice, never by Forge. `forge import` reads it and calculates to every number LibreOffice stored, the months, the total row and the summary (asserted in `excel_io/mod.rs`, `inherited_budget`); it names three things: the price rise from July and October's typed staff cost, kept as formulas that pick by row, and H2's revenue over H1's, a sum over part of a column, imported as its value. `forge audit` follows net income down to October's 21,000. With a pay rise to 22,500 a month and both put back as formulas (`SUMIFS` by month for H2 over H1), net income is 642,935.46; left typed in, October would have overstated it by 1,125. `check_export.py` exports the model and has LibreOffice recalculate every formula on load: 120 numbers, 103 formulas, 0 different. The README says what import does not read.
- **The takeover's rival bidder, as a game (`examples/acquisition/deal_game.yaml`, `forge game`).** Quillon's board asks for sealed best and final offers, and Valdane and Thornvale each bid a 30% or a 40% premium. Each payoff is the bidder's value created from `deal.yaml` (a point of premium costs 12.24, so Valdane's 224.51 at 30% is 102.11 at 40%); Thornvale's synergies are a round 530 against Valdane's 603.15. Both bidding 30% and both bidding 40% are equilibria, and both expect 61.2 less at 40%: the knockout bid is a trap, not a way out. With cost cuts worth 400 (`thornvale_cost_only`), both bid 30%. Worked out by hand and with nashpy 0.0.43 and asserted in `game.rs`; `validation/games` checks the file against Gambit (25 named games, from 23); Studio's acquisition set opens it, and `studio/tests/games.mjs` checks it. Where `deal_bid.yaml` types in Thornvale's chances, this works its choice out.
- **The bagel README shows `forge audit`.** The year's profit, 786.32, traced to its 15 inputs (9 in `bagel.yaml`, 6 in `costs.yaml`) and two data columns, asserted in `audit.rs`. No story example's README showed the command before.

## [10.0.0-rc.31] - 2026-11-20

Games in Forge Studio. A model with a `games:` section shows each game on the Model view, worked out again with every input and scenario: a payoff table with its equilibria marked, a tournament's ranking and matches, and each game's results under the names a formula reads them by. Studio has a Games set with the seven `examples/games` models, and the bagel has a price war. No wrong answer is fixed in this release (the Bayesian network's last digit is not one: docs/STABILITY.md promises no numbers to the last digit), so the two weeks still run from rc.30: the stable release is no earlier than 2026-12-04.

### Fixed
- **The list of scenarios in "Scenario 'x' not found" is in name order.** It listed them in the order of a hash table, so the same mistake gave a different message from one run to the next (`Available: ["big", "zero", "bad", "later", "base"]`). `forge compare` and the other commands that apply a scenario now list them sorted. `comparison_json` no longer adds a `null` row for a date variable that has no values. Tests in `compare.rs` fail on the commit before (the order, with five scenarios, matched by chance once in 120 runs).
- **A Bayesian network gives the same probabilities, to the last digit, on every run.** Its nodes were kept in a map whose order is random from one run to the next, and the factors of the inference were multiplied in that order, so the last digit could move: `forge bayesian examples/bagel/slow_week.yaml -q roadwork -e sales=slow -e weather=rainy` gave P(no road work) as 0.7272727272727272 on some runs and 0.7272727272727273 on others (both 8/11 to within one unit in the last place). The nodes and each conditional probability table are now kept in name order, so the answer is the same on every run and whatever order the nodes are written in the file, and the order in which nodes are listed, and which of several errors in a network is reported, no longer change from run to run either. A test builds the slow-week network 200 times, in each of the six orders of its nodes, and requires the same bits each time; it fails on 4fe05ea.
- **The `cargo binstall` install checks ask GitHub with the job's token.** The macOS check of rc.30 took 18 minutes: every request cargo-binstall made to GitHub's API to find the release's archive got 403 Forbidden, the limit of 60 requests an hour for a caller without a token, which GitHub's macOS runners share and had used up. cargo-binstall waited 120 seconds for each of the four macOS archive names it tries, twice, so the first of its five tries failed after 16 minutes and the second passed after a 91-second wait; under the new 20-minute limit (Changed, below), one more wait would have failed the check. The checks now pass `GITHUB_TOKEN`, which raises the limit and changes nothing that is installed. A user's own computer has its own 60 an hour.
- **The README counts six prompts in `docs/PROMPTS.md`, and names the games one.** #316 added a sixth prompt (games); the README still said "Five more prompts" and listed five. It now says six and lists games.

### Changed
- **One version of each crate but syn (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** `multiple_crate_versions` is no longer allowed in `Cargo.toml` or `forge-wasm/Cargo.toml`. statrs is used without its default features, so nalgebra and its four versions of glam are out of the tree (99 lines fewer in `Cargo.lock`). petgraph, which pulled a second hashbrown and foldhash, is replaced by `src/core/array_calculator/graph.rs`: the same topological sort and strongly connected components, visiting nodes in the same order, checked against petgraph on 5,000 random graphs, so the calculation order, which error is reported first and every number are unchanged. syn 2 and 3 are both still in the tree, because tracing-attributes, zeroize_derive and windows-implement have no syn-3 release; `clippy.toml` allows syn alone (`allowed-duplicate-crates`, counted in `validation/repo`), so any other duplicate fails clippy.
- **The install checks after a release stop after 15 and 20 minutes.** On 2026-11-20 the macOS `cargo binstall` check of rc.30 was still on its install step 25 minutes after the other five checks had passed (the step takes seconds elsewhere and retries five times at most 150 seconds each), and a job with no limit runs six hours before GitHub stops it. The Homebrew checks now fail after 15 minutes and the binstall checks after 20, each with its log.

### Added
- **The calculator's core is asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `core/array_calculator/mod.rs`, `recursive.rs`, `order.rs` and the new `formats.rs` (the check of a formula's `format:` against its value, moved out of `mod.rs`, which was over 1,500 lines) are at 100% of lines and regions in a full run. New tests give each way a calculation stops its exact message: a later row that fails or is not a number (in a column of text, of true/false or of numbers), a formula that does not parse, a table with formulas and no rows, a `format:` of the wrong kind for its value (a scalar, a column, a column of dates, a format the parser would refuse, in `calculate_all` and in the outputs of a simulation), a cycle, a name that fits two scalars, a scalar formula that gives a column, and, in a group of columns that read each other through `PREV`, a failing row, a syntax error, an empty table, a read of the whole column, an ambiguous name and a scalar named like a column. Code no input could reach is gone: `cell_dependencies`, `extract_scalar_dependencies` and `dependency_graphs` cannot fail and no longer return a `Result` (which also removes a `clippy::unnecessary_wraps` allow, so the budget for lint allows in `validation/repo` falls from 37 to 36), the steps of a calculation hold their plan and context as stated invariants where an `if let` had an `else` that never ran, a name laid over the context twice is put back correctly without a set that tracked it, and the cycle check of the grouped steps (they cannot have one). The timing test of 16,000 rows of `INDEX`/`MATCH` and `SUMIF` ran only in a release build, so the coverage run never reached it; it runs in every build now with the same limits (10 and 24 times a plain search of the column; a debug build took 0.9 and 3.5 times, release 3.4 and 11.8). The test count in `README.md` and `VALIDATION.md` is "over 2,650".
- **The bagel example has a price war (`examples/bagel/price_war.yaml`, `forge game`).** Bagel Lune and the shop across the street each hold $16 a dozen or cut to $14 every month. The payoffs are each shop's monthly margin before rent and staff, read from the plan (`bagel.yaml`, `costs.yaml`): 21,489 each if both hold, 25,585 and 12,894 if one cuts, 19,189 each if both cut. Played once, cutting dominates and both lose 2,300 a month: a prisoner's dilemma the plan's own numbers make. Played month after month with a 0.95 chance that both shops are still open, tit for tat and grim trigger cannot be beaten by a discounter (the threshold is 0.640); with loyal regulars (a cheaper shop takes 55%, not 70%) holding dominates, and with the rival's lease ending (0.6) only the discounter is stable. Every number is checked by hand, with nashpy 0.0.43 and with the axelrod library 4.14.0 (4,000 seeded matches of each pair, within 1.19 standard errors) and asserted in a `bagel_example` test.
- **Forge Studio shows a model's games (ADR-068).** A model with a `games:` section shows each game on the Model view: a payoff table with the pure equilibria marked, the equilibria with their chances, the strategies removed as dominated, and a tournament's ranking, collectively stable players and the match of every pair. They are worked out again with every input changed and every scenario tapped, so dragging the share a cheaper shop takes in the bagel's price war, or tapping `loyal_regulars`, moves the equilibrium from (cut, cut) to (hold, hold). The bagel set has `price_war.yaml` (open it from Files), and the Tools tab has `forge_game`. To draw the table from Forge's numbers alone, `forge game -o`, `forge_game` and forge-wasm's `game` now report every cell's payoffs (`cells`), and forge-wasm's `model` names the games. `studio/tests/games.mjs` checks every number on screen against the `game` tool in the plan and both scenarios, and runs in CI.
- **Forge Studio has a Games set.** The seven models of `examples/games` open from the welcome list: the stag hunt (first), chicken, Axelrod's tournament, the inspection game, a tournament with mistakes, the battle of the sexes and a tournament that goes on with a chance after each round. `studio/tests/games.mjs` opens each from Files and checks every game in it against the `game` tool, every equilibrium with its chances included, and that the stag hunt's scenario `c3` and a cost typed on its card move its mixed equilibrium to (3 - c) / 4 stag, worked out by hand. Every test that runs on all the sets (the numbers on screen, the grid, formulas and elements changed on their cards, suggestions, keys, scenarios) runs on it, and forge-wasm calculates the stag hunt natively and in the browser to the same numbers (`tests/cases.json`). Running those tests on it found that Studio left out a game's results: `forge calculate` gives them as scalars a formula reads (`stag_hunt.even_mix.me.stag`, `stag_hunt.equilibrium_3.me.stag`), and the page neither showed them nor offered them while a formula was typed. Each game's card now lists its results by name with Forge's values, and the suggestions offer them, from Forge's `calculate` answer (a third equilibrium is named only when there is one). Where a game's results crowd a name out of the 16 offered for its first three letters, the suggestion tests require it once more is typed.
- **`forge tornado` and `forge real-options` are asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `cli/commands/prediction/tornado.rs` and `real_options.rs` are at 100% of lines and regions in a run of the prediction tests, with tests for each refusal and its exact text (a missing file, text that is not YAML, no section, an unknown key, no inputs or options, an input range the wrong way round, an unknown output or option, a volatility out of range, an output that does not calculate), `--verbose`, `--compare-npv`, `--option`, `--output-var`, a bar of an input that does not move the output, an option with no rule of thumb, and `-o` to a file and to a folder that is not there. The prediction commands that write `-o` share `write_result` (it serialises, writes and says so). Code no input could reach is gone: `RealOptionsEngine::analyze` could not fail and no longer returns a `Result`, the second YAML read of a model that was just parsed cannot fail, and a result with no `probability_exercise` (it is always empty, so `forge real-options` never printed it) has no line for it. The field stays in the JSON.
- **`forge compare` is asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `cli/commands/compare.rs` is at 100% of lines and regions in a run of its own tests: the table of a model with a date, a description, a `format:` and a verbose line (with every character of the output), the CSV and the JSON of a date, a scenario with no value (`—`), each way the command stops (a missing file, an unknown scenario, an override of a name the model does not have, a calculation that fails), and the terminal's width: `COLUMNS`, then the terminal's own (read from a pseudo-terminal opened by the test), then none. `terminal_width` takes what it reads as parameters, and `window_columns_of` the descriptor, so a test gives them. The comparison's lookup of a description no longer has a branch that could not happen.

## [10.0.0-rc.30] - 2026-11-20

Games. A `games:` section (format 5.5.0) finds each game's dominated strategies, pure and mixed equilibria and even-mix payoffs, and a `tournament:` plays repeated games between built-in strategies with exact expected values; `forge game`, `forge_game` (the 21st MCP tool) and forge-wasm read them, and seven examples ship in `examples/games`. The games are compared with Gambit, the axelrod library and R in CI, and no disagreement was found. Wrong answers fixed (`MAX` and `MIN` of dates, `XNPV` with a date before the first, `ROUND` at -309 and below, `HOUR`, `MINUTE` and `SECOND` past 9999-12-31, and more under Fixed), so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-12-04. Forge Studio downloads 679 KB less.

### Changed
- **The coverage floors in CI rise to 97.1% lines, 94.8% functions and 96.3% regions (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** From 95.6, 92.6 and 94.6: CI's run on main after the tests of the Excel exporter and the formula translator measured 97.15%, 94.86% and 96.39%. Floors are set from CI's number, not a local run; they go on rising to 100. The test count in `README.md` and `VALIDATION.md` is "over 2,600", which the last merges passed (the repository check that compares them with the real count failed on main until now).
- A tornado bar that does not move said "the output's formula does not use it", which was not always so (a game's equilibrium reads every payoff and can stay put; `=MAX(x, 100)` reads x). It now says "the output does not use it, or does not move over this range".

### Added
- **The games are checked against Gambit, axelrod and R in CI (roadmap T2-GAME-THEORY-E2E, ADR-068).** `validation/games` runs `forge game -o` on 23 named games (kinship-protocol's three at four costs each, the inspection game at three penalties, battle of the sexes, matching pennies, rock-paper-scissors, a game solved by three steps of dominance, and degenerate ones) and on the examples that ship, and on seeded random games from 2 x 2 to 6 x 6 with ties on purpose, and compares the equilibria with Gambit (pygambit 16.7.0); the match values of every pair of the nine strategies in three settings with the axelrod library (4.14.0), as averages of seeded matches within 4.5 standard errors; and the closed forms of 2 x 2 games with R. A disagreement fails the run unless Forge reports the game as degenerate, and a degenerate report has to be proved by the suite, so it cannot excuse a disagreement it does not describe. The `Games vs Gambit, axelrod and R` job in `validation.yml` runs it on every pull request that changes `src/` or `validation/`, and weekly with a new seed. No disagreement was found, so Forge did not change.
- **`forge sensitivity` is asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `cli/commands/analysis/sensitivity.rs` has tests for each refusal with its exact text (a missing file, an unknown or ambiguous name, a date as the second input, each malformed range, in the core, the printed table and the JSON), an output the model does not have, and the verbose lines of both tables; the tests that took a result apart with `let ... else panic!` now read its JSON. The table and the JSON find the model's names through one function, and the matrix's cells through another, so the checks they repeated (a date input, the second input's name) are made once. Code no input could reach is gone: an error for JSON that cannot fail to write, and one for setting an input whose name was just found.
- **Games in the model format (roadmap T2-GAME-THEORY, ADR-068, format 5.5.0).** A `games:` section holds games played once: players, their strategies, and a payoff table whose cells are numbers or formulas over the model (`hare: {stag: =3 - c, hare: =3 - c}`). Forge works out each game's strictly dominated strategies, its pure equilibria (any number of players), its mixed equilibria (two players, by support enumeration), and each strategy's payoff against an even mix, and stores them as scalars named under the game (`inspection.equilibrium_1.human.audit`), which formulas can read and `scenarios`, `compare`, `tornado`, `sensitivity`, `goal-seek` and `simulate` can use as outputs. kinship-protocol's stag-hunt flags become `=IF(stag_hunt.even_mix.me.stag > stag_hunt.even_mix.me.hare, 1, 0)`. A cell that cannot be used names itself (`me's payoff in games.stag_hunt.payoffs.hare.stag`); a game that reads its own results is a cycle; a result a game does not have says how many equilibria it has; a degenerate game says so. Every equilibrium in the tests is checked with nashpy 0.0.43 or by hand. `forge schema v5.5` and `forge upgrade --to 5.5.0` know the format; files of the earlier formats read as before. `forge export` warns and names the games (`games: not exported (stag_hunt)`): Excel cannot find an equilibrium, so the payoff formulas are not written and the results are numbers as of the export; before this check it wrote them without a word, and a changed input in Excel moved nothing. `forge game model.yaml [--game NAME] [--scenario NAME] [-o FILE]` prints each game's dominated strategies, equilibria, ties and even-mix payoffs, and writes them as YAML or JSON.
- **Tournaments of repeated play (roadmap T2-GAME-THEORY, ADR-068).** A game in `games:` can be a `tournament:` that plays a symmetric game of two strategies round after round between built-in strategies (`always_cooperate`, `always_defect`, `tit_for_tat`, `suspicious_tit_for_tat`, `tit_for_two_tats`, `grim_trigger`, `win_stay_lose_shift`, `generous_tit_for_tat` with `forgive`, `random` with `cooperate`), each against every other and, with `self_play`, against itself. A match lasts `rounds`, or goes on after each round with the chance `continuation`; `noise` flips each move. Each match is a joint Markov chain of at most nine states, stepped or solved, so the results are exact expected values with no seed, and a tornado or a sweep over `noise` or `continuation` is smooth; any of these numbers, and a strategy's parameter, can be a formula. Results are scalars: `<t>.score.<p>`, `.rank.<p>`, `.cooperation.<p>`, `.match.<a>.<b>` and `.stable.<p>` (collectively stable: no player does better against it than it does against itself). `forge game` prints the ranking, the match table and the stable players. kinship-protocol's tournament gives grim 2.596, tft 2.44825, ad 2.208, ac 2.1, random 1.90875 (it typed in 2.622, 2.445, 2.212, 2.112 and 1.9, half of a table that cannot be right); AlwaysCooperate overtakes AlwaysDefect at a cost of 0.108, not 0.1. The nine strategies are checked against the Python axelrod library 4.14.0: every pair, with noise, over fixed rounds and with a continuation, is within 2.4 standard errors of its average over seeded runs. With noise 0.05, TitForTwoTats wins and TitForTat falls from first to third.
- **`forge_game`, the 21st MCP tool, and `game` in forge-wasm.** The same arguments as `forge game` (`game`, `scenario`), each report marked `kind: table` or `kind: tournament`.
- **Seven game examples (`examples/games`, `forge examples`):** kinship-protocol's stag hunt, chicken and Axelrod tournament, with the flags it wrote by hand now read from the games and its stored results reproduced (the tournament's within 0.026, explained); the inspection game, where a tornado shows a harsher penalty lowering the audit rate and not the cheating; a tournament with noise; the battle of the sexes; and a sweep of the continuation at which GrimTrigger becomes stable, (T - c - R) / (T - P). Each number is asserted in an inline test against nashpy, axelrod or a hand calculation.
- **The table, scalar and array readers are asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `parser/variables.rs` and `parser/arrays.rs` have tests for each refusal with its exact text: a period column that is not one, a rich column whose values or `format:` do not fit, a column that is neither a list nor a formula, a scalar whose `value:`, `formula:`, `format:` or keys are wrong, a date scalar with a unit or a number format, each rule a date breaks, and an empty cell in a column that is not numbers. The tests that matched a column on `match ... _ => panic!` now compare the whole `ColumnValue`. No input could reach the "unsupported array type" error, the "invalid number format" error or the second parse of a checked date, so they are gone.
- **The date rewriter, the in-place YAML writer and the parser's schema errors are asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `excel/workbook_refs/dates/rewrite.rs` (what a date in an exported formula becomes, and each reason an export refuses one), `writer/in_place.rs` (what `calculate` and `upgrade` write into a file you typed, and the checks that read it back) and `parser/schema_errors.rs`, `schema.rs` and `model.rs` (what is said about a model that does not fit its format) each have inline tests that assert the exact formula, text or reason, and are at 100% of lines and regions in a run of the tests that use them. The checks that read an edited file back moved to `writer/in_place/verify.rs`, which keeps both files under 1,500 lines. Code that no input could reach is gone: the check that a converted file has as many scenarios as the original (its documents are already equal by then), the wording for a text reference read as a number that could only be a call or an `&`, and the errors for a schema that does not load, which now take the schema's text as an argument so a test can give a broken one.
- **The Excel importer is split by what it does and asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `src/excel/importer.rs` was 1,479 lines with no room for its tests; it is now `importer/mod.rs` (the workbook's layout and the public API), `sheet.rs` (a sheet's cells, formulas and number formats), `scalars.rs` (the Name / Value sheets) and `table.rs` (table sheets). New tests build workbooks with `rust_xlsxwriter` for each case the importer handles: a column of one number format, of two, or of a format for text; a date format on a number; a header that is a number; a sheet with only a header; a column without a header, and an empty one; text that starts with a date; numbers shown as dates by formulas that differ, and a column whose later rows have no formula (both imported as values, with the reason); numbers beside longer formulas; a boolean scalar; an older workbook's formula as text; a formula Forge cannot read; a date scalar in the model and in an included file; a sheet named like an included file's that is not one; and the date columns of an included table. All four files are at 100% of lines, functions and regions. Code that could not fail no longer has a branch for it (`convert_to_column_value` gives a column of empty cells for an empty column rather than an error no caller could get); `cells_of` and `imported_format` take an iterator object and a slice, so the coverage run counts one copy of each and not one per caller.
- **`forge audit` is asserted in full (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** The command that prints the audit trail had no test of its own; 19 tests now cover what it prints (the formula, the calculated value or date, a stored value that matches or is stale, a constant formula, a repeated branch, a column's rows and empty cells), the NPV and IRR warnings about empty cells, a model that does not calculate, names that resolve nowhere (an unknown include, a missing column of an included table), the depth limit of 30, and the error that lists what the model has. `Scope::column_values` indexes the table it is given, which the caller has checked.
- **`forge import` is tested in every mode (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** Tests cover its verbose, split, multi-document and text forms, each place it cannot write, each kind of value it writes (booleans, empty cells, dates, formats), and when an included file already there holds other values. Two small changes came with them. A workbook with a scalar and a scalar group of the same name (`a` and `a.x` on the Scalars sheet) is refused with a message ("'a' is both a scalar and a scalar group"); before, `a.x` was dropped without a word. And the check that a table and a scalar group do not share a name runs once, when the workbook is read, so every form of the import gives the same answer.
- **More of `calculate`, `validate`, `variance` and `Rounded` is asserted (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** New inline tests cover what `calculate` and `validate` print for an empty cell, a unit warning, a scenario, a batch and a result already stored; the terminal, Excel (read back with calamine) and YAML reports of `forge variance`, the lines it does not compare and each status word; and every kind of value that `Rounded` passes through to a serializer. `print_table_results` no longer has a branch for a column that is missing after a calculation, which cannot happen.
- **No test is ignored any more (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `round_trip_corpus` needed an environment variable and was skipped everywhere; it is now `round_trip_examples`, which exports and imports every model in `examples/` and asserts the numbers come back (`monte-carlo.yaml`, whose formulas are draws, is asserted to be refused as it is). The 5,000-row `SUMIFS` test was skipped in debug builds; it now runs in every build and asserts its result (247,500), and holds the one-second time of ADR-058 in a release build only, as the 16,000-row tests do. The budget for ignored tests in `validation/repo` falls from 2 to 0.
- **`forge upgrade`, `forge watch` and the API server are tested with nothing compiled out (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** `watch` and the server's shutdown were stubbed out of the coverage run, which the rules forbid; both stubs, and the `cfg_attr(coverage, allow(...))` that went with them, are gone, so the budgets for coverage-only code and for lint `allow`s in `validation/repo` fall to 0 and 37. Watch mode is now `src/cli/commands/watch.rs`: what starts the watcher is a parameter, so tests feed it events (a change to the model, a stray `.txt`, a watcher error, the end of the stream) and read what it prints, and one test watches a real directory. The server takes its shutdown future as a parameter: a test serves on a free port, answers `/health`, and stops it; another sends the process SIGINT and SIGTERM. `forge upgrade` (16 tests: the 5.0.0 split, each short-form step, includes and their loops, a failing backup, a dry run) writes the backup and the file through one function, so a backup that cannot be made says "Failed to back up and write" and leaves the file alone. The three files are at 100% of lines, functions and regions (watch.rs one region short); the CI floors rise to 95.1 / 91.8 / 94.9 (lines, functions, regions), from 93.1 / 89.2 / 91.9. Two small behaviour changes: watch mode's relevance check treats any `.yaml`/`.yml` event path alike, and `forge watch` on a file it cannot read says "File not found".
- **`forge update` is tested, with no coverage exception (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** The command was compiled out of the coverage run (a stub stood in for it, citing ADR-006), which the rules forbid. It now takes the programs it runs (`curl`, `tar`), the platform, the binary it replaces and its download directory as arguments, and 36 inline tests drive it with a shell script standing in for `curl`: a newer release installed with the old binary kept as `.bak`, an answer of no, `--check`, a release with no checksums, a checksum mismatch, a failing or missing `tar`, an archive without the binary, and each error the update can report. The result: `cli/commands/update.rs` is at 100% of lines, functions and regions, and the budget for code compiled only for or without coverage in `validation/repo` falls from 5 to 3. Behaviour changes in two small ways: the new binary is written with mode 755 where it is created (it was copied, then its mode set), and a system that cannot name the running binary is refused before anything is downloaded.
- **MCP and the REST API give the examples' numbers (roadmap T1-INTERFACE-PARITY).** The headline numbers of `examples/bagel` were asserted through the command line only; an agent building a model through MCP, a main use, had only small inline models and the one-command files. Every one of the 20 MCP tools now has an inline test that runs a bagel model by file path and as inline content (`content` and `includes`), and asserts the numbers `bagel_example` asserts: the year's profit 786.3203617, the flour shock -16943.271075 (a scenario that overrides a value in the included `costs.yaml`), the tornado's ten outputs, break-even at 15.9666 and 24.2687 hours for 60,000, the weighted expected value 621.641286, the variance of costs judged as costs, 18.3541 a dozen for an 80% chance of profit, the simulation's threshold probabilities and rank correlations, the pop-up's 61,500, the options' 64604.6835 and 279.7243, the bootstrap against numpy and the 3/11 chance of roadwork. The examples built since are in too: the takeover's 224.5079 (`calculate`), its weighted 139.4853 (`scenarios`) and the free cash flow of the included target at which it creates nothing, 51.40 (`break-even` on an `@target...` input), and the vineyard's 10,000 correlated seasons (`simulate`). The three discovery tools run no model, so they are checked against the models that use them (the schema the tool serves accepts bagel, the function list has the functions it calls, the starter example it serves calculates). Each of the five REST endpoints has an in-process test through `build_router` on bagel (`bagel_over_http` in `src/api/server.rs`), and `docs/FEATURES.md` has a request and a response for each. One difference between the doors was found and fixed (below).
- **A scenario without a probability sits beside the weighted ones (roadmap T1-UNWEIGHTED-SCENARIOS, ADR-067).** `forge scenarios` refused a file if any scenario had no probability, though `forge compare`, `calculate --scenario` and `simulate --scenario` use such a scenario, so a deal's payment structures (choices, not chances) could not sit beside its risk cases. It now weighs the scenarios that have a probability, leaves out the rest and names them: a line in its output with the `forge compare` command that shows them, and `unweighted` in its JSON, its `-o` file, the MCP tool and Studio. The probabilities it weighs must still sum to 1, and the error names the scenarios left out, so a forgotten probability shows itself; with none to weigh, or `--scenario` naming one left out, it says so and points to `compare`. Studio's Analyze tab gives Forge's reason when it cannot weigh a model's scenarios, where it said the model had none. `examples/acquisition` compares paying all in cash, all in shares and 60/40: the same 224.51 created, and year 3 accretive (+1.3%) only in cash, with leverage at 2.60, past the agencies' 2.5. Checked against the example's Python.
- **`forge tornado`, `goal-seek`, `break-even` and `sensitivity` vary an input of an included file (roadmap T1-VARY-INCLUDED).** Found building `examples/acquisition`: `--vary @target.company.free_cash_flow_next_year` was "not found" in all four, though a scenario overrides the same name and `forge simulate` draws one. The name is written as a scenario writes it, `@alias.name`, in full or bare inside the included file, and through a file that file includes; the included file is calculated with each value, and a date there is refused as one in the model is. An `_includes` entry that is not there is an error that lists the model's entries. In the acquisition example, Quillon's free cash flow can fall to 51.40 a year from 62 before the deal creates nothing. Tests beside each command check every value against the plain model with that input.
- **`examples/acquisition`: a takeover, from the board's model to year one (roadmap T1-MA-EXAMPLE).** Project Kittiwake (fictional): Valdane Instruments bids 31.20 a share for Quillon Sensors, a 30% premium, 60% in borrowed cash and 40% in new shares; Quillon and Valdane are included files. `deal.yaml` gives the value the deal creates (224.51, the synergies covering the premium 1.68 times, 91% of their worth after year 5), earnings per share year by year (12.0% dilutive in year 1, accretive from year 4, neutral in year 1 as the press release counts it), four weighted scenarios, a tornado in which the discount rate and terminal growth move the value more than any synergy, the walk-away price (35.60), the synergies or the cash needed for earnings a share to hold in year 3, and the banker's table of premium against synergies. `deal_risk.yaml` simulates 10,000 futures in which every synergy leans short (mean 147.13 against 224.51 at plan), an earn-out that moves Quillon's growth risk to its sellers, and the premium that creates value in three futures of four (34.2%). A rival bidder (`decision-tree`), Quillon's next platform (`real-options`), 24 comparable premiums (`bootstrap`), a diligence red flag (`bayesian`) and the deal a year after closing (`variance`: value created halves, and most of the loss is Quillon's own growth, not the synergies) complete it. The numbers were written again in Python from the description and are asserted beside each command (`acquisition_example`). Forge Studio opens it as an example set, and forge-wasm compares its calculation with the native one. What it made hard is in the roadmap: T1-VARY-INCLUDED, T1-SEEK-UNUSED-FORMULA and T1-UNWEIGHTED-SCENARIOS.
- **`examples/vineyard`: a season when the harvest and the price move together (roadmap T1-EXAMPLE-CORRELATION).** Les Trois Pentes (fictional) sells its grapes to its region's wineries; a big regional crop sells cheap. `vineyard.yaml` is a normal year (72,140 profit, break-even at 1,593.81 a tonne) with four scenarios and a tornado; `vineyard_risk.yaml` draws the crop (PERT), the price (lognormal) and a spring frost (`MC.Discrete`), with the crop and the price correlated at -0.7; `vineyard_independent.yaml` is the same file with the correlation at 0. Over 10,000 seasons the correlation cuts the chance of a loss from 33.4% to 25.6% and the worst 5% from -111,209 to -79,829, and costs 8,766 of the mean: the vineyard never sells a big crop at a high price. It is the first example with `correlations:`. numpy and scipy checked Forge's draws (their rank correlation -0.690 against the copula's -0.683, each input's distribution at five quantiles, one frost in ten), recalculated every season from them, and ran 2,000,000 seasons of their own; Forge agrees within four standard errors, with and without the correlation and with both sampling methods. Tests in `src/monte_carlo/correlation.rs` (`vineyard_example`) and beside `scenarios`, `tornado` and `goal-seek`. Forge Studio opens it, and forge-wasm compares its calculation and its simulation with the native ones.
- **`examples/fund`: a venture fund's cash flows on their dates (roadmap T1-EXAMPLE-DATES).** Corvane Seed Fund I (fictional) invests 30,000,000 in eight companies and sells them years later, on dates set with `EDATE`; it calls its fee each 31 March (`EOMONTH`) from a fee table read with `XLOOKUP`, and the managers take 20% carry from the last exit. The investors' ledger gives the return on the companies (15.68% by `XIRR`), the investors' own after fees and carry (10.83%), and their flows' value at 12% (`XNPV`, -1,599,342.07). Four scenarios (a year's delay costs 1.8 points and 3.0 million), a tornado, and a goal seek: exits 1.2324 times the plan give the investors 15%. Python with scipy, written from the README, agrees on every return to 1e-9 and every value to the cent; LibreOffice recalculates the exported workbook to Forge's digits. Gnumeric 1.12 has no `XLOOKUP` or `LET`, so the 26 dated flows are typed into a new validation case, `edge_fund_cash_flows.yaml` (5 cases, 2,156 in all), which it judges. Building it found two items for the roadmap: `MAX` of a date column is a number on which a date format is refused (T1-DATE-OF-MAX), and Microsoft documents `#NUM!` for a date before the first in `XNPV` and `XIRR`, where Forge and LibreOffice calculate (T1-EXCEL-XNPV-ORDER). Forge Studio opens it, and forge-wasm compares its calculation with the native one.

### Changed
- **Forge Studio downloads 679 KB less: Forge reads its model schemas itself (roadmap T1-ONE-VALIDATOR, ADR-069).** The `jsonschema` crate was 684 KB of the browser module (measured on 2026-11-19, 1,887,038 bytes gzipped), and it could not be left out: without it, misspelt keys, a table column that is not a list, a date where none can be and a misspelt scenario probability would pass in the browser and fail on the command line. A small reader of the 18 JSON Schema keywords the seven schema files use (`src/parser/schema_reader.rs`, checking in `schema_check.rs`) now checks every model on the command line, in the MCP server, in the REST API and in the browser, reading the same files in `schema/`. The patterns those files use are matched by a matcher of the reader's own (`src/parser/schema_pattern.rs`), because the `regex` crate alone was 277 KB of the module and nothing else in the browser calls it. The module is 1,261,203 bytes gzipped, against 1,939,940 on main with the games, 678,737 bytes (35%) less; the size budget in CI falls from 2,000,000 to 1,362,000. Models are accepted and refused as before, and with the same lines, word for word and in the same order, except four kinds of line that were `jsonschema`'s own words and are now Forge's. That was checked on 78,838 models, 44,473 of them refused by main: every example as each of the seven formats, and bad models made from each one. Every model a test reads is also checked by `jsonschema`, now only a dev-dependency, and the two must agree. The changed lines are `'case' must be a date (YYYY-MM-DD), not the text "text"` (was `value does not match "^[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}$"`, 3,241 lines), `'cash.year.start' must be a month (YYYY-MM) or a day (YYYY-MM-DD)` (54), `'monte_carlo.correlations[0].variables' has more than 2 entries` (was `value has more than 2 items`, 8) and `'games.axelrod.tournament.players' needs at least 2 entries` (was `value has less than 2 properties`, 4). The browser comparison in CI gains 14 refused models, each refused with the command line's message. 53 crates leave the browser build (`jsonschema`, `referencing`, `fancy-regex`, `fluent-uri`, `ahash`, `getrandom` 0.3, `idna` and ICU among them), and with them the size settings #296 gave them.
- **Forge Studio downloads 87 KB less: the JSON Schema crates are built for size.** The browser module was 1,973,854 bytes gzipped against a budget of 2,000,000, so one dependency bump (`jsonschema` 0.61, +57 KB) was enough to fail CI. The schema check runs once for a model that is read and never in a loop, so its crates (`jsonschema`, `jsonschema-regex`, `jsonschema-value`, `referencing`, `fancy-regex`, `fluent-uri`, `fraction`) are now built with `opt-level = "z"` in the `wasm` profile, and the calculator stays at `"s"`. The module is 1,887,038 bytes gzipped, 113 KB under the budget, and as fast: simulations and seeks took 0.97 of the time and all cases 0.98, over five interleaved runs of each build (the noise between runs of one build is about 5%); every WebAssembly answer still equals the native one. The regex, Excel and zip crates at `"z"` saved 50 KB more and read 3 to 4% slower, so they are left out. Nothing changes for the command line or the MCP server.
- **The coverage floors in CI rise to 95.6% lines, 92.6% functions and 94.6% regions (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** From 95.1, 91.8 and 94.1: CI's run on main after the tests of `forge watch`, `forge upgrade` and `forge serve` measured 95.69%, 92.68% and 94.64%. Floors are set from CI's number, not a local run, which reads about a point higher on regions; they go on rising to 100.
- **The round-trip test covers every example, and the 5,000-row `SUMIFS` test holds its time in every build.** `round_trip_examples` read only the 11 models at the top of `examples/`; `round_trip_corpus` walks the subfolders too, 48 models: 39 come back from Excel with no difference, and the 9 that do not calculate on their own are named and each draws from an `MC.` distribution. The 5,000-row `SUMIFS` test checked its time in a release build only; it is now timed against a plain nested loop doing the same 25 million comparisons in the same process (about 7 times the loop in debug and release alike, limit 100 times), because a fixed limit cannot hold in debug, release and the coverage run (12 s).
- **The check that counts ways round the coverage rule finds `coverage` in any `cfg`, `cfg_attr` or `cfg!` (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** It matched only `cfg(coverage)` and `cfg(not(coverage))`, so `all(not(coverage), ...)` and `any(not(coverage), test)` were compiled out of the coverage run without being counted: that is how `forge watch` stayed out of the report. The budget for it is 0 and the code now found is none. Also tested: `BinomialTree::early_exercise_boundary` (values from an independent Python calculation, the same Cox-Ross-Rubinstein recursion) and the MCP tools' answer to a call with no model.
- **CI restores compiled dependencies instead of building them again in every job.** One push built Forge about a dozen times from scratch: test, lint, the oldest Rust, two WebAssembly builds, the release build, coverage and the two validation suites, about 25 runner-minutes of compiling. The cache had been turned off with the note "proc-macro issues". `Swatinem/rust-cache` is in those jobs now: it keys on the toolchain and `Cargo.lock`, caches the dependencies and not Forge's own crates, so coverage and clippy still compile Forge itself; and only runs on `main` save a cache, which pull requests restore. `wasm-bindgen-cli` and `cargo-audit`, which were built from source on every run, are restored from the same cache, so `cargo install` finds them and does nothing (0.07 s here, against 1.1 minutes for `wasm-bindgen-cli`). The release workflow is unchanged and still builds from nothing.
- **The Validation workflow installs R without its recommended packages: 20 packages, not 866.** The R job's install took 155 minutes on 2026-11-18, all of it apt downloading 849 MB: `r-base` brings R's recommended packages, and they bring GDAL, a speech recogniser and a JavaScript runtime, which the validators never load. It is now `r-base-core` and `r-base-dev` (the compilers the CRAN packages are built with), and the CRAN packages build four at a time; the install takes under two minutes. The Gnumeric job installs Gnumeric and LibreOffice the same way: Gnumeric is 7 packages, not 91, and the suite passes all 2,150 cases with it, LibreOffice 26.2 included. The validators load `jsonlite`, `boot`, `mvtnorm`, `mc2d`, `derivmkts`, `data.tree` and `bnlearn`, and the suite is unchanged.

### Fixed
- **The printed two-input table of `forge sensitivity` takes an input of an included file.** `--vary2 @target.company.free_cash_flow_next_year` said "Variable ... not found", though `--format json` and the one-input table took the same name (T1-VARY-INCLUDED): the printed matrix checked the name again against the model's own scalars only. That check is gone; the name is found once, as for the other inputs. The test (`the_printed_matrix_takes_an_included_input_too`) is in the same commit as the fix.
- **A value with no output in `forge sensitivity`'s one-input table shows `ERR`, and its reason is under the table.** The whole error was printed in the output column, several lines long, and the column was as wide as its longest line (190 characters for a division by zero), so the table no longer lined up.
