The cell now shows `ERR`, as the two-input matrix does, and each failed value's reason follows the table (`ERR s.x = 1.0000: ...`). The JSON is unchanged.
- **A scenario override that is not a day names its place as the other errors do.** `up: {rate: "2026-02-30"}` said "'Scenario 'up': 'rate'' is not a date", with the quotes doubled; it now says "'scenarios.up.rate' is not a date", as the schema's checks name the same place.
- **The repository checks build the binary they run, so they cannot test an old one.** `cargo test -p mollendorff-forge-repo` ran whatever `target/debug/forge` held; after a checkout of newer code without `cargo build`, `tables_for_example_files_match` failed with "expected ident at line 1 column 2", because a binary from before 29b3729 wrote YAML into `forge real-options -o results.json`, though the code was right. The checks now run `cargo build --bin forge` once before the first command (the pre-commit hook and the Makefile did so already; CI names its release binary with `FORGE_BIN`, which is unchanged). A test fails if the binary is older than any file in `src/`; the doc table check fails on the old binary and passes once it is rebuilt.
- **`forge serve` logs where it listens, and whether it asks for a token (roadmap T1-COVERAGE-GATE).** With no `RUST_LOG` it printed nothing at all, not even "Forge API Server starting on ...", the notice that no token is set, or the folder it is limited to: its default log setting named `forge`, and the program's log messages come from `mollendorff_forge`. It now logs those lines by default, and still never the token. Found by measuring coverage: the lines that build the message were never run, because the log level that would have run them was off.
- **`MAX`, `MIN`, `LARGE`, `SMALL`, `MAXIFS` and `MINIFS` of dates give a date (roadmap T1-DATE-OF-MAX, ADR-060 addendum).** `=MAX(deals.exit_date)` was the day number 47369, and `format: "d mmm yyyy"` on it was refused as "a date format, and the value is a number", though Excel shows 8 Sep 2029 for the same formula. It is a date now when every number the function chooses from is a date: `MAX(d, DATE(2026, 6, 1))`, `LARGE(t.d, 2)`, `MINIFS(t.d, t.kind, "exit")`; with a plain number among them, or over no dates, it stays a number. In `examples/fund`, `last_exit` has its format and shows 8 Sep 2029 in `forge calculate` and as a date cell in the workbook. A result can change: a text function of such a value reads the date, not the number (`=LEFT(MAX(d), 4)` is `"2029"`, was `"4736"`; `MAX(d) & ""` is `2029-09-08`), and `ISNUMBER` of it is FALSE. Arithmetic and comparisons give what they gave. Tests beside each function, in the export's reading of a result (`rewrite.rs`) and for the fund (`the_funds_last_exit_is_a_date`) fail on the commit before.
- **`XNPV` gives `#NUM!` when a date comes before the first (roadmap T1-EXCEL-XNPV-ORDER).** Forge calculated it anyway (26.80 for -100, -50 and 200 on 2021-01-01, 2020-01-01 and 2022-01-01); Excel gives `#NUM!` (`ERROR.TYPE` 6), checked in Excel for iOS on 2026-11-19. Microsoft's documentation says the same of `XIRR`, but Excel calculates it (0.23587 for the same flows), as Forge does, so `XIRR` is unchanged. A result can change: a model whose dates are not in order after its first now shows the error for `XNPV`; put the earliest date first. The XNPV and XIRR code moved to `financial/dated.rs` to keep `financial.rs` under 1,500 lines. Tests in that file fail on the commit before; two cases in `edge_excel_checks.yaml` hold Excel's answers (2,162 validation cases now).
- **An included file that includes another file works as inline content (roadmap T1-INTERFACE-PARITY).** `forge_simulate` on `examples/bagel/bagel_simulate.yaml` sent as `content` failed with "Included file not found: .../costs.yaml", though the same model by path simulates: it includes `costs_risk.yaml`, which includes `costs.yaml`, and only the files the model itself includes were written to the temporary folder. The `includes` map now also holds the files an included file includes, under their own `as` names (`{"costs": ..., "base": ...}`), written relative to the file that names them; an include with no content in the map is named in the error, one namespace for two different files is refused, and a file named twice is written once. The same holds for `content` on the REST API. Inline and by path, the simulation gives the same threshold probabilities and rank correlations. Tests in `src/inline_model.rs` (`a_file_an_included_file_includes_*`, `a_nested_include_*`) fail on the commit before; `docs/MCP.md` (Inline Includes) describes it.
- **`MC.Discrete` can be used (roadmap T1-DISCRETE).** The function list, `docs/FEATURES.md` and `forge simulate --help` named it, but a model with it failed with "Unknown distribution type: Discrete". It takes two array constants (ADR-064), the values and their probabilities, each a row or a column: `MC.Discrete({0, 0.3}, {0.9, 0.1})` is 0 nine times in ten and 0.3 the tenth. The probabilities must add to 1, the two arrays must be as long, and anything else written is refused with the form to use. `examples/vineyard` draws a spring frost with it. Tests in `src/monte_carlo/distributions.rs` (`discrete_*`); they fail on the commit before.
- **Correlations that cannot hold together are refused naming the inputs.** The message named a cell of a matrix the model does not show, "Correlation matrix is not positive definite (element [2,2])"; it now says "the correlations of a, b and c cannot all hold at once", which is what a with b 0.9, b with c 0.9 and a with c -0.9 means, and how to fix it. A coefficient of exactly 1 or -1 is refused the same way, as before. Tests in `src/monte_carlo/correlation.rs`.
- **`forge simulate --seek` is not stopped by a formula its output does not use (roadmap T1-SEEK-UNUSED-FORMULA).** `--seek "P(valuation.value_created > 0) = 0.75" --vary offer.premium --min 0` failed on `valuation.synergy_cover: Division by zero`, a ratio over the premium that the value does not read; break-even, goal-seek, sensitivity and tornado had been given the same fix. Each of the seek's simulations now calculates every formula but fails only for one its output uses (`ArrayCalculator::calculate_outputs`, a table column included); `forge simulate` itself still reports a failure anywhere. The acquisition example's seek starts at a premium of 0, and gives the same answer from 0 as from 0.1.

- **`ROUND(x, -309)` and below gives 0, not an error (found by checking the roadmap's list of differences from Gnumeric).** From 10^309 the rounding unit is not a number a computer holds, so `ROUND(12345.6, -400)` said it had no result; Gnumeric and LibreOffice give 0, as does Forge now, and so does `ROUNDDOWN`. `ROUNDUP` of a number there still has no result, since the answer is a whole unit no double holds. **A result can change:** a model that relied on the error now gets 0.

- **`HOUR`, `MINUTE` and `SECOND` of a number past 9999-12-31 are `#NUM!` (found by checking the roadmap's list of differences from Gnumeric, in Excel for iOS).** `HOUR(2958466.5)` was 12 and `HOUR(1E+20)` was 0; Excel gives `#NUM!` for both, and for `MINUTE` and `SECOND` of `1E+20` and `HOUR(1E+10)`, while `HOUR(2958465.5)`, noon on the last day, is 12. **A result can change:** a model that read a time from a serial that large now gets the error.

- **The two 16,000-row timing tests (`SUMIF`, `INDEX/MATCH`) compare with a plain search of the column on the same machine, not with a fixed time.** The `SUMIF` limit was 2 s and a CI runner took 2.09 s and 2.19 s, failing the "README commands and timings" step on `main` twice (2026-11-18); a longer fixed time would hardly tell that runner from the copies of a column the test guards against (about 5 s there). Now the limit is 24 times (`SUMIF`) and 10 times (`INDEX/MATCH`) the time of 16,000 searches of 16,000 whole numbers, between what the search takes (15 and 4 times) and what the copies took (35 and 29 times). No change to Forge itself.

- **The Validation workflow's installs stop after 180 minutes, not 45.** The 45 minutes of rc.29 failed the R job on `main` (2026-11-18): the install of R and its packages takes 3 minutes on one run and took 155 on another the same morning, which passed. The limit is now above what has been seen, and still stops a hung install well before GitHub's six hours.

## [10.0.0-rc.29] - 2026-11-18

A sum near zero is 0, as in Excel. **A result can change:** `=0.1 + 0.2 - 0.3` was 5.55E-17 and is now 0, and so is a `SUM` that cancels, and everything calculated from them (a balance check written as its own cell now passes, as it does in Excel). Excel's rule was measured on 69 formulas in Excel for iPhone: the result of a formula's last `+` or `-`, and the last addition of `SUM`, is 0 when it is less than 8 units in the last place of the larger operand, and not inside parentheses around the whole formula, another operation or a function (ADR-066). A formula that wants the noise writes it in parentheses, as in Excel. `forge break-even`, `goal-seek`, `sensitivity` and `tornado` are no longer stopped by a formula their output does not use, ADR-065's comparisons are checked in Excel (all 13 agree), and the Validation installs stop after 45 minutes. A wrong answer fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-12-02. One difference from Excel is written down, not changed: Excel cuts a number typed in a formula to 15 significant digits (roadmap T1-EXCEL-LITERAL-DIGITS).

### Changed
- **The Validation workflow's installs stop after 45 minutes.** Installing Gnumeric, LibreOffice and R takes a few minutes as a rule; on 2026-11-18 the Ubuntu mirror was slow, two installs took 27 minutes and more, and nothing would have stopped one that hung before GitHub's six hours. A hang now fails the job within the hour, on the install step.
- **ADR-065's comparisons are checked in Excel (roadmap T1-EXCEL-EQUALITY).** They were taken from Microsoft's documentation. In the Excel app for iPhone (2026-11-18), Excel gives Forge's answer to all 13 formulas of the ADR, among them `1E15 + 1 = 1E15` (TRUE, where Gnumeric and LibreOffice give FALSE) and `XLOOKUP(0.1 + 0.2, ...)` over a range of 0.3s (#N/A, where LibreOffice finds 0.3). No result changes. The same sheet gave `0.1 + 0.2 - 0.3` and `1E15 + 0.2 - 1E15` as 0, where Forge gives 5.55E-17 and 0.25: Excel sets a final subtraction near zero to zero. That is now roadmap T1-EXCEL-SUBTRACTION, and VALIDATION.md and docs/STABILITY.md say so. ADR-065, `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_equality.yaml` and VALIDATION.md give Excel's answers.

### Fixed
- **A sum near zero is 0, as in Excel (ADR-066, roadmap T1-EXCEL-SUBTRACTION).** `=0.1 + 0.2 - 0.3` was 5.55E-17 and `=1E15 + 0.2 - 1E15` 0.25, where Excel gives 0 to both; a balance check written as its own cell, `difference: =assets - liabilities - equity` read by `=IF(difference = 0, ...)`, failed in Forge where it passes in Excel, and so did a `SUM` of debits and credits that cancel. Excel's rule was measured in the Excel app for iPhone on 69 formulas (2026-11-18), and Forge follows it: the result of a formula's last `+` or `-` is 0 when it is less than 8 units in the last place of the larger operand, which only numbers that nearly cancel can be, and so is the last addition of `SUM`, wherever it is. Not inside parentheses around the whole formula, another operation or a function: `=(0.1 + 0.2 - 0.3)` and `=ABS(0.1 + 0.2 - 0.3)` are 5.55E-17 in both. `1E15 + 1 - 1E15` is 1 (8 units) and `1 + 1E-15 - 1` 0 (5 units), as in Excel; `SUMPRODUCT`, `SUMIF` and the comparisons of ADR-065 are unchanged. Each row of a row formula is a cell, as in Excel. A wrong answer fixed: by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries it. 68 of the 69 formulas, with Excel's answers, are tests in `src/core/array_calculator/mod.rs` (`final_sum`), which fail on the commit before (the 69th is about a typed number, below), and 13 are cases of `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_subtraction.yaml` (2,151 cases in all). A test of the in-place writer that showed the noise of `(a + b) - 418.33` now writes it in parentheses, as Excel needs too. Found by the same sheets: Excel cuts a number typed in a formula to 15 significant digits, and Forge does not (roadmap T1-EXCEL-LITERAL-DIGITS).
- **`forge break-even`, `goal-seek`, `sensitivity` and `tornado` are not stopped by a formula their output does not use.** Each moves an input and reads one output, but a formula anywhere in the model that failed at that value of the input failed the whole command, or, in the matrix of `sensitivity` with two inputs, left the cell empty (`ERR`). `forge break-even examples/realestate/realestate.yaml --output valuation.npv --vary deal.price` stopped on `returns.equity_irr` ("IRR needs at least one positive and one negative cash flow") at 1% of the price, where the search starts, though the value does not use the IRR; it needed `--min` and `--max`. It now finds 4,753,414.10 without them, as the Python of `realestate_example` gives. The output is calculated with every other cell, and is an answer only when it and everything it uses calculated: when it fails, or uses a failed formula, the command fails with the same report as before. Tests in `src/cli/commands/analysis/goal_seek.rs` (`realestate_break_even`), `src/tornado/engine.rs`, `src/cli/commands/analysis/sensitivity.rs` (`two_inputs`) and `src/core/array_calculator/mod.rs` (`one_scalar`); those of break-even and the tornado were run on the commit before and fail there.

## [10.0.0-rc.28] - 2026-11-18

Fixes from the evaluation of rc.27 (2026-11-15), and uncertainty that moves year by year. **Two changes can break a setup someone wrote:** `forge mcp` keeps file paths inside the folder it starts in unless given `--root` or `--any-path`, and a formula nested more than 128 levels is refused (split it into named values or columns). Eight wrong answers are fixed, among them comparisons that now follow Excel's rule (`0.1 + 0.2 = 0.3` and `0.1 + 0.2 > 0.3` were both TRUE; ADR-065), `VLOOKUP` and `HLOOKUP` reading their index, `PERCENTILE.EXC` at its lowest k reading the value before the first, and `forge export` leaving an uncertain input's cell empty. A table column can now be an uncertain input drawn for each row on its own, and `examples/realestate` values a building under uncertainty, with the flexibility to sell early. By docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-12-02. ADR-065's comparisons are from Microsoft's documentation and not yet checked in Excel (roadmap T1-EXCEL-EQUALITY).

### Changed
- **No `too_many_lines` allow is left in the main crate or `forge-wasm`: 14 functions of 131 to 572 lines are split by what they do (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** The five evaluator dispatch tables (financial, statistical, date arithmetic, `DATEDIF` and Forge's own functions) have one function per spreadsheet function, listed in one table with a line each; `is_excel_function` is a sorted list searched by bisection, with a test that it stays sorted and has no repeats; the two Excel formula translators are a sequence of named passes, and the two passes that were written twice are shared; `translate` in the workbook references, `write_call` in the date rewriter, `write_number` for `TEXT` and the `calculate` command are cut at their stages. Nothing was rewritten: each part is the code that was there. The Gnumeric `Cargo.toml` allow hid nothing, and the R suite's three long functions are split too.
- **No `approx_constant` allow is left (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** 18 allows, each over a test module, were switched on; 17 hid nothing, and the 3 that did were `0.7071` in the sine and cosine tests of `trig.rs`, which now say `FRAC_1_SQRT_2`, the number they meant.
- **No `cast_precision_loss` is allowed any more: a count or a whole number becomes a number through `precision::count`, `whole` and `unsigned` (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** The crate-wide allow in `Cargo.toml` and in `forge-wasm`, and 25 attributes (nine of them covered a whole file of the evaluator), hid about 100 places where a length, a place in an array, a day count or a random integer was turned into a number with `as f64`. The three functions in `core/precision.rs` give the number `as f64` gives, to the last bit, for every input (above 2^53 the nearest number, a tie to the even one), built from 32-bit halves that convert without loss, with a test at each edge. No result changed: `forge simulate` (both samplings, seeds, a scenario, a seek), `bootstrap`, `tornado`, `scenarios`, `decision-tree`, `real-options`, `bayesian` and `calculate` print the same bytes on the examples before and after, the Gnumeric suite gives the same result for all 2,138 cases, and the R suite the same for its 50 passing and 7 skipped tests. The two validation suites do not link Forge, so each keeps its own small conversion (`whole` in `validation/gnumeric`, `count` in `validation/r`), with tests.
- **`forge simulate`'s printing is a module of its own, `src/cli/commands/simulate/print.rs`.** `simulate.rs` would have passed 1,500 lines with the tests of the real-estate example; it is now a folder, the simulation in `mod.rs` and how the results are printed and written to a file in `print.rs`, each with its tests. Nothing it prints changed.
- **The formula evaluator has no cast that could truncate, lose a sign or wrap (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** One `#![allow]` covered the whole evaluator, and 18 more covered its files and functions: 104 casts. Each number Excel reads as a whole number (a row, a count, a month, a return type, a mode) is cut toward zero through a checked conversion, as Excel cuts it, and a number no integer of the type holds is the error Excel gives, where `as` saturated it to the largest integer in silence. The edges are tested function by function, and checked against Gnumeric 1.12.56 where it has the function. Messages name the number as it was written: `LARGE: k=-0.5 out of range`, not `k=0`. `t.col[-1]` is out of bounds, where it read the first value. An array index, `INDEX`, `CHOOSE`, `OFFSET`, `ADDRESS`, `XMATCH`'s modes, `WEEKDAY` and `WEEKNUM`'s return types, `YEARFRAC`'s basis, `TEXT`'s dates and `RANDBETWEEN` give the same results as before for every number they took. `DATEDIF` counts its months and days in exact floats. A comparison of 152 commands against the binary before shows the same output. `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs` counts 95 lint allows, from 114.
- **Outside the calculator, no cast that could truncate, lose a sign or wrap is allowed (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** 37 allows hid 44 casts in the simulation, bootstrap, real options, tornado, the Excel export and import, the command line and MCP. A float becomes an integer through a checked conversion (`num_traits::ToPrimitive`), and each edge is decided and tested where `as` saturated in silence. A percentile below 0 or above 100 reads the smallest or largest value and a NaN the smallest, as before. `forge sensitivity` refuses a range with more steps than can be counted, where it tried to allocate them. Excel rows and columns are counted as Excel addresses them (`u32`, `u16`), so the writer's own limits refuse a cell past the sheet, where a column past 65,535 wrapped to the first. A binomial tree has `u16` steps: `binomial_steps` above 65,535 is refused when the model is read. Its work grows with the square of the steps, and the exponents and the time step are now exact conversions. MCP takes `iterations` as a count this machine can hold. A comparison of 152 commands against the binary before shows the same numbers. `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs` counts 118 lint allows, from 155.
- **No `float_cmp` is allowed any more: 64 allows of exact float comparison are gone (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** They hid 236 exact comparisons, 226 of them in tests. A test asserts an exact number with `assert_exact!`, which compares the bits, so a result off in its last digit, or `-0.0` for `0.0`, fails where `assert_eq!` let it pass; all 1,983 library tests pass with it. The ten exact comparisons in the engine are exact by design, as in Excel: `MODE` counts equal values, a variance line on budget has no variance, `DDB` and `VDB` find the first and last period, and the in-place writer keeps a number written as `16` or `16.0`. They now call `core::precision::same`, IEEE equality under a name, with a test against every edge (zeros, infinities, NaN, subnormals). No answer changes. `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs` counts 155 lint `allow` or `expect` attributes, from 213.
- **No test is outside the code it tests: the checks of the repository's own text are `validation/repo`, and the `tests/` folder is gone (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE, done).** The 8 files left in `tests/` and the Gnumeric suite's `doc_counts` checked what Forge says about itself: the conventions, the no-skipping ratchet, the counts in the docs, the examples in docs/EXAMPLES.md, the README's commands, the posts, docs/WORKFLOWS.md and the install script. Each is now a module of `validation/repo`: its code finds what the check reads, and its tests, inline, assert that the repository passes. The checks that run commands run the built binary as a reader types them, like the Gnumeric and R suites (ADR-057), because a crate that depends on Forge's library cannot pass the `cargo` lint group without allowing `multiple_crate_versions`. CI runs them on the release binary in a new Repository checks job, which the release jobs wait for; the pre-commit hook and `make test` build the binary and run them. The README check no longer skips `forge simulate --seek` in a debug build, and the Gnumeric count check is inline in its `runner.rs`. `tests/no_skipping.rs` is `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs`, and it counts 0 test files in `tests/` folders, from 9, and 5 places of coverage-only code, from 8.
- **The 17 test files in `tests/` that started the `forge` binary are inline, and call the command line in-process (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE).** A test runs `forge` with `cli::forge(args)`, which is `run_from` on buffers, and talks to the MCP server through `mcp::Session`, an in-memory connection to `serve_on` with a timeout on each answer. Each file's tests went to the command they check: `compare`, `report`, `simulate`, `variance`, `sensitivity`, `goal-seek`, `tornado` and the other analyses; tests of the whole command line, such as `agent_friction` and `--version`, went to `src/cli/run.rs`; the formula-depth tests to the calculator; the MCP folder limit to `src/mcp/mod.rs`. `forge functions | head` ending quietly is the one check only a real process can make, so it is now a step of CI's MCP Protocol Test job. The 75 tests are 74 library tests and that step, so there are 1,981 library tests, from 1,907. Most of the files ran nothing under coverage (`cfg(not(coverage))`), so `tests/no_skipping.rs` counts 8 places of coverage-only code, from 24, and 9 test files in `tests/` folders, from 26: the checks of the repository's text, which come next. The coverage floors in CI rise to 92.4% of lines, 88.5% of functions and 91.1% of regions.
- **`analysis.rs`, `excel_io.rs` and `prediction.rs` are folders with a module per command, to leave room for those tests.** They held several commands each, in 1,308 to 1,498 lines; every item moved as it was. `compare_core` moved to `compare.rs`, and `forge compare` reads the terminal's width from its console, as 4c meant it to. The property tests' saved failure cases (`properties.proptest-regressions`) move next to `src/lib.rs`, where proptest looks for them since the tests moved there; they were not re-run in between.
- **The command line runs in-process: each command writes to the console it is given, not straight to the terminal (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE).** `cli::run_from(args, console)` is `forge` from its arguments to its exit code. `main.rs` hands it the process's streams, and a test hands it buffers (`cli::console::capture`) and reads what a user would see. Commands write with `outln!` and `errln!` instead of `println!`, and the help, version and usage errors go through the same console in clap's colours. The command-line definitions moved from `src/main_args.rs` to `src/cli/args.rs`, with each command's flags in a struct of its own. Run against the binary before the change, 152 commands, each with and without colour, give the same output, messages, exit code and files. The exceptions are run-to-run noise (timings, and names printed in HashMap order) and these:
  - `forge schema v9` reports its error like every other command (`Error: Validation error: Unknown model format 'v9'. ...`) instead of a red `Error:` of its own.
  - When `forge examples NAME --run` fails, it gives the exit code as `1`, not `Some(1)`. It now runs the example in the same process instead of starting `forge` again.
  - A write that fails, such as on a full disk, fails the command with `Error:` and exit 1; `println!` panicked with exit 101.
  - The REST API's export and import no longer print the command line's progress on the server's output.
  - `forge update`'s question reads the answer through the console, without a panic if it cannot.
- **`main.rs` and `forge mcp` are measured by the coverage, which now runs the built program after the tests (`scripts/coverage.sh`, used by CI and `make coverage`).** It runs `forge --version` and an MCP session over stdin and stdout, which no in-process test can reach. Their coverage-only stubs are gone, and so is `run_api_server`'s: a configuration it refuses returns before the server's log starts, and a test runs that path. `tests/no_skipping.rs` counts 24 places of coverage-only code, from 31, and 213 lint allows, from 215. The coverage floors in CI rise to 91.9% of lines, 87.9% of functions and 90.5% of regions. Six command functions that had grown to over 100 lines are split by what they print, with no `too_many_lines` allowed.
- **The 19 test files in `tests/` that call the library are inline in the module they test (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE).** Each is a group in that module's `mod tests`: the array constants in the formula parser, the bagel and demo examples in `cli/commands/examples.rs`, the cached text and text numbers in the exporter's `cells.rs`, the column copies and scalar timings in the calculator, the row aggregates in `whole_column.rs`, empty cells, scalar dates and number formats in the parser, scenario forms in `scenarios/config.rs`, the function list in the registry, large numbers in the in-place writer, the import tests in the importer and `previous_rows.rs`, the real dates in `workbook_refs/dates/`, the property tests in `lib.rs`, and forge-wasm's cases in its `lib.rs`. The date kinds moved from `dates/mod.rs` to `dates/kinds.rs` to keep it under 1,500 lines. 26 files are left in `tests/` folders, from 45: the 17 that start the binary and the 9 that check the repository's text. The release-build and weekly workflows run the moved timing and property tests by their new names. The library tests are the same, 86 more of them inline.
- **No test module is left in a file of its own (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE).** The last 18 are inline in the file or module they test: the REST API's three, each engine's integration tests (Bayesian, bootstrap, decision trees, real options, scenarios, tornado, Monte Carlo) in the engine's `mod.rs`, `forge compare`'s and `forge report`'s in their files, the function registry's in `registry.rs`, the parser's two in `parser/mod.rs`, the what-if tests in `cli/commands/mod.rs` and the unknown-key tests in `lib.rs`, where all the configurations meet. The 1,798 library tests are the same before and after. The coverage floors in CI rise to 88.9% of lines, 85.4% of functions and 87.3% of regions, as the inline tests moved in.
- **The six files that passed 1,500 lines with their tests back are split by what they do, every test inline in the module it tests (roadmap T1-TESTS-INLINE).** The evaluator (`value.rs`, `operators.rs`), the calculator (`order.rs`, `names.rs`, `errors.rs`, `includes.rs`), the workbook references (`workbook_refs/reverse.rs`, and `dates/` as `rewrite.rs` and `print.rs`), the MCP server (its 20 tools in four routers: model, analysis, uncertainty, reference) and the Excel exporter (`exporter/layout.rs`, `exporter/cells.rs`). 18 test modules are left in files of their own, from 36, and the 1,798 library tests are the same before and after; nothing a user sees changes. Three module-wide `float_cmp` allows went with the old files: their exact comparisons go through `is_exactly`, which compares the bits.
- **No skipping the coverage, lint and formatting rules, and CI now enforces a coverage floor.** The Coverage job printed a rounded function percentage for a badge; it now prints line, function and region coverage and fails below a floor that only rises, to 100, and `make coverage` fails below 100. `tests/no_skipping.rs` counts each way round the three rules (on 2026-11-16: 218 lint `allow` or `expect` attributes, 16 lints allowed in Cargo.toml files, the `cargo` lint group missing in two crates, 31 places of coverage-only code and 2 ignored tests, and the tests kept outside the code they test: 45 files in `tests/` folders and 36 test modules in files of their own) and fails if any count rises, so they can only fall, to zero (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING, T1-TESTS-INLINE). The 1,500-line limit now covers every crate, tests included. The rules are in CLAUDE.md.
- **`forge mcp` keeps file paths inside one folder by default.** Without `--root`, the folder is the one the server starts in, so an agent, or a prompt injected into one, can no longer name any file you can reach. `--any-path` gives the old behaviour. A desktop app may start the server in `/` or your home folder: name the folder with `--root` there (docs/MCP.md). `forge serve` is unchanged.
- **`forge export` writes `VLOOKUP` and `HLOOKUP` as formulas where Excel's table is one column or one row, as Forge's is.** Both were always written as their values, with a note, since they ignored their index. A `VLOOKUP` over a table column or a column constant `{1;2;3}`, and an `HLOOKUP` over a row constant `{1,2,3}`, now mean the same in Excel: the same index rules and searches. An `HLOOKUP` over a table column is still written as its value (Excel's table would be a column of the sheet, searched in its first cell), and so is a lookup that touches a date, a row constant in a `VLOOKUP` and a computed table; the warning says which. In a row formula, the table of either is the whole column (ADR-058), as the calculation already read it. `forge import` still reads neither: a workbook's table may be wider. Checked by 22 new cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_lookup_one_line.yaml`, which pass in Gnumeric 1.12.56 after export and import (2,114 cases in all, 2,113 passed, 1 skipped), and by `VLOOKUP` and `HLOOKUP` in the generated cases of `validation/sweep` (2,700 a pull request): 2,400 cases over six seeds agree with LibreOffice 26.2 and Gnumeric but one: `-1E-12` found 0, fixed by ADR-065 (below).

### Added
- **A table column can be an uncertain input, drawn for each row on its own.** `forge simulate` refused `shock: "=MC.Normal(0, 0.06)"` in a table with "Unknown function: MC.Normal" on each row, so a market that moves year by year needed one scalar a year and a `CHOOSE` to pick them. Each row of such a column is now an input of its own, named as a row of an output is (`years.shock (row 2)`), with its own draws, its own place among the drivers and its plan value; the rows are independent. `forge calculate` names the column when it refuses it, and `forge export` writes its plan values with the formula in a note. `examples/realestate/realestate_flex.yaml` uses it for its yearly shocks. Tests check that three rows of MC.Normal(0, 0.1) sum with a standard deviation of sqrt(3) x 0.1 and are not correlated.
- **`examples/realestate`: a building of 24 flats valued under uncertainty.** Linden Court (fictional) is offered at 4,600,000. `realestate.yaml` is the building year by year: rent, vacancy and costs, a sale in year 10 at an exit cap rate, and a 65% loan. The plan says it is worth 155,715.31 more than it costs, with an 11.37% return on the equity; four scenarios, a tornado and a break-even price go with it. `realestate_risk.yaml` simulates 10,000 futures with five uncertain inputs, and the median falls to -28,741.40: the flaw of averages. `realestate_flex.yaml` adds the flexibility of Geltner and de Neufville: the market moves year by year in cycles, and selling once the building fetches 135% of its price is worth 100,582.17 on average over holding ten years, and -54,006.87 in a random walk. The numbers were written again in Python from the description and agree with Forge's. Forge Studio opens it as an example set, and forge-wasm compares its calculation with the native one.
- **`examples/microgrid`: an island trading diesel for solar and a battery over 20 years.** Ilha Corva (fictional) burns 1.2 million litres of diesel a year. `microgrid.yaml` is the island year by year in two tables, `energy` and `money`: how much solar the grid can take depends on the battery, which fades and is replaced in year 11; a 10-year loan is repaid before then. The plan, 3.4 MWp and 10 MWh, is worth 13,161,304.86 at 8%, and four scenarios, a tornado, a break-even and two size sweeps go with it. `microgrid_risk.yaml` simulates 10,000 futures, and `microgrid_decision.yaml` weighs building now against a smaller system or waiting a year for cheaper batteries. The numbers were written again in Python from the description and agree with Forge's; a test recalculates each of the decision tree's 36 numbers from the model, so the tree cannot fall behind it. Forge Studio opens it as a tenth example set, and forge-wasm compares its calculation with the native one.
- **`forge simulate` shows the plan beside the mean (roadmap T1-SIM-MEAN-VS-PLAN).** `forge calculate` gives the bagel year a profit of 786.32 and `forge simulate` a mean of -15,925.94 for the same plan, and nothing said why: the uncertain inputs lean above their plan values. Each uncertain input now shows its plan value beside its sampled mean (`assumptions.staff_hours_per_day mean=32.67 plan=32.00 (value)`), and each output its value at plan, the model calculated once with every uncertain input at its plan (`At plan: 786.32`), with a note when the mean is more than a tenth of a standard deviation away; a table column's rows get an `at plan` column. The plan value is the `value:` written beside the `MC.*` formula, or else the distribution's mode (triangular, PERT), mean as typed (normal, lognormal), midpoint (uniform) or likeliest value (discrete). The draws are made first, so no other number changes. `-o results.json`, the YAML and `forge_simulate` carry `at_plan` and the inputs' `mean`, `plan` and `plan_from`. The bagel example now writes the plan's figures as `value:` beside its four uncertain inputs, and its README explains the gap. `tests/simulate_plan.rs` checks the value at plan against `bagel.yaml`, the inputs' means against their closed forms, and the gap against the closed form of a model linear in independent inputs (-16,703.34).
- **`forge simulate --output-var` shows only the outputs named (roadmap T1-SIM-SHOW-ONE).** `forge simulate examples/bagel/bagel_simulate.yaml` prints every output in `monte_carlo.outputs`, about 80 lines; `--output-var year.profit` prints that one, with the same numbers it has among all of them (the draws do not depend on the outputs). Repeat it for more; a name can be written without its group when only one output ends with it (`worst_month_profit`), a table column is all its rows, and a name that is not an output, or ends more than one, exits 1 with the outputs listed. `forge_simulate` takes the same choice as `outputs`. It cannot be used with `--seek`, which reports its own output. Tests in `tests/simulate_outputs.rs` and `src/mcp/server_tests.rs`.
- **Build attestations for the release archives.** The Release workflow signs a statement that it built each archive from its commit; `gh attestation verify <archive> --repo mollendorff-ai/forge` checks it (SECURITY.md). `checksums.txt` stays, for broken downloads.
- **Clippy on the oldest supported Rust, tests included.** The MSRV job now lints every target (`--all-targets`) as well as building; one doc comment that Rust 1.97's clippy refused is split, and the ten test sites it refused (`float_cmp` on exact whole numbers, a `const fn`) are fixed, so `cargo clippy --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings` passes on 1.97.

### Fixed
- **Two numbers compare as Excel compares them: to 15 significant digits for `=`, `<`, `>` and the rest, exactly for an exact lookup (ADR-065, roadmap T1-EQUAL-NEAR-ZERO).** `=` and `<>` treated any two numbers within 1E-10 as equal, so `-1E-12 = 0`, `1E-12 = 2E-12` and `1E-300 = 0` were TRUE, while `<`, `<=`, `>` and `>=` compared exactly, so `0.1 + 0.2 = 0.3` and `0.1 + 0.2 > 0.3` were both TRUE and `0.1 + 0.2 <= 0.3` FALSE. All six now round each number to 15 significant digits, as Microsoft describes Excel's comparison: `0.1 + 0.2 <= 0.3` is TRUE, `0.1 + 0.2 > 0.3` FALSE, `1 + 1E-14 = 1` FALSE, `-1E-12 = 0` FALSE. `SWITCH` matches as `=` does, and the criteria of `COUNTIF`, `SUMIF` and their family compare by the same rule, so `COUNTIF` counts `100 * 1.1` as 110 (it compared within 2.2E-16). An exact lookup (`MATCH` 0, `XMATCH`, `XLOOKUP`, `VLOOKUP` and `HLOOKUP` with FALSE) compares exactly, as Excel's does: `MATCH(0.1 + 0.2, {0.3}, 0)` is #N/A, as in LibreOffice and Gnumeric, where it found 0.3. `RANK` finds its number exactly. Excel was not at hand: the rule is from Microsoft's documentation and Q&A answers, LibreOffice 26.2 gives Forge's answer on 24 of 24 checks, and ADR-065 lists the formulas to check in Excel (roadmap T1-EXCEL-EQUALITY). `1E15 + 1 = 1E15` is TRUE by the rule and FALSE in both engines. Every example's results, the 2,138 cases of `validation/gnumeric` (24 new, in `edge_equality.yaml`) and the 27,000 generated cases of the weekly seed are unchanged or pass; the `equal-near-zero` rule in `validation/sweep/known.py` is gone. Tests in `src/core/precision.rs`, `operators.rs` (its `fifteen_digits` tests), `lookup/match_fn.rs` (`exact_numbers`), `criterion.rs` and `statistical.rs` fail on the commit before.
- **`VLOOKUP` and `HLOOKUP` read their index, and search as `MATCH` does.** Both ignored their third argument: `VLOOKUP(2, {1;2;3}, 5, FALSE)` was 2, where Excel gives #REF!. Forge has no two-dimensional values (ADR-064), so the table of a `VLOOKUP` is one column and that of an `HLOOKUP` one row, and the only column or row to return from is the first. The index is now cut to a whole number (`1.9` is 1, and `TRUE` and `"1"` are 1), and is #VALUE! below 1 and #REF! past the table, as Microsoft documents for Excel. It is checked before the search, so a value that is not there is still #REF!. The #REF! message suggests `XLOOKUP` or `INDEX` with `MATCH` to return another column. The approximate search (`TRUE`, the default) found the largest value at most the lookup value, searching every value, and skipped text. It is now `MATCH`'s with match type 1, the halving search Excel uses: on values sorted ascending it gives the same answer, on other values it gives what `INDEX(range, MATCH(v, range, 1))` gives, as `LibreOffice` does, and `VLOOKUP("b", {"a";"c"}, 1, TRUE)` is "a", where it was #N/A. Checked in `LibreOffice` on ranges of cells (it gives Err:502 for both bad indexes). A test that accepted either an error or a number now asserts one. Tests in `src/core/array_calculator/evaluator/lookup/xlookup.rs` (its `one_column` tests) fail on the commit before.
- **`forge export` writes an uncertain input as its plan value, where it left the cell empty.** An `MC.*` input has no Excel equivalent and no calculated value, so its cell was empty while the warning said "written as a value", every formula that read it ran on nothing in Excel, and the model was "exported, but not checked" because it did not calculate. Each uncertain input, the model's and an included file's, is now calculated and written at its plan value, the one `forge simulate` shows the outputs at (the `value:` beside it, or its distribution's mode, mean or midpoint), with the Forge formula in the cell's note as before: `examples/monte-carlo.yaml` exports its NPV at plan, 2,794,690.96, as a Python calculation gives it, and `examples/bagel/bagel_simulate.yaml` its year at 786.32. A formula left without a value, in a model that does not calculate, is now listed as "left empty". `forge import` still brings the cell back as its value; the distribution stays in the YAML file. Tests in `src/cli/commands/excel_io/export.rs` (`uncertain_inputs`).
- **Six formulas crashed on a number past an `i32`, and now give Excel's error or its answer.** In a debug build each panicked on an overflow, and in a release build it wrapped to a wrong result. Each has a test that fails on the commit before (5a3b541):
  - `DATE(2020, 1E+10, 1)`, `DATE(2020, 1, -1E+10)`, and `EDATE` or `EOMONTH` with `-1E+10` months, are #NUM!, as in Gnumeric.
  - `TIME(1000000, 0, 0)` is 0.666…, the time of day it lands on, as in Gnumeric. An hour, minute or second no `i32` holds is #NUM!, as in Excel.
  - `SEQUENCE(1E+20, 1E+20)` is #VALUE!. An array holds from 1 to 1,048,576 values, the rows of a sheet: 0 rows or columns is #CALC! and a negative count #VALUE!, as in Excel, where `SEQUENCE(0)` was an empty array.
  - `RANDARRAY` with a minimum above its maximum is #VALUE!, as in Excel, and with the two equal it gives that number. Before, both crashed on an empty random range.
  - `DB(1000, 100, 3E+9, 2)` is 0, as in Gnumeric: the rate rounds to 0.000.
  - `PERCENTILE.EXC` at its lowest k, `1/(n+1)`, is the first value. For 48 values k × 49 is 0.9999999999999999, which was cut to rank 0 and read the value before the first.
- **`ISEVEN(1E+20)` is TRUE and `ISODD(1E+20)` FALSE, as in Gnumeric.** A number past 2^63 was cut to the largest `i64`, which is odd. The remainder of the float is exact for every number.
- **`WORKDAY` stays in the dates Excel holds.** Counting past 9999-12-31, or back before 1900-01-01, is #NUM!, as in Gnumeric. It gave a day in the year 10000, or a day number below 1, and a count of `1E+10` days ran for billions of steps.
- **`forge real-options -o results.json` writes JSON.** It wrote YAML whatever the file was called, where every other analysis writes JSON for a `.json` path and YAML otherwise. It now uses the same rule. A test in `src/cli/commands/prediction/real_options.rs` fails on the commit before.
- **A distribution with an argument that is not a finite number is refused, not simulated into NaN or a crash (roadmap T1-NONFINITE).** `1E+400`, `inf` and `NaN` are read as numbers. `MC.PERT(0, 1, 1E+400)` ended `forge simulate` on a panic (exit 134) and a `forge mcp` server with it; `MC.Triangular(0, 1, 1E+400)`, `MC.Lognormal(1, 1E+400)` and `MC.Normal(0, nan)` printed NaN statistics with exit 0, a silent wrong answer. Each argument of each distribution must now be finite, and so must `max - min` (`MC.PERT(-1E+308, 1E+308, 1E+308)` has finite arguments and an infinite range) and the draws at both ends (`MC.Normal(0, 1E+308)`); the error names the scalar and the argument, as in `m.x: MC.PERT max must be a finite number, not inf`, exit 1 on the command line and an error answer over MCP, after which the server answers the next call. A wide triangular distribution such as `MC.Triangular(-1E+307, 0, 1E+307)` drew infinite values because its quantile multiplied before taking the root; it now takes the roots first when the product would overflow, and a model that calculated before draws exactly the same values. Tests in `src/monte_carlo/distributions.rs` and `tests/nonfinite_distributions.rs`, which fail on the commit before.
- **`MC.PERT(0, 1, 1E+308)` samples instead of panicking.** Its arguments and its range are finite, but its shape multiplied 4 by `max - mode` before dividing by the range, which overflowed to infinity, and the Beta it builds panicked. The shape divides first; multiplying by 4 is exact, so every other PERT draws the same values. A test in `src/monte_carlo/distributions.rs`.
- **A formula nested too deep is refused, not calculated until the stack overflows (roadmap T1-DEPTH).** A formula of 10,000 parentheses, or of 100,000 terms added, ended `forge calculate` with a stack overflow (exit 134), and `forge mcp` died on a call with one. A formula may now nest 128 levels (parentheses, function arguments, `[ ]`, a run of `-` or `^`, and each operator of a chain such as `a + b + c`, which nests to the left in the tree); one level more is an error that names the cell and the limit, exit 1 on the command line and an error answer over MCP, after which the server answers the next call. **A formula nested more than 128 levels that used to calculate (the command line managed about 1,000) is now refused**; split it into named values or columns. `tests/formula_depth.rs` checks both sides of the limit for each way to nest, and the deepest tree the limit lets through, on a 2 MiB thread.
- **Stale counts and wording, now checked by tests.** VALIDATION.md and docs/STABILITY.md said Excel was checked once, on 18 results; they now give the three rounds (2026-11-06, -14 and -15). VALIDATION.md said fourteen cases are checked against Forge only; the suite has 35, and `validation/gnumeric/tests/doc_counts.rs` now compares that count too. The README's unit-test row gave a dated count (1,873) and now points to the CI badge. "Battle-proven" is gone from docs/FPA-PREDICTION-METHODS.md, and a test keeps such words out of the docs.

### Removed
- **`src/core/array_calculator/dates.rs`, 612 lines that nothing called (roadmap T1-NO-SKIPPING).** An `allow(dead_code)` hid it: a second, older set of date functions (`DATEDIF`, `EDATE`, `EOMONTH`, `WORKDAY`, `NETWORKDAYS`, `YEARFRAC`, a day-of-week by Zeller's congruence) beside the ones the evaluator runs, untested, and with 24 casts that could truncate, lose a sign or wrap behind one more allow. No result changes: a comparison of 152 commands against the binary before shows the same output. `validation/repo/src/no_skipping.rs` counts 114 lint allows, from 118. The coverage floors in CI rise to 93.1% of lines, 89.2% of functions and 91.9% of regions, from 92.4%, 88.5% and 91.2%: the file was counted, and never run.

## [10.0.0-rc.27] - 2026-11-15

Forge is easier to start with an AI and easier for a program to use, after fresh agents ran the prompts and Sentinel ran the pipeline: `install.sh`, a prompt to paste, five tested prompts (docs/PROMPTS.md), four workflows with diagrams (docs/WORKFLOWS.md), and every item the agents reported fixed (T1-AGENT-FRICTION, T1-PIPELINE-FINDINGS). **Two changes can break a command someone wrote:** `-o` is always the output file and `-v` always `--verbose` (the output variable is `--output-var`), and a constant where `COUNTIF` and its family read a range is refused, because Excel refuses it too. The second is a wrong answer fixed (a workbook Excel could not open), so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-11-29.

### Changed
- **`-o` is always the output file and `-v` always `--verbose` (roadmap T1-AGENT-FRICTION).** In `forge sensitivity` and `forge break-even`, `-o` named the output variable, and in `sensitivity`, `goal-seek` and `break-even`, `-v` was `--vary`, while every other command used them for the output file and `--verbose`. Those short forms are gone (`-v` is now `--verbose` there too), so an old `-o profit` or `-v price` is refused instead of meaning something else. The output variable is `--output-var`, as in `forge tornado`; `--output` still works for it. A unit test in `src/main_args.rs` checks the short flags of every command and that every `forge ...` example in the help parses (it found `-v2` and `-r2`, which never existed). The command-line definitions moved from `src/main.rs` to `src/main_args.rs`.
- **The quick start puts `install.sh` first, and its `cargo binstall` line works.** The bare `cargo binstall mollendorff-forge` found no version, as Forge has only release candidates; it now asks for `>=10.0.0-rc.1` and disables compiling, which binstall did silently for six minutes in a sandbox whose GitHub token cannot use the GraphQL API. Building from crates.io (`cargo install mollendorff-forge --version '>=10.0.0-rc.1'`) is listed beside building from a copy of the repository.

### Added
- **A bare name works where it names one scalar.** `forge break-even`, `goal-seek` and `sensitivity` (and their MCP tools) take `price_per_dozen` for `assumptions.price_per_dozen`, as the same model's scenarios and tornado sections write it; a name that fits two scalars is refused with both. **`forge variance --fail-on-alert`** exits with 1 when a line exceeds the threshold, for a script or CI (without it the exit code is 0, as before, and the help now says so). **`forge real-options`** prints what each option adds over the traditional NPV, and the JSON has `value_added`; it used to label a defer option's project value "option value".
- **docs/WORKFLOWS.md: the AI pipeline has run once.** Sentinel ran the fourth workflow on Apple's release for Q4 of fiscal 2026, with an AI in a chat and Forge as the only calculator; the page links to the run, which is in git (the saved filing, the model, Forge's results, the brief, every prompt and answer), and says what the run does not show: a person started it and checked four input figures, it is one quarter of one company, and no scheduled job ran it. The roadmap item T2-PIPELINE-DEMO is done; what Forge made hard for a program is T1-PIPELINE-FINDINGS (two documentation errors in docs/MCP.md: the Monte Carlo `enabled` flag, which `forge simulate` never reads, and the function count, 173 against 203; and a tornado that lists an input that cannot move the output with a swing of 0 and no note).
- **The last results of T1-EXCEL-CHECKS, checked in Excel (2026-11-15).** Excel agreed with Forge on all eight that calculate: `LARGE` with k = 1.2 and 1.8 (rounded up), `MATCH` type -2, `MATCH` over mixed numbers and texts, `DB` with a fractional life and period, `COUNTIF(dates, "*")` (0). They are in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_excel_checks_2.yaml`, 2,092 cases in all, and the roadmap item is closed. ADR-064 is marked Accepted.
- **docs/WORKFLOWS.md: four ways to use Forge, with diagrams.** A person alone (YAML in git), a person with an AI in a chat, a pipeline without AI (Forge as the deterministic calculator at month-end) and a pipeline with AI (an agent writes the model, Forge checks and calculates, a person reviews the pull request). Each has a Mermaid diagram, which GitHub draws, and the commands it uses, run against rc.26: failed formulas exit with 1, `forge validate` exits with 1 on a file whose stored values no longer match its formulas, and `forge report` gives JSON or CSV. The page says plainly that the two pipelines are architecture not yet run end to end; roadmap item T2-PIPELINE-DEMO is to show the AI one with Sentinel. `tests/workflows_doc.rs` checks that every command the page names exists. The README gets a "Four ways to use Forge" section near the top, with a diagram of the four around one engine.
- **The prompts say the conversation goes on.** Each of the six prompts (the README's and the five in docs/PROMPTS.md) now tells the AI that the user will keep asking questions about the model and to use `forge --help` for the rest. A fresh agent given the first prompt and three follow-ups (rent rising from month 7, a second product, NPV of the monthly profits) answered all three with Forge in about 35 seconds of commands; the checks agree (the rent rise costs 6 x 600 = 3,600 of profit). The tests check the line.
- **docs/PROMPTS.md: five prompts to paste into an AI, and how to write your own.** Everyday work first: build a model from a description; bring a workbook in from Excel and back; budget against actual; what-if (scenarios, break-even, goal seek, sensitivity); risk and decisions (simulation, tornado, decision tree, real options). Each installs Forge with `install.sh`, asks the AI to check one result its own way rather than repeat Forge's numbers, ends with every YAML file printed to open in Forge Studio (pasted on its YAML tab, then "Check and apply", which was tried), and says what to do without a sandbox. Each was run by a fresh agent on 2026-11-15 before it went in, and the page gives the outcome under each; all five worked on the first try, in one to three minutes. `tests/install_script.rs` checks that every prompt installs the same way, ends the same way, and names only example files that exist. The README links to it.
- **`install.sh`: Forge in one line, with nothing to build.** `curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/mollendorff-ai/forge/main/install.sh | sh -s -- --examples` downloads the release archive for the system (Linux or macOS, x86_64 or ARM), checks it against the release's `checksums.txt` and refuses it if they differ, puts `forge` in `~/.local/bin` (or `FORGE_INSTALL_DIR`), and with `--examples` puts that release's examples in `./forge-examples`. It finds the latest release from GitHub's redirect, with no API call, so a sandbox's GitHub token cannot get in the way. Checked on Linux x86_64 with curl and with wget, under dash, for an older release (rc.24's checksum file names a folder too), a version that does not exist, a damaged archive and an examples folder that already exists; not yet run on macOS or ARM. `tests/install_script.rs` checks that it asks for archives the release workflow publishes.
- **README: a prompt to paste into an AI** ("Try it with your AI", right after Forge Studio). It names the project exactly, installs with `install.sh`, runs the bagel example, and asks the AI to recompute two results with its own code rather than compare them with the README, then change an input and explain the result. Found when Claude and Grok, asked to try Forge on another machine, could not tell which Forge was meant and then built it from source. A fresh agent given the prompt installed Forge in 2.4 s and agreed with Forge to six decimals (2026-11-15). It needs an AI that can run commands: the maintainer's tries worked on Claude and Grok, free tiers included, and not on the free tiers of ChatGPT and Gemini, which have no sandbox; the prompt ends by asking an AI that cannot run it to say so, invent nothing and point to Forge Studio.

### Fixed
- **A Bayesian network with a continuous node is refused with a reason.** The node type was accepted and inference then failed with "No factors remaining". It now says that continuous nodes are not supported yet (ADR-023 lists them as future work) and to give the node states and probabilities. The unreachable internal function `most_likely_explanation` is renamed `most_likely_states`: it gives each node's own most likely state, not the most probable explanation of the whole network. Roadmap item T2-BAYESIAN-LINK records what a Bayesian network is missing for a financial model.
- **What five fresh agents stumbled on (T1-AGENT-FRICTION).** `forge validate` on a model never calculated said "All scalar values match their formulas!" with nothing stored; it now says the formulas are valid and how many have no stored value yet. After an edit it says the formulas are valid and the stored values out of date, and to run `forge calculate`. In `forge variance`, a line on target said `is_favorable: true` but was not counted as favorable; it is now `null`, neither. `forge export` names what stays only in the YAML file (analysis sections such as `tornado:`, `better:`, comments), and `forge import` says when every formula was kept. Help: `forge tornado` showed `--output npv` for the output variable, `forge calculate` did not mention the `.bak` copy, and `forge variance` did not explain `better:`. The examples' comments gave paths under `examples/`, which fail in the `forge-examples/` folder `install.sh` makes; the starter says where the other format versions are listed. Tests: `tests/agent_friction.rs`.
- **Forge refuses a constant where `COUNTIF` and its family read a range.** Checked in Excel on 2026-11-15, `=COUNTIF({1,2,3,2},2)` and `=SUMIF({1,2,3},">1")` are both refused ("There's a problem with this formula"), though Forge, Gnumeric and LibreOffice accepted them, so a model using one exported a workbook Excel could not open. Forge now refuses a constant in a range argument of `COUNTIF`, `SUMIF`, `AVERAGEIF`, `COUNTIFS`, `SUMIFS`, `AVERAGEIFS`, `MAXIFS` and `MINIFS`, with the reason (the first two were checked in Excel, the rest are the same family). A constant is still fine as a criterion and in `SUMPRODUCT`, `MATCH` and `INDEX`. A parser change, noted in the title of the pull request.
- **What a program found hard in Forge (roadmap T1-PIPELINE-FINDINGS, from Sentinel's first run).** `docs/MCP.md` said a Monte Carlo section needs `enabled: true`; `forge simulate` never reads the key, so the doc now says it is accepted and not read. It also said `forge_functions` lists 173 functions; there are 203, and `tests/doc_counts.rs` now reads `docs/MCP.md` too. A tornado input that cannot move the output used to be listed with a swing of 0 and no reason; the table now says "Changing this input does not change the output: the output's formula does not use it", and the JSON bar carries a `note` field only in that case.

## [10.0.0-rc.26] - 2026-11-15

Five wrong answers fixed after 40 formulas were checked in real Excel, array constants such as `{1,2,3}` in formulas (ADR-064), and terminal output that reads better: `forge variance` shows `n/m` for a percentage that means nothing, and `calculate`, `scenarios` and `compare` show numbers with their `format:`. These are wrong answers fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-11-29.

### Fixed
- **`main` did not build** after #222 (array constants) and #223 (shared columns) were merged one after the other: each passed on its own, but #223 made `Value::Array` hold a shared list and #222 built one from a plain list. The constant is now made into a shared list, and three date helpers moved from `evaluator/mod.rs` into `evaluator/serial.rs`, which the two together had taken past 1,500 lines.

Checked in Excel (iPhone app) on 2026-11-14: 40 formulas where Gnumeric, LibreOffice, Forge or Microsoft's documentation disagreed. Excel agreed with Forge on 35, most of them against one or both engines (`ACCRINT`, `PRICEDISC` and `YIELDDISC` over month ends and long spans, `VDB` from a fractional period, `SYD` past the life, `TIME` with fractions, `HOUR`/`MINUTE`/`SECOND` of a negative number, `WEEKNUM` types 11 to 17 at year end, `DATEDIF "YD"` from 29 February, `DATE` with a year below 1900, `VALUE("TRUE")`, `ADDRESS` with abs_num 5, `MAXIFS` with a pattern over numbers) and once against Microsoft's own `VDB` page (Excel gives 540.19, not 396.31). All 40 are in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_excel_checks.yaml` with Excel's values; `validation/sweep/known.py` cites them, and the two rules Forge now matches the engines on are gone. The five where Excel differed were wrong answers in Forge:
- **`DB` with a fraction of a month** used the fraction: `DB(10000, 1000, 5, 1, 6.5)` was 1998.75. Excel cuts the month (1845), as LibreOffice does.
- **`LARGE` with a fractional k** cut k down: `LARGE({5,1,3,3,8}, 1.5)` was 8. Excel rounds it up (5), as both engines do, while `SMALL` cuts it (`SMALL(.., 1.5)` is 1, which Forge gave): `LARGE(k)` is `SMALL(n + 1 - k)`.
- **`MATCH` with a match type other than 1, 0 or -1** was an error. Excel counts it by its sign: `MATCH(3, {1,3,5}, 2)` is 2.
- **`MATCH` with match type 1 or -1 on values not sorted that way** searched from the first: `MATCH(0, {1,3,5,7,9,11}, -1)` was 1. Excel halves the range, as both engines do, and finds 6. On sorted values the answer is the same, the last of repeated values included.
- **`TEXT` of a negative number that rounds to zero** kept the minus: `TEXT(-0.004, "0.00")` was `-0.00`. Excel writes `0.00`, as both engines do.

These are wrong answers fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries them. T1-YEARFRAC-FEB is done; T1-EXCEL-CHECKS keeps what is still open.

### Added
- **Array constants in formulas (ADR-064)**: a row `{1,2,3}` or a column `{1;2;3}` of numbers, texts and `TRUE`/`FALSE`, as in Excel: `=LARGE(x, {1,2,3})`, `=MATCH(v, {"a";"b";"c"}, 0)`, `=SUMPRODUCT({1,2,3}, {4,5,6})`. A constant is a whole list, as a column given to an aggregate is (ADR-058); `SUM({1,2,3} * 2)` is 12, and in a row formula the constant is the same list on every row. `forge export` writes it as Excel does, and `forge import` keeps a workbook formula with one, where it imported the formula as its values ("unsupported character '{'"). A constant with rows and columns (`{1,2;3,4}`) is refused, as Forge has no two-dimensional values, and so is an error value such as `#N/A` in the braces (`NA()` gives it); each refusal says why. `examples/capex-plan` types the IRS's 7-year MACRS rates as a constant. 20 cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/functions/array_constants.yaml` (three judged by LibreOffice, each with its reason), and `tests/array_constants.rs` for the refusals and the import.
- **`forge variance` shows `n/m` for a percentage that means nothing** (roadmap T2-VARIANCE-NM): when the budget is zero, or the budget and the actual are on opposite sides of zero. On examples/bagel the year's profit went from 786.32 to -28,313.87 and the report said -3,700.8%; it now says `n/m` beside the 29,100.19 variance, and a line under the table says why. Such a row stays an alert (a change across zero is larger than any share of the budget; a change from a zero budget is now an alert too, where its percentage was printed as 0.0%). JSON, YAML and the MCP tool keep `variance_pct` and add `pct_meaningful`; the Excel report writes `n/m`. Forge Studio's variance cards show `n/m` too. `tests/output_readability.rs`.
- **The terminal shows numbers with their `format:`** (ADR-061, roadmap T2-CLI-FORMAT), as `forge report` and Forge Studio do. `forge calculate` prints `year.margin = 0.2%  (0.00203274189215848)`: the formatted value, then the full one, which is what the file holds; a column with a format prints its formatted values, separated by `;` because a format may write commas, with the full values under them. `forge scenarios` and the table and Markdown of `forge compare` print the formatted value. CSV, JSON, the MCP tools and the file are unchanged. The README's rolling-forecast lines show the new output. `tests/output_readability.rs` checks the printed text against `forge report`'s.
- **The README's Quick start gives the commands to download, check and unpack the Linux release archive** (roadmap T2-LINUX-INSTALL), which it named only as "the archive for your system". `tests/readme_commands.rs` checks that every archive it names is one the release workflow builds.

### Changed
- **A row formula whose result has several values** says so ("the result has 3 values, and a row formula gives one value a row. Aggregate it with a function such as SUM or INDEX"), where it said "result is not a number".

## [10.0.0-rc.25] - 2026-11-14

A release's `checksums.txt` now verifies with a plain `sha256sum -c`, `forge calculate` no longer slows to a crawl on models with thousands of scalars, and the release binaries are no longer packed with UPX. No wrong answer is fixed, so the stable date in docs/STABILITY.md stays where rc.24 put it: no earlier than 2026-11-28.

### Fixed
- **`forge calculate` took time growing with the cube of the number of scalars.** Every formula laid each short name of the model (`price` for `summary.price`) over the context and checked it against every name laid before it, so 1,000 scalars took 0.3 s, 4,000 took 14 s and 26,600 did not finish in 10 minutes. Short names are now looked up when a formula reads one, and only the few that hide a name of their own (a top-level `price` read from section `summary`) are laid over the context. 26,600 scalars take 0.3 s, a chain of 26,600 formulas each reading the one before as well. A model of many small sections (1,000 sections of 10) was quadratic too: each section's short names were found by going through every scalar. `VLOOKUP`, `HLOOKUP` and `OFFSET` in a row formula copied the whole context (every scalar and table) for each row: 8,000 rows of `VLOOKUP` took 2.8 s and now take 0.6 s. What is left there, a column copied for each row, is roadmap T1-COLUMN-COPIES. Results are the same: the Gnumeric suite, the unit tests and the generated cases give what they gave. `tests/scalar_scaling.rs` checks that 10,000 scalars take under 2 s in a release build; new tests in `scalar_scope_tests.rs` check that a LAMBDA or LET name, or a table column, still hides a short name, and that INDIRECT reads one. Roadmap T1-SCALAR-SCALING.
- **`sha256sum -c checksums.txt` failed next to a downloaded release archive.** The release's `checksums.txt` named each archive with the build job's folder in front (`forge-aarch64-apple-darwin/forge-aarch64-apple-darwin.tar.gz`), while the archive is published without it, so the check said "No such file or directory" although every hash was right (rc.24 and earlier). `scripts/release-checksums.sh` writes the published names now; the release workflow checks the file against the archives before publishing and again against the assets downloaded after publishing, so a wrong `checksums.txt` fails the release run. `forge update` and the Homebrew formula script read both forms. The README's Quick start shows the check, and says that `forge calculate` writes its results into the model and keeps a `.bak` copy.

### Changed
- **A row formula that reads a whole column no longer copies it for every row** (roadmap T1-COLUMN-COPIES). `Value::Array` holds its values in an `Arc`, and the columns of the calculation are kept the same way, so reading `d.k` whole in each row is a reference count, not a copy; `collect_values_as_vec`, which `SUMIF`, `COUNTIF`, `AVERAGEIF`, `FILTER` and others read their ranges with, returns the shared column too. 16,000 rows (release): `=INDEX(d.v, MATCH(k, d.k, 0))` 5.2 s → 0.5 s, `VLOOKUP` 2.9 s → 0.5 s, `XLOOKUP` 5.5 s → 0.6 s, `=SUM(d.v) + k` 2.9 s → 0.9 s, `SUMIF` 6.3 s → 1.2 s. Each row still searches the column, as in Excel, so the time still grows with the square of the rows. Results are unchanged: the files Forge writes for these models and for 24,400 generated cases are byte for byte the same as before, and the Gnumeric suite passes as before. `tests/column_copies.rs` (run in release by the Validation workflow, with `scalar_scaling`) fails on the old code.
- **Test counts brought up to date** in README.md, VALIDATION.md and validation/gnumeric/README.md: 1,871 tests and 2 doctests; 2,024 Gnumeric cases in 57 files, 150 of them judged by LibreOffice. ADR-063 is marked Accepted.
- **Release binaries are no longer packed with UPX.** The Linux x86_64 and Windows builds were; packed executables are a common trigger for antivirus false positives (Windows Defender and corporate scanners quarantine them), they unpack into memory on every start, which a command an AI agent may call hundreds of times pays for, and the step ran with `|| true`, so a failed packing shipped an unpacked binary without a word. The `.tar.gz` and `.zip` archives are compressed already, so the download grows less than the file does (the Linux binary compresses to 5.5 MB with gzip alone). Every platform now ships the binary as built. `make build-compressed` is still there for anyone who wants a packed binary of their own.
- **The generated cases run as one model a batch** (`validation/sweep/generate.py`), no longer split into models of 1,000 for the slowdown above: the weekly 26,600 cases take 5 s with both engines, against 27 s.

## [10.0.0-rc.24] - 2026-11-14

Wrong answers fixed in the lookup, math, text, statistical and financial functions (found by sweeps and by the new generated cases, ADR-062), `NA()` is now the error #N/A, and `forge validate` refuses a table with columns of different lengths. Forge Studio gets a welcome card. These are wrong answers, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-11-28.

### Added
- **A count or a command in the docs that is wrong now fails a test** (roadmap T1-CLAIMS). `tests/doc_counts.rs` compares every function count written in the README, `docs/FUNCTIONS.md`, `docs/FEATURES.md`, the help text, the MCP instructions and Cargo.toml with the registry, `docs/FUNCTIONS.md`'s category headings with the code, and the test count with the code (the docs say "over 1,850 tests", in steps of 50). `validation/gnumeric/tests/doc_counts.rs` compares the case, LibreOffice case and file counts in the README, VALIDATION.md and the suite's README with what the suite loads. `tests/readme_commands.rs` runs each `$ forge ...` block in the README and checks that every line shown is a line printed; `forge simulate --seek` takes over a minute in a debug build, so it is skipped there and run in release by the Validation workflow. A mutation check (a changed `397.04`) fails it. They found two stale claims at once: `docs/FEATURES.md` and the instructions the MCP server gives an agent said 202 functions, and there are 203. The case counts were stale as well: 2,024 cases (1,873 pass, 1 skipped with Gnumeric on rc.24), 150 for LibreOffice, 57 files.
- **Generated cases against Gnumeric and LibreOffice in CI (ADR-062).** `validation/sweep/generate.py` draws each of 133 functions' arguments from a seed, toward the values where spreadsheets go wrong. A pull request runs seed 1 (2,660 cases, 3 s), and the Monday run of the Validation workflow a new seed every week (26,600 cases, 22 s). A case where Forge disagrees with the engines fails, unless `validation/sweep/known.py` covers it: 21 rules, one class of input each, with the reason and where it is tracked. A failure prints the command that replays it.

### Fixed
Wrong answers found by the generated cases (seeds 1 to 10 and 202646). Each now gives what both engines give, or LibreOffice where Gnumeric cannot judge, or the exact value where every engine loses digits. Cases are in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_generated.yaml`, and the Forge-only answers have unit tests.
- **`NA()` was an empty value**, the same as an empty cell (ADR-059). So `IFNA(MATCH(...), x)` and `ISNA(MATCH(...))`, the usual test for "not found", failed instead of catching it; `IFERROR` and `ISERROR` took an empty cell for an error; and `ISBLANK(NA())` was TRUE. `NA()` is now the error `#N/A`, which a lookup that finds nothing also gives (`MATCH`, `XMATCH`, `XLOOKUP`, `VLOOKUP`, `HLOOKUP`, `RANK.EQ`, `IFS`, `SWITCH`). `IFNA` and `ISNA` catch it and no other error. The `IS` functions never fail. `TYPE` of an empty cell is 1. A formula that reads `NA()` now fails, as in Excel, where before it read nothing.
- **`CORREL`, `SLOPE`, `INTERCEPT`, `RSQ`, `FORECAST`, `COVARIANCE.P` and `COVARIANCE.S` failed when a column had an empty cell**: it was dropped from one column only. A pair with an empty cell is left out, as in Excel.
- **`DATEDIF` with the start after the end gave a number**; it is #NUM!.
- **`HOUR`, `MINUTE` and `SECOND` of a negative number gave a negative result**, and a time that rounds up to midnight gave hour 24. A negative number is #NUM!; midnight is the next day's 0.
- **`PMT`, `PV`, `FV`, `IPMT`, `PPMT`, `RATE` and `NPER` lost digits at a rate near 0**: `PMT(1e-9, 24, -2.5, -0.227)` was 0.11362885, where it is 0.11362886. `RATE` stopped short of the root with a large loan (`RATE(276, -100, 1E15)` was -0.0725; it is -0.0951). `CUMPRINC` lost digits at a rate of 50% (7E-6 relative) and is now a closed form, exact to 15 digits. `NPER` is exact to 15 digits where both engines lose some.
- **`NORM.DIST` far in a tail was an error**; the density there is 0.
- **`DB` gave nonsense past the life** (-6.7E+36) and 0 for a life of 0. It is #NUM! outside LibreOffice's range: salvage from 0 to the cost, a period up to the life + 1, a month from 1 to 12. **`DDB` with a fractional period**, or past the salvage, used a loop over whole periods; it uses the closed form `VDB` and both engines use. **`VDB` with a salvage above the cost** is #NUM!, as both engines give.
- **`RRI` refused a negative ratio over one period**: `RRI(1, 100, -50)` is -1.5, as `POWER` gives.
- **`COUNTIF` and the other criteria did not compare text with `>`, `<`, `>=` and `<=`**: `COUNTIF(names, ">c")` was 0. They compare text, ignoring case. A pattern (`*`, `?`) no longer matches an empty text, which the workbook holds as an empty cell, and neither a pattern nor a comparison with text matches a date, which the workbook holds as a number.
- **`RANK.EQ` counted 1E-12 as equal to 0**, one place early; it counts the values above, exactly.
- **`MOD` computed n - d·INT(n/d) in floating point**: `MOD(7.3, 0.1)` was -5.8E-16, negative for a positive divisor, and `MOD(1E15, 1E-12)` was 0.0068, larger than the divisor. It is the exact remainder, with the divisor's sign.
- **A number as text rounded its binary value to 15 digits**: -985667.1985084685 was written ...468, not ...469. It rounds the shortest decimal form, as LibreOffice does.

These are wrong answers fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries them.

### Fixed
- **`forge validate` accepted a table whose columns have different lengths.** A table with a 3-row and a 2-row column passed validation ("All tables are valid"); the error came only when a formula read the table. `validate` now refuses it, naming the table, the column and both lengths, and exits 1. `forge export` still writes it, as columns of different lengths are ranges in a workbook (the Gnumeric suite's fixtures use them), but now warns with the same message. Roadmap T1-VALIDATE-GAPS; `tests/validate_gaps.rs`.
- **A misspelled scalar name was reported as `Unknown column: a.price`, with no suggestion.** It now says `Unknown name: a.price. Did you mean 'a.prce'?` when the name is close to one in the model, as `.asimov/project.yaml` requires. A missing column of a table that exists still says `Unknown column`.
- **`forge calculate` could not write a whole number of about 9.2e18 or more back to the model.** It wrote it as digits alone (`100000000000000000000`), which YAML reads as an integer too large to hold, so the written file did not parse and Forge refused to write any result ("results not written, the file is unchanged"). A model with `x: 10000000000` and `=x * x` failed this way; so did any result such as 6.02e23. A whole number from 1e15 on is written with a decimal point now (`100000000000000000000.0`) and reads back as the same number. No answer was wrong: the calculation refused rather than writing. Found by the new property tests; `tests/large_numbers.rs` and a wider `numbers_read_back_exactly` check it.
- **The CI Test job passed when tests failed.** It ran `cargo test | tee test-output.txt` in GitHub's default shell, `bash -e`, without `pipefail`, so the step's status was `tee`'s. It runs with `shell: bash` now, which sets `pipefail`.
- **The R formula suite could not fail.** `validation/r/validators/r/run_all.sh` ran each validator through `tee`, so it tested `tee`'s exit status, not R's: on rc.23 it printed 121 passed and 15 failed, then "All validators passed", exit 0. It stops at the first failing validator now (`set -o pipefail`), and the check was shown to fail with a deliberately wrong value. The 15 failures were `DATE` and `WORKDAY` checks: since ADR-060 Forge prints a date as text and the R helper expected a serial number; it reads both. 136 of 136 checks pass.
- **Forge did not build on Rust 1.97.0** (8 uses of `Option::map_or_default`, stable only since 1.98, and 14 more in the validation suites). They are `map(..).unwrap_or_default()` now.

### Added
- **`forge mcp --root DIR` and `forge serve --root DIR`** (roadmap T1-MCP-ROOT): every file path a tool or request names must be inside `DIR`. A path is resolved first (`..` and symbolic links followed) and refused unless it is inside, for reading (`file_path`, `yaml_path`, `budget_path`, `actual_path`, an import's workbook) and for writing (an export's workbook, an import's model, results written back). A model that includes a file outside `DIR`, directly, through another include or in any document of a multi-document file, is refused before that file is opened: the parser itself runs the check, at exactly the paths it would read. A link to a file that does not exist yet is refused, as writing it would create the file it points to. A relative path is relative to `DIR`, and the MCP server's instructions tell the agent the folder. Without `--root` nothing changes. `docs/MCP.md` and `SECURITY.md` describe it, including its limit: it checks what requests name, before the command runs, and is not a sandbox. `src/mcp/root_tests.rs`, `src/api/root_tests.rs`.
- **Property tests** (`tests/properties.rs`, the `proptest` crate, roadmap T1-PROPERTIES) for four rules that must hold for any model, on generated models with numbers from 1e-6 to 1e12, negative and zero, and empty cells: `format:` never changes a value; `forge export` then `forge import` gives the same numbers; calculating gives the same output every time, and calculating the file Forge wrote changes nothing; a seeded simulation repeats exactly. A pull request runs about 150 cases from a fixed seed (`PROPTEST_RNG_SEED=1`, about 3 seconds); the weekly workflow runs 1,000 per property from that week's seed, printed so a failure can be replayed.
- **Forge Studio greets a first visit with a welcome card** (ADR-056 addendum): what the page is (a real model, calculated in the browser, nothing sent anywhere) and three tips (change an input, open a formula with Edit, where Grid, YAML and Files lead). Got it removes it for good on that device (a flag in `localStorage`); "Show the welcome tips again" on the Files tab brings it back. It replaces the guided tour parked on 2026-11-13, now that newcomers are trying Studio. `studio/tests/welcome.mjs` covers it.
- **`rust-version = "1.97"`** in Cargo.toml, the same `msrv` in `clippy.toml` (clippy stops suggesting newer APIs), and a CI job that builds on the declared version.

### Changed
- **`forge --help` says Release candidate, not Beta**, as docs/cli/README.md does.
- **Bug-fix rule:** a failing test now comes first for every bug fix (an error, a crash, a wrong message), not only a wrong answer: `.asimov/project.yaml`, CONTRIBUTING.md and the pull request template.
- **Stale numbers and labels corrected:** 1,826 tests and 2 doctests, 1,931 Gnumeric cases (139 by LibreOffice, 55 files, 14 checked against Forge only), `VALIDATION.md`'s CI wording (beta Rust runs weekly, not on every push), the function count in a Cargo.toml comment (203), and the goal-seek block in the README (the command prints 397.04 and 80,000.00).
- **Author lines name Claude plainly** in Cargo.toml (`Claude (Anthropic)`) and 23 ADRs, without the self-given title.
- A committed `__pycache__` file is removed and ignored.

### Removed
