- **Three ADRs about work that is not Forge's:** ADR-026 (FPGA acceleration for high-frequency trading), ADR-028 (crystal analysis for DANEEL, never built) and ADR-030 (a go-to-market plan from the Elastic-2.0 period). All three were deferred, not scheduled or superseded. ADR-031 keeps the licence history. No code or behaviour changes.

### Fixed
Wrong answers found by sweeping the statistical, lookup, math and text functions (about 5,900 generated cases) against Gnumeric and LibreOffice; each now gives what both engines give, or what LibreOffice and Excel's documentation give where Gnumeric differs. Cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_text_lookup_sweep.yaml`; the sweep is in `validation/sweep/`.
- **Wildcards were read as plain text.** `*` (any run of characters), `?` (one character) and `~` (the next character itself) now work in the criteria of `COUNTIF`, `SUMIF`, `AVERAGEIF`, `COUNTIFS`, `SUMIFS`, `AVERAGEIFS`, `MAXIFS` and `MINIFS` (also after `=` and `<>`), in `MATCH` and `VLOOKUP` with an exact match, in `SEARCH`, and in `XMATCH` and `XLOOKUP` with match mode 2. `COUNTIF(names, "b*")` counted only a cell holding `b*`. A pattern matches text only, never a number or an empty cell, as in Excel.
- **`XLOOKUP` ignored its search mode**: `-1` (from the last) found the first match. `XMATCH` and `XLOOKUP` now share one search, and the binary search modes 2 and -2 are read (on sorted values they find what a search from the first finds) instead of being errors.
- **`FIND` and `SEARCH` counted bytes, not characters**: `FIND("γ", "αβγ")` was 5, and a start inside a character such as `FIND("β", "αβγ", 2)` crashed Forge. A start below 1 is #VALUE!, as it was not.
- **`REPLACE` with a start past the end of the text crashed Forge**; it adds the new text at the end, as Excel does.
- **`LEFT`, `RIGHT`, `MID`, `REPT`, `REPLACE` and `SUBSTITUTE` accepted a negative count or a position below 1** and gave text; they are #VALUE!. `REPT` longer than Excel's 32,767 characters is #VALUE! too.
- **`ROUND`, `ROUNDUP`, `ROUNDDOWN` and `TRUNC` rounded the binary value**: `ROUND(1.005, 2)` was 1.00, `ROUNDDOWN(4.35, 2)` 4.34 and `ROUNDUP(0.1 + 0.2, 1)` 0.4. They round the value to 15 significant digits first, as Excel does (1.01, 4.35, 0.3). `FLOOR`, `CEILING` and `MROUND` do the same with their quotient (`FLOOR(0.3, 0.1)` was 0.2).
- **`FLOOR` and `CEILING` of a positive number with a negative significance gave a number**; it is #NUM!. `FLOOR` with a significance of 0 is #DIV/0! (it was 0); `CEILING`'s is still 0, as in Excel.
- **A number turned into text kept 17 digits**: `CONCAT(0.1 + 0.2)` was `0.30000000000000004` and `LEN(2/3)` 18. Excel and LibreOffice keep 15 significant digits (`0.3`, 17); a whole number below 1E+16 is written in full and a larger one as `1E+20`.
- **`CONCAT` of a column wrote `[a, , b, c]`**; it joins the values (`abc`).
- **`VALUE` read `inf` and `nan` as numbers, and `1,2,3` as 123**, and refused `50%`, `$1,000`, `(5)`, `5-`, `2024-01-15` and `12:30`. It now reads what Excel reads in an English locale and nothing else.
- **`MATCH` with a fractional match type** is cut to a whole number, as LibreOffice does (0.5 is an exact match).

These are wrong answers fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries them.

## [10.0.0-rc.23] - 2026-11-14

Eight wrong answers in date and financial functions fixed, plus a Studio fix for the decimal comma. They are wrong answers, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-11-28.

### Fixed
Wrong answers found by sweeping the 45 date and financial functions (7,959 generated cases) against Gnumeric and LibreOffice; each now gives what both engines give, or what Excel's documentation says where they differ. Cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_date_finance_sweep.yaml`; the sweep is in `validation/sweep/`.
- **`NETWORKDAYS` and `WORKDAY` ignored their holidays.** The third argument was accepted and dropped, so a range with holidays counted them as working days. A date, a day number or a column of them is read now; empty cells are skipped.
- **`WEEKDAY` with return type 11 to 17 answered as type 1**, and any other unknown type did too. Types 11 to 17 start the week on Monday to Sunday; other types are #NUM!.
- **`DATEDIF(start, end, "YM")` gave 0 instead of 11** when the end was in the same calendar month as the start, on an earlier day, a year later (2023-01-30 to 2024-01-01).
- **`DATE` with a year from 0 to 1899 used that year**: `DATE(99, 1, 1)` was the year 99. Excel adds 1900 (Microsoft's example: `DATE(108, 1, 2)` is 2008-01-02). A year below 0 or above 9999, or a date after 9999-12-31, is #NUM!.
- **`TIME` past 24 hours gave more than a day** (`TIME(25, 0, 0)` was 1.0417): it starts the day again, as Excel does (0.0417). A negative time is #NUM!.
- **`EFFECT` and `NOMINAL` used a fractional number of periods per year**; Excel truncates it (`EFFECT(0.05, 2.7)` is `EFFECT(0.05, 2)`).
- **`IPMT` and `PPMT` at a rate of 0 accepted a period outside 1 to nper**; it is #NUM!, as at any other rate.
- **`DDB` gave 0 for a period after the life**; it is #NUM!.

These are wrong answers fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries them.

### Fixed
- **Forge Studio read a decimal comma as a thousands separator**: `0,03` typed on a card was 3, not 0.03, with no warning, on a phone set to French. A comma is now a decimal mark (`0,03`, `3,5`, `4,5 %`) except in the shapes that are thousands (`1,250` is 1250 as before, `1,234,567`, `1.234,56` and `1 234,5` with a space or no-break space). A group that does not fit, such as `1,2,3,4`, is refused instead of read. `studio/tests/format.mjs` and `layout.mjs` cover it.

## [10.0.0-rc.22] - 2026-11-13

`YEARFRAC` with basis 0, from the last day of February to a 31st, gives the answer of Gnumeric, LibreOffice and the algorithm Microsoft confirmed for Excel (not yet checked in Excel itself), and the command line and the MCP tools agree on dates. The fix is to a wrong answer, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again from this release: no earlier than 2026-11-27.

### Added
- **Studio's `formats.mjs` test** checks that the bagel's and the forecast's formatted numbers on the cards and in the grid are the text Forge gives, with the values unchanged. It runs in CI.

### Changed
- **`examples/rolling-forecast` uses `format:`** (ADR-061): revenue with thousands separators, variances and the volume, mix and price split with a shortfall in parentheses, `share_of_year` as `0.0%`. No number changes: `forge calculate` gives the same output as before, byte for byte, and `tests/demo_examples.rs` passes. A period column does not take a `format:` yet (`months.month.format` is refused), so the months keep their ISO dates.
- **`examples/rolling-forecast` has `format:` on more of its numbers**: units (`#,##0`), prices (`#,##0.00`), growth rates (`0.0%`), the budget by month, the year to date, the actuals so far and the last actual (`d mmm yyyy`). Its numbers are the same.

### Fixed
- **The CI job that regenerates `docs/FUNCTIONS.md` failed when `main` moved while it ran** (after a release). It runs one at a time and rebases and retries its push now.
- **`YEARFRAC` with basis 0, from the last day of February to a 31st, was one day short.** `YEARFRAC(DATE(2026,2,28), DATE(2026,12,31), 0)` gave 0.8333 (300/360, the NASD rule) and gives 0.8361 (301/360) now, as Gnumeric and LibreOffice do, and as the algorithm Microsoft confirmed matches Excel's `YEARFRAC` (D. A. Wheeler, OASIS office-formula list, 2008). Microsoft's `YEARFRAC` page warns that basis 0 "may return an incorrect result" for this start. No other pair of dates changes. `PRICEDISC`, `YIELDDISC` and `ACCRINT` keep the NASD count: an Excel check of `PRICEDISC` on month ends (2026-11-06) agrees with it, and nothing published says Excel's security functions share `YEARFRAC`'s exception. A wrong answer fixed, so the two weeks before a stable release start again from the release that carries it (docs/STABILITY.md).
- **`forge sensitivity` with one date input did not refuse it.** It printed an error on every row, then "Sensitivity analysis complete", and exited 0. It stops with the message the two-input form and the other analysis commands give, and a failing exit code (ADR-060).
- **The MCP tools `forge_compare` and `forge_scenarios` gave a date as its day number** (`46326`), where `forge_calculate` and `forge compare --format json` give `"2026-10-31"`. They give the text now (ADR-060).
- **Help and schema text said 5.4.0 was only for a date as a scalar.** `forge schema`, `forge upgrade --help`, the refusal when `forge upgrade` would go back to an earlier format, and the MCP `forge_schema` description name `format:` (ADR-061) and the empty cell (5.3.0) too.

## [10.0.0-rc.21] - 2026-11-13

A date can be a scalar (ADR-060) and `format:` holds an Excel number format beside `unit:` (ADR-061); both are in model format 5.4.0. Neither fixes a wrong answer, so the stable date stays no earlier than 2026-11-27.

### Changed
- **Forge Studio is at <https://forge.mollendorff.ai/>.** The old address, `mollendorff-ai.github.io/forge/`, redirects there. Models saved in the browser at the old address stay there: save them to Files and open them at the new one. The links in the READMEs point to the new address; the check after each release opens whatever address Pages reports.

### Added
- **`format:` beside `unit:` (ADR-061, model format 5.4.0).** An Excel number-format code that says how a number is shown, never what it is: `price: {value: 16, format: "$#,##0.00"}`, a data column `{value: [...], format: "0%"}`, a formula column `{formula: "=...", format: "#,##0;(#,##0)"}`, a date code on a date (`"mmm yyyy"`). `forge export` writes it into every cell of the scalar or column, and `forge import` reads it back where a column's cells share one (a code Forge does not read, or cells that differ, give a warning and no format). `forge report`'s table and Markdown show the formatted text, its CSV and JSON the value; JSON from `calculate` (MCP, the REST API, forge-wasm) has a `formatted` map beside the values, which do not change; Forge Studio shows what Forge formats and adds no rule of its own where there is a format. Forge accepts only a code it writes as Excel does, measured in Gnumeric 1.12 and LibreOffice 26.2 on 25 codes: a code it does not read (a fourth section, a condition such as `[>1000]`, a locale tag), a date code on a number and a number code on a date are refused with the reason. A model without `format:` exports the workbook it exported before. `examples/bagel` formats its price, its season and its dozens (a scalar, a data column and a formula column) and its margin, and is 5.4.0 now; its numbers do not change. 90 cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_number_formats.yaml` and `tests/number_formats.rs` check it.
- **A scalar can be a date (ADR-060, model format 5.4.0).** `last_actual: 2026-10-31`, or `value: 2026-10-31` in the mapping form, is a date, read as a cell of a date column is (ADR-054): its day number where a formula reads a number (`=YEAR(last_actual)`, `=last_actual - start`, `">" & last_actual` in a criterion), its text where a formula reads text (`=LEFT(last_actual, 4)` is `2026`, `ISNUMBER` is FALSE). A formula scalar whose value is a date (`=EOMONTH(last_actual, 12)`) is a date too. Dates print as dates in `calculate`, `report`, `compare`, `audit` and Forge Studio (which gives a date input a date field and no slider), are text in JSON (`"2026-10-31"`), and are written back as dates in a 5.4.0 file (as the day number in an older one). `forge export` writes a date cell with the format `yyyy-mm-dd`, set by Forge because Gnumeric and LibreOffice guess the format of a formula cell differently (measured); `forge import` reads a date cell back as a date and writes 5.4.0. A scenario can give a date input another date. A month (`2026-10`), a date before 1900-03-01, an impossible date and `unit:` on a date are refused with what to write; formats before 5.4.0 refuse a date as a scalar and name 5.4.0; `forge upgrade --to 5.4.0` and `forge schema v5.4` exist. `sensitivity`, `goal-seek`, `break-even`, `tornado` and `probability-seek` refuse a date input, and `variance` lists a date among the scalars it does not compare. `examples/rolling-forecast` writes `last_actual: 2026-10-31`; its numbers do not change. 19 cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_scalar_dates.yaml` and `tests/scalar_dates.rs` check the ADR's table.
- **Forge Studio opens `examples/capex-plan` and `examples/rolling-forecast`** (nine example sets, from seven). The page's tests run on both: the numbers on screen, the grid, the formula edits and the suggestions are checked against what Forge returns for them.

### Changed
- **`TEXT` reads the number formats finance uses** (ADR-061): up to three sections, `positive;negative;zero`, so `TEXT(x, "#,##0;(#,##0)")` is `(1,235)` for -1234.567; a colour in brackets (`[Red]`, no text); `_x` as a space; `*x` as nothing; and trailing commas, each dividing by 1,000 (`0.0,,"M"`). Each was an error and gives what Gnumeric and LibreOffice give now; that changes a model only where an `IFERROR` caught the error.
- **A formula column keeps its metadata.** The long form `{formula: "=...", unit: ..., notes: ...}` dropped its `unit:` and `notes:` when the file was read; they are kept now, and the exported workbook shows them as the column's note, as for a data column.
- **`DATE` gives a date**, as `EDATE`, `EOMONTH`, `WORKDAY` and `TODAY` do (ADR-060). A column or scalar whose formula is a `DATE` is a date: shown as one and exported as a date cell, where it was the day number. Where a number is read nothing changes. Where text is read it is the date's text, as for a date column: `LEFT(DATE(2026, 1, 1), 4)` is `2026` (was `4605`), and `ISNUMBER` of it is FALSE. No example, test or validation case read a `DATE` as text. `forge import` writes a workbook's `=LEFT(DATE(...), 4)` as its value, with a warning, since Excel gives `4605`. Two formulas over a column that holds dates as text (`n/a` among them) are now exported live instead of as values, because the date is compared as its text, which Excel finds equal too: `maturity = DATE(2026, 1, 31)` and `MATCH(DATE(2026, 12, 1), loans.maturity, 0)` (checked in Gnumeric by the existing cases).

### Fixed
- **Forge Studio did not suggest `TRUE` and `FALSE` inside a formula**, only `TRUE(` and `FALSE(`, so the `TRUE` of `=SUMIFS(months.revenue, months.is_actual, TRUE)` in the rolling forecast could not be completed. Found by running the suggestion tests on that example.

## [10.0.0-rc.20] - 2026-11-13

A table column can aggregate a column, of another table or its own (ADR-058), and a column of numbers can hold an empty cell (ADR-059, model format 5.3.0), which does what Excel does with one; `COUNTBLANK` makes 203 functions. Two wrong answers in `forge import` are fixed: an empty cell came in as 0, and a range where Forge reads the row's value came in as a formula with another answer. By docs/STABILITY.md those fixes start the two weeks before a stable release again: from rc.20, no earlier than 2026-11-27. The README and the longer docs are rewritten and shorter.

### Added
- **An empty cell in a column of numbers (ADR-059, model format 5.3.0).** `null` in a column of numbers is an empty cell, as a month that has no actual yet: `actual: [985, 1032, null]`. It does what Excel does with one, as measured in Gnumeric 1.12 and LibreOffice 26.2, which agree on every case: aggregates skip it and count a real 0 (`AVERAGE` of 10, empty, 30, empty, 0 is 13.33); a formula reads it as 0, `""` or FALSE, so `blank + 1` is 1 and `blank = 0` and `blank = ""` are TRUE; `ISBLANK` is TRUE and `ISNUMBER` FALSE; a formula that reads one gives 0, never an empty cell. `COUNTBLANK` is new (203 functions). `MAX`, `MIN` and `SUM` of only empty cells are 0, as in Excel (they were an error and -0). `NPV`, `IRR` and `MIRR` skip an empty cell, as Excel does, which moves the later cash flows a period earlier; `forge audit` warns when one reads a column with one, and `forge functions` says so. `XNPV` and `XIRR` leave out the cash flow with its date (they stopped with "values and dates must have same length"). `forge calculate` keeps the nulls, the JSON output shows `null`, `forge export` writes an empty cell, `forge import` reads one back, and `forge upgrade --to 5.3.0` sets the format. Formats 5.1.0 and 5.2.0 refuse a null and name 5.3.0. A model without null gives the same output as before, byte for byte (checked on every file in `examples/` and `test-data/`). `examples/rolling-forecast` holds null for the months not closed yet, and `is_actual` is `=ISNUMBER(actual_standard_units)`; its numbers do not change. 44 cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_empty_cells.yaml` and unit tests check the ADR's table. Forge Studio's cards skip the empty cells in their sparkline and range.
- **A table column can aggregate a column: of another table, or its own (ADR-058).** In a row formula an aggregate's range is the whole column and its criterion the row's value, as on a summary sheet in Excel. A table of quarters is `revenue: "=SUMIFS(months.revenue, months.fiscal_quarter, quarter)"`, a share of the total is `=revenue / SUM(revenue)`, and `=PERCENTILE.INC(results.npv, p)` takes a different `p` in each row. This used to stop with "aggregations should be in scalars section". One table of functions and argument positions (`whole_column.rs`) is read by the evaluator, the calculation order and `forge export`, which writes a range as an absolute range; `forge import` reads it back. With several arguments `SUM`, `MAX` and the like still work row by row (`=MAX(0, ebit)`), and a row-count mismatch now says how to aggregate the whole column. A column that aggregates itself is circular, and a column calculated row by row through `PREV` cannot be read whole by its own group. `examples/rolling-forecast` has a table of quarters in place of four scalars written by hand, with each quarter's budget, variance and share of the year. `tests/row_aggregates.rs` checks one case per function against values worked by hand, and 50 new cases in `validation/gnumeric/tests/edge/edge_row_aggregates.yaml` check the exported workbook in Gnumeric and LibreOffice.

### Changed
- **Row lookups and conditional aggregates are faster.** `XLOOKUP`, `MATCH`, `XMATCH`, `INDEX` and `ROWS` in a row formula copied the whole model on every row; they now read the column in place (4,000 rows looking up 4,000: 0.79 s before, 0.26 s now, release build). A criterion of `SUMIF`, `COUNTIFS` and the like is read once per call instead of being turned into text for every value (`SUMIFS` in each of 5,000 rows over 5,000 rows: 1.16 s before this change, 0.47 s).
- **`-x` of a 0 is 0, not -0**, as Excel shows it.
- **`forge import` keeps a share of the total as a formula.** `=A2/SUM($A$2:$A$5)` imports as `=n / SUM(n)`; it was imported as its values.

### Fixed
- **Forge Studio offered nothing inside a formula typed without quotes.** After the strip inserts `formula: `, typing `=fi` left an unquoted formula, and the suggestions of rc.19 only read a quoted one. Both now work, and a `#` ends the formula. `studio/tests/yamlformula.mjs` covers the unquoted case.
- **`forge import` turned an empty cell among numbers into 0 without a word.** Excel skips an empty cell in `AVERAGE`, `COUNT`, `MIN` and the like, so a column of 10, empty, 30, empty, 0 has an average of 13.33 in Excel and had 8 in the imported model. An empty cell is now imported as an empty cell (below), and the model gives Excel's 13.33. A wrong answer fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again at the release that carries it.
- **`forge import` gave a different answer for a range in an argument Forge reads row by row.** In a workbook not written by Forge, a column such as `=MAX(0, $B$2:$B$6)` or `=NPV(0.1, $B$2:$B$6)` was imported as `=MAX(0, revenue)` or `=NPV(0.1, revenue)`, which Forge calculates from the row's revenue, not the column's: a plausible number, and a different one. Such a column (and one cell where Forge would read the whole column, `=SUM(B2)`) is now imported as its values, with the reason in the warnings. A workbook written by `forge export` never has one. This is a wrong answer fixed, so by docs/STABILITY.md the two weeks before a stable release start again at the release that carries it.

## [10.0.0-rc.19] - 2026-11-12

Twenty-six new functions (202 in all) and two new examples, `rolling-forecast` and `capex-plan`, and Forge Studio suggests names and functions inside a formula in the YAML editor. No wrong answer was fixed since rc.13, so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md still runs from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Added
- **Forge Studio suggests names and functions inside a formula typed in the YAML editor.** The strip above the editor offered only keys; inside a quoted formula (`formula: "=SU`, or `revenue: "=dozens * re` in a table) it now offers what the cards offer: the names that formula can use, as that formula writes them (a name of its group or table bare, any other as `group.name`, `table.column` or `@file.group.name`), and Forge's functions, taken from Forge and not from a list in the page. The scope comes from where the formula sits in the file, in the main file and in the files it includes. Keys are offered again once the formula is closed. `studio/tests/yamlformula.mjs` types the first three letters of each of the 218 names and functions in the 77 formulas of the examples' model files, at their place in the file, and checks that each is offered and completes to the same word, that nothing is offered after the formula, in a comment or for a plain value, and that the strip in the editor inserts exactly the text.
- **`examples/rolling-forecast`: a rolling forecast for FP&A.** A fiscal year that starts in April, actuals up to October 2026 and a forecast for twelve months after them, two products, a budget looked up month by month, quarters, year to date, the next twelve months, and the variance split into volume, mix and price that add up to it exactly. `tests/demo_examples.rs` checks its numbers against Python, and the exported workbook recalculates to them in Gnumeric. Building it found three gaps, now on the roadmap: a table column cannot aggregate another table (T2-TABLE-AGGREGATES), a number column cannot have an empty cell (T2-MISSING-VALUES), and a scalar cannot be a date (T2-SCALAR-DATES). The README has a paragraph on it.
- 26 functions, 202 in all: `RRI`, `SYD`, `VDB`, `CUMIPMT`, `CUMPRINC`; `SLOPE`, `INTERCEPT`, `RSQ`, `FORECAST`, `FORECAST.LINEAR`, `COVARIANCE.S`, `COVARIANCE.P`; `NORM.DIST`, `NORM.S.DIST`, `NORM.INV`, `NORM.S.INV`; `GEOMEAN`, `PERCENTILE.INC`, `PERCENTILE.EXC`, `QUARTILE.INC`, `QUARTILE.EXC`; `MROUND`, `TEXTJOIN`, `XMATCH`, `WEEKNUM`, `ISOWEEKNUM`. They are checked against Gnumeric, LibreOffice, R and Python (229 cases in `validation/gnumeric`) and export to Excel. Left out on purpose: `TREND` and `GROWTH` (they return arrays); `XMATCH` takes no wildcards and no binary search.
- `examples/capex-plan`: a loan, three depreciation methods and a revenue trend, with a test against Python.

### Changed

- The normal distribution's CDF and PDF no longer go through `statrs`, which is accurate to about 11 digits; Forge's own is accurate to about 15.

## [10.0.0-rc.18] - 2026-11-12

Forge Studio on a phone (the key suggestions sit on the keyboard, Delete takes less room), the validation suites in this repository, and `forge simulate`, `variance`, `sensitivity` and `break-even` printing numbers as the other commands do. No wrong answer was fixed since rc.13, so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md still runs from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Fixed
- **Forge Studio on a phone: the YAML editor's key suggestions were out of sight, and Delete made every card taller.** Typing at the end of a long file makes the page scroll to keep the caret in view, which took the strip of suggestions (above the editor) off the top of the screen, so on an iPhone nothing seemed to be offered. While the editor has the focus the strip is now docked at the bottom of what is visible, on the keyboard (on a wide screen it stays where it was). Delete on the Model tab is a small bin beside the slider, or beside a result's name, instead of a row of its own, so a screen shows about a third more cards. The confirmation still asks before anything is deleted. `studio/tests/keys.mjs` now checks that the strip is docked, sits on a keyboard of 344 px, takes a tap, and goes back when the editor loses the focus (it fails without the change).
- **`LOG(number, base)` works, and `MODE` exists.** `LOG` took one argument, so Excel's `=LOG(8, 2)` stopped with "requires 1 argument(s), got 2"; the base is now optional (10 without it), and a base of 1 or less than 0 is an error as in Excel. `MODE` and `MODE.SNGL` return the most frequent value, the first met when several tie, and an error (Excel's #N/A) when none repeats. `STDEV`, `STDEV.P`, `VAR` and `VAR.P` always worked but were missing from `forge functions` and the MCP list: all six are listed now (176 functions, from 169). 14 new cases in the Gnumeric suite check them against Gnumeric. A new test fails when a function in the list is unknown to `forge export`.

### Added
- **The two validation suites are in this repository** (ADR-057): forge-e2e-gnumeric in `validation/gnumeric` and forge-e2e-r in `validation/r`, with their history. The Validation workflow runs the suites of the commit it builds, so a change to the engine and to a suite is one pull request (#161 needed two) and nothing outside Forge changes what its CI does. Their lint and unit tests run in CI, which forge-e2e-r never had. A new check, Expected values, fails a pull request that changes or removes an expected value of the suites unless its title says `changes expected values`. The Validation workflow also runs on `main` after a merge that changes the engine, `Cargo.toml` or a suite. The two old repositories are to be archived once it runs green there.

### Changed
- **`forge simulate`, `forge variance` and `forge sensitivity` print numbers as `forge report` does.** `simulate` printed `2731555.7601` and `variance` `386827.449538`; they now print `2,731,555.76` and `386,827.45`, and the sensitivity table is as wide as its names and numbers, so its header lines up. `forge sensitivity --format json` prints the numbers at their 15 significant digits for programs that read it (the table is not made for that).
- **`forge break-even` has a header of its own** and no longer says "Goal Seek" under "Break-Even Analysis"; a time horizon of one prints `1 year`.
- **`forge import` says which included files it wrote** beside the model (`Included files: costs.yaml`), in the `included_files` field of the JSON result too.

## [10.0.0-rc.17] - 2026-11-12

The YAML editor in Forge Studio suggests the keys of the model format, Studio downloads 20% less, and `forge scenarios`, `simulate --seek`, `decision-tree` and `real-options` print numbers as the other commands do. A workbook's cached text is stored as text, and CI no longer lets one hung run block the release. The Grid tab, which the rc.16 tag already contained, is now listed under rc.16. The two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md is unchanged, still from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Added
- **Forge Studio suggests the keys of the model format in the YAML editor** (roadmap S12). A strip above the editor offers the keys that fit where the caret is (`value:`, `unit:`, `formula:`, `better:`, `notes:` under a scalar; `file:` and `as:` in an include; `probability:`, `description:` and `scalars:` in a scenario; the sections of the model at the top), and a tap inserts exactly the key. The keys are not a list in the page: they come from the JSON Schema Forge returns for the file's own format version (the `schema` tool, for the version in `_forge_version`), read through its references, alternatives and if/then/else, so they follow the format when it changes. Nothing is offered after a colon, inside a formula or text, or in a comment, and where the schema describes nothing (the sections it leaves to the commands that read them, such as `bootstrap` and `decision_tree`) nothing is offered. `studio/tests/keys.mjs` types the first two letters of each of the 162 documented keys in the examples' 16 files (formats 5.1.0 and 5.2.0) at their place in the file, and checks that each is offered, that only the schema's keys are offered, and that the strip inserts exactly the key; it fails when list items are not understood.

### Changed
- **`forge import` reads every `PREV` back as `forge export` wrote it.** A column whose `PREV` was part of a larger formula (`PREV(closing, loan.principal) * (1 + loan.rate) + x`) came back as `IF(ROW() = 1, ...)`, the same calculation written differently, and one that read two rows up (`PREV(x, 0, 2)`) came back as values. The first rows of the column are lined up with the later ones to give the `PREV`, which is kept only if it gives every first row as the workbook holds it. A running balance built by hand in Excel still comes in as `IF(ROW() = 1, ...)` (roadmap T3-IMPORT-PREV, ADR-053).
- **A formula that gives text keeps that text in an exported workbook.** The workbook stored `TEXT(n, "000")` = `010` as the number 10, and the words `TRUE` and `FALSE` as booleans, in the cached result a viewer shows without recalculating (Quick Look, iOS Files). Excel recalculated and was right; those viewers were not. The text is now stored as text. Adds the `zip` crate to the `excel` feature (already in the build through the workbook writer and reader).
- **Forge Studio downloads 20% less: Forge's WebAssembly is built for size.** A new `wasm` profile (opt-level `s`, otherwise the release profile) builds forge-wasm for the page and for CI. The module goes from 2.32 MB to 1.86 MB gzipped, as GitHub Pages serves it (6.6 MB to 5.4 MB unpacked), with the same speed: on the bagel example, a 10,000-run simulation takes 1.79 s against 1.62 s and calculate and tornado are faster (measured in Node on 2026-11-11). opt-level `z` would save 31% but makes that simulation 64% slower, and `wasm-opt -Oz` on top saves 0.2%, so neither is used. The WebAssembly answers equal the native ones on every case (`forge-wasm/tests/node/compare.mjs`), and CI now fails if the module grows past 2.0 MB gzipped.
- **`forge scenarios`, `forge simulate --seek`, `forge decision-tree` and `forge real-options` print numbers as `forge report`, `goal-seek` and `tornado` do.** They each had their own rule: a scenario's values to six decimals (`786.320362`, `24176.715596`), a probability seek's solution as `18.324083`, and money as `$61500.00` and `$-117000.00`. Values now have two decimals and thousands separators (`786.32`, `24,176.72`), a number under 1 keeps four significant digits (`0.002033`), the seek's solution is `18.32`, and money is `$61,500.00`, with the sign before the dollar sign (`-$117,000.00`). The rule of thumb and the recommendation of `real-options` say `$198,000` and `$4,605`, not `$198000` and `$4605`. JSON, YAML and `--output` files keep the numbers, to 15 significant digits. Nothing the validation suites read changes: they parse JSON, and the one table they read from text (`forge sensitivity`) is not touched. This does not move the stable date (no number changes, only how it is shown).

### Fixed
- **A CI run that hangs on `main` no longer holds up the ones after it, or the release.** The change of #145 gave every push to `main` the same concurrency group, so a run whose job hung (on 2026-11-11 the WebAssembly build of the rc.16 merge sat "in progress" for an hour, a runner problem at GitHub) kept every later run "pending", Auto Release included, and rc.16 was not tagged. Pull requests still cancel their older runs; every other run now has a group of its own. Every CI job also has a time limit (15 to 45 minutes; GitHub's default is six hours), so a hung job fails and can be run again.
- **Auto Release falls back to the API when GitHub refuses the tag push.** For rc.16 GitHub refused `git push` of the tag ("refusing to allow a GitHub App to create or update workflow `.github/workflows/ci.yml` without `workflows` permission") although the commit was already on `main` and rc.14 and rc.15 had gone through. The tag is then made through the API, which makes a ref to a commit GitHub already has.

## [10.0.0-rc.16] - 2026-11-11

Forge Studio works offline and can be added to the Home Screen, and adds and deletes model elements on its cards. No wrong answer was fixed since rc.13, so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md still runs from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Added
- **Forge Studio has a Grid tab: the model as a spreadsheet reads it** (roadmap S11, ADR-056 addendum). Each table is shown as rows and columns, with the column's one formula under its name in the heading, and the scalars as a list with their formulas; the numbers are Forge's, in the scenario that is applied, formatted as on the cards. It is read-only: a tap on a cell or a heading shows its value and formula, and "Show its card" opens the Model tab at the card, where editing stays. On a phone the row labels and the headings stay in view while the table scrolls inside its card. `studio/tests/grid.mjs` compares each of the 2,385 cells and 155 scalars of the examples with what Forge returns (and with a scenario applied), the heading formulas, the formula a tap shows, and that the card is brought into view.
- **Forge Studio suggests names and functions while a formula is typed** (roadmap S10). In the formula field of a card, and in the form that adds an element, a strip above the field offers what the word being typed could be: the names of the model as the formula would write them (a name of the same group or table bare, any other as `group.name`, `table.column` or `@file.group.name`), then Forge's functions (with their syntax in the tooltip). A tap inserts exactly the suggestion's text and the keyboard stays. The names come from the model as Forge reads it and the functions from Forge's own list (the 169 of the `functions` tool), not from a list in the page, so a function Forge does not have is never offered. `studio/tests/suggest.mjs` types the first three letters of each of the 328 names and functions in the 113 formulas of the examples, and checks that each is offered and completes to the same word, that only Forge's functions are offered, that nothing is offered inside a text or for a number, and that a formula built with the strip is saved as typed.

### Added
- **Forge Studio adds and deletes model elements on its cards** (roadmap S9, ADR-056 addendum). Each group of inputs and results has an Add button for a new input (a number, with an optional unit) or a new formula, and each table one for a new column calculated by a formula; every input, formula and column has a Delete button, with a confirmation. The new lines are written at the end of the group, in the indentation and the style the file already uses, and a deletion removes the element's lines and nothing else (a comment above it stays). As for editing a formula, the change is kept only when the model still validates and Forge reads back exactly the model before with that one element added or gone; a name already in the model, a formula Forge refuses, or deleting an input other formulas use shows Forge's message, and the file is as it was. `studio/tests/elements.mjs` adds an input and a formula to every group, and a column to every table, of every example (64 elements in 7 sets), deletes each again, and checks byte for byte that only the new lines appeared and that the file came back; `studio/tests/text.mjs` covers the ways a file is written (indentation, block sequences, comments, units that need quotes, the top level).

- **Forge Studio works offline and can be added to the Home Screen.** Once Forge is running, the page keeps all its files (the page, Forge's WebAssembly, the examples) on the device with a service worker, so it opens and calculates with no connection, in a hospital, a tunnel or a plane. On an iPhone, Share, Add to Home Screen puts it on the Home Screen with its own icon and opens it without Safari's bars (`manifest.webmanifest`, `apple-touch-icon.png`). A new release is a new cache, taken over only when Studio is next opened after being closed, so the page and Forge always come from the same release. `studio/tests/offline.mjs` checks in CI, on the WebAssembly build, that every file of the site is kept, that with the connection cut the page opens in the same state as online with no failed request, and that a later release replaces the cache.
- **Eight posts in `docs/posts/`, one question each, answered with the examples.** The price that makes the bagel year pay (15.97 on the plan, 18.32 for a four-in-five chance), which months lose money, the second shop now or a pop-up first, the slow week (rain or roadwork), what waiting a year on the lease is worth, how sure an average day is, what 300 more a month does to a mortgage, and where October's money went. Each post runs the command on a file in `examples/` and shows what Forge printed; `tests/posts.rs` runs those commands and fails if a post stops matching the output. Writing them found a misleading line in `examples/bagel/README.md`: July and August (rows 9 and 10) are the months that reliably make money, not July to September. No behaviour of Forge changes, so this does not move the stable date.

## [10.0.0-rc.15] - 2026-11-11

The Excel workbook in Forge Studio, saving to Files and iCloud Drive, and numbers with at most 15 significant digits everywhere Forge writes or prints them. No wrong answer was fixed since rc.13 (the rounding changes how a number is shown, not its value to 15 digits), so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md still runs from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Added
- **Forge Studio saves to Files and iCloud Drive.** On the Files tab, Save opens the share sheet with the model's files (the main one and the files it includes), and the workbook's Save does the same with the `.xlsx` `forge export` writes; on an iPhone or iPad, Save to Files puts them in iCloud Drive or any folder. Where a browser cannot share files, they are downloaded as before. Safari opens the share sheet only while it is handling the tap, so the workbook is made when the Files tab opens and shared at once. `studio/tests/save.mjs` checks with a recording share sheet that the right files are shared, that the workbook is `forge export`'s part by part, and that both calls happen within the tap (a wait for anything outside it fails the test).
- **Excel export and import in Forge Studio** (roadmap S8-EXCEL-IN-BROWSER). The Files tab downloads the model as the workbook `forge export` writes, and opens an `.xlsx` file, which Forge imports into a model as `forge import` does. `rust_xlsxwriter` and `calamine` build for the browser (with `rust_xlsxwriter`'s `wasm` feature, so a workbook's creation time comes from the browser's clock), and `forge-wasm` gains the tools `export` and `import`. The workbook Studio downloads for the bagel and mortgage examples is the one `forge export` writes: the same parts, each the same byte for byte, apart from the time it was made, to the second, in `docProps/core.xml`; made in the same second, the two files are equal byte for byte. `studio/tests/excel.mjs` checks this in CI on the WebAssembly build, and that `forge export`'s bagel workbook, opened in Studio, calculates to `year.profit` 786.32.
- **`ExcelImporter::from_bytes`, `export_bytes_core` and `import_text_core`**: a workbook read from memory, and export and import without files, with the same workbook, model and warnings as `forge export` and `forge import` (a test imports the same workbook from a file and from its bytes and compares). They build with the `excel` feature alone, without the command line.

### Changed
- **Numbers Forge writes or prints have at most 15 significant digits, as in Excel, so binary rounding noise no longer shows.** `forge calculate` stored `418.3299999999999` for a sum of prices that is 418.33, and `2170.9700000000003`; the bagel dry run printed `year.worst_month_profit = -7935.533406400002`. The engine calculates in full `f64` precision, about 17 digits, and the last one or two are noise from binary fractions (`0.1 + 0.2` is `0.30000000000000004`). Excel calculates the same way and shows 15 digits. Forge now does too, at its edges only: the values `forge calculate` writes into a file, the terminal output of `calculate` and `compare`, `csv` and `json` of `forge report` and `forge compare`, the `--output` files (JSON or YAML) of `scenarios`, `simulate`, `decision-tree`, `real-options`, `tornado`, `bootstrap`, `bayesian` and `variance`, the YAML `forge import` writes, and the MCP and REST API results. One function does it, `core::precision::to_15_digits`, and `core::precision::Rounded` for serialised results. The calculation is not rounded: a formula still gets every digit of the one before, so no result changes by more than one part in 10^15. Exported `.xlsx` files keep full precision, as Excel's own files do. `examples/quota_forecast.yaml` and `examples/scenarios.yaml`, the two examples that store results, now store them to 15 digits. `tests/float_noise.rs` fails if a value stored in `examples/` has more than 15 digits, if calculating any example writes or returns one, or if the bagel dry run prints the noise again; it also checks that the engine still keeps the noise between formulas (`(14.11 + 404.22) - 418.33` is 5.68e-14, not 0). This is not a wrong answer under docs/STABILITY.md (the numbers were right to 15 digits), so it does not move the stable date.

### Fixed
- **A new push to a pull request cancels the CI and Validation runs it supersedes.** Both workflows now share a concurrency group per pull request with `cancel-in-progress` for pull requests only, so a rebase or a fix push no longer leaves the old run using up the 20 concurrent jobs of the Free plan. Runs on `main` are never cancelled.
- **`forge --version` says which commit a build came from.** A build of main said `forge 10.0.0-rc.11`, the same as the release, though it had code the release did not, so a bug report could not say which code it came from. A build of any commit other than a release now says `forge 10.0.0-rc.13 (a8ec1db)`, or `(a8ec1db, modified)` with uncommitted changes; a release, a crates.io build and any build without Git history say the plain version, as before. It is text read at build time by `build.rs`, not a `-dev` version in `Cargo.toml`: Auto Release tags a version change, so a `-dev` version on main would publish a release for every merge. The release workflow sets `FORGE_RELEASE` as well, and the install checks that compare `forge --version` with the version still pass.
- **The MCP tool `forge_export` without a path did not say which formulas it wrote as values.** Exported to a file, it lists each formula Excel cannot express that was written as its value; exported to memory (`excel_base64`), it listed only whether the model calculates. Both now give the same warnings.
- **Forge Studio was not published after rc.14.** The Pages workflow waited for the Release workflow to finish (`workflow_run`), but a release started by Auto Release runs with GitHub's own token, and its end starts no other workflow. The Release workflow now starts the Pages workflow itself, with the release's tag, once the GitHub release exists.

## [10.0.0-rc.14] - 2026-11-11

The first release with Forge Studio, a web page that runs Forge itself in the browser and is published on GitHub Pages with each release (ADR-055, ADR-056). No wrong answer was fixed since rc.13, so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md still runs from rc.13 (no earlier than 2026-11-24). The command line, MCP and the REST API give the same results; `forge compare`, `forge goal-seek` and `forge tornado` print their tables differently (below).

### Added
- **A formula can be edited on its card in Forge Studio** (roadmap S7-CARD-FORMULAS): scalars, table columns and the formulas of included files have an Edit button. The new formula is written into its one line of the YAML, in the way the file already writes it (`"=..."`, `'=...'`, plain, `{formula: ...}` or a `formula:` line), keeping the key, a comment and the rest of a flow mapping; plain text that YAML would misread is put in double quotes. It is kept only when Forge accepts it: the model validates (for a model with distributions, a ten-run simulation evaluates every formula), and Forge reads back exactly the formula typed and nothing else changed. Otherwise Forge's message is shown and the file is as it was. `studio/tests/formulas.mjs` changes every formula of every example on its card (113 in 7 sets) and checks byte for byte that only its line changed, by the two parentheses added, and that four invalid formulas are refused with Forge's message and no change; `studio/tests/text.mjs` covers the spellings the examples lack (comments after a formula, flow mappings, quoting).
- **Forge Studio colours formulas with the VS Code extension's rules, from one copy** (roadmap S6-HIGHLIGHT-ONE-SOURCE, ADR-056 decision 4): the build reads the patterns of `editors/vscode/syntaxes/forge-formula.injection.json`, in their order, into `formula-rules.json`, and the editor uses them as they are (matching as TextMate does: the earliest match, then the first rule). The hand-made copy this replaces had drifted in three places: an extra `@` allowed inside an included name, a number could start inside a name, and a function name had no word boundary before it. `studio/tests/highlight.mjs` fails when the built rules differ from the grammar's patterns or their order, when the grammar gains a scope Studio has no colour for or a pattern JavaScript cannot run, when highlighting any YAML file of `examples/` (26 files, 222 formulas) changes the text once its tags are removed, or when a formula does not get the grammar's classes. Only the colour of each scope and the YAML around formulas (keys, comments, strings) are Studio's own.
- **Forge Studio puts the YAML and the cards side by side on wide screens** (roadmap S5-SPLIT-VIEW, ADR-056 decision 3). Above 900 px the editor is on the left and Model, Analyze, Tools and Files on the right, each side scrolling on its own, with a divider that can be dragged (or moved with the arrow keys) and buttons to hide either side; the width and the choice are kept in the browser. Forge reads the editor's text as it is typed, after a pause, so a number typed there changes its card, and a number changed on a card shows in the editor. Below 900 px nothing changes: one view at a time, tabs at the bottom. `studio/tests/layout.mjs` checks in CI that no view scrolls sideways at 320 px, and at 1440 x 900 that typing in the editor changes the card, a card changes the editor, the divider moves and either side hides; it saves screenshots at 390 x 844 and 1440 x 900 in light and dark as the `studio-screenshots` artifact.
- **Forge Studio is published on GitHub Pages after each release** (roadmap S4-PAGES, ADR-056 decision 6): the workflow `pages.yml` follows the Release workflow, builds `forge-wasm` and Studio at the release's tag, deploys them, and then opens the published address in a headless browser and checks that the page shows that release's version and the bagel example's `year.profit` 786.32 (`studio/tests/live.mjs`). It can also be started by hand with a tag to publish a release that already happened. Pull requests still only build Studio as an artifact. Pages is switched on once, in the repository's settings (Source: GitHub Actions); until then the deploy step fails and nothing else is affected.
- **Forge Studio, in `studio/`** (roadmap S3-STUDIO-PORT, ADR-056): a phone-first web page that opens the repository's examples (bagel, mortgage, monthly plan, DCF, household, rentals, starter) and shows each model as cards, with scenarios, goal seek and break-even, a tornado, simulation with a seed, budget against actual, the 20 MCP tools run on the open model, and a YAML editor whose formula colours follow the VS Code extension. Every number comes from Forge through `forge-wasm`, in a background worker: the page reads no YAML and calculates nothing (`studio/tests/no-engine.mjs`), and changing an input on a card changes that line of the YAML and nothing else. In CI a headless browser opens each example and checks that every scalar on screen is `forge calculate`'s for that model, and the same with a scenario applied (`studio/tests/screen.mjs`). The mockup's JavaScript engine, which matched Forge on the examples but was a second engine (ADR-055), is not carried in. Not published yet (S4). See studio/README.md.
- **`model`, a forge-wasm tool for Studio**: the model as read, before calculation, with each scalar's input value or formula, each table's columns in the file's order, and the included files' scalars.
- **Forge's tools in WebAssembly** (roadmap S2-FORGE-WASM, ADR-055): a crate `forge-wasm` in this repository's workspace, not published, for Forge Studio (ADR-056). One function, `call(tool, request)`, takes the model's files and a tool's arguments as JSON and answers with that tool's result: validate, calculate, audit, scenarios, compare, goal seek, break-even, sensitivity, tornado, simulate, probability seek, variance, report, decision tree, real options, bootstrap, Bayesian networks, functions, examples and schema. Each runs the same core function as the command line and MCP. Every case in `forge-wasm/tests/cases.json` (31, all on `examples/`) gives the same answer from files in memory as from disk, and CI compares the WebAssembly build's answers with the native ones. The module's size is in that job's summary. See forge-wasm/README.md.
- **`parser::with_files_in_memory(files, f)`**: inside `f`, `parse_model` and the commands' core functions read the given files instead of the disk, includes too. `parser::read_model_text` reads one file that way. `mollendorff_forge::VERSION` is Forge's version.

### Changed
- **The commands' core functions build without the `cli` feature** (`calculate_core`, `simulate_core` and the rest), so the browser runs the same code as the command line. Watch, update, export and import still need `cli`; writing a variance or simulation report to `.xlsx` needs `excel` and says so without it. `colored` is no longer optional. The command line's output is unchanged.
- **The engine builds without the command line, the servers and Excel** (roadmap S1-CORE-NO-OS, ADR-055), the first step towards running Forge in a browser. A new default feature, `cli`, holds the command line, the MCP server, the REST API and Excel (`excel`); `cargo build --lib --no-default-features` builds the parser, the calculator and the analysis engines alone, and CI builds that for `wasm32-unknown-unknown`. `cargo install mollendorff-forge`, `cargo build` and the release binaries are unchanged: on every example, the same 74 commands (calculate, validate, scenarios, simulate, export and the rest) gave the same output before and after, apart from timings, file paths and the workbook's creation time.
- **A model can be read from memory**: `parser::parse_model_text(name, text, files)` takes the main model's text and the texts of the files it includes, by the path written in `_includes`, and reads nothing from disk. It gives the same model and the same results as `parse_model` on the same files, including an include of an include (tests in `src/parser/memory_tests.rs`).
- **`jsonschema` no longer fetches schemas over HTTP.** Forge's schemas are built in and never referred to the web, so nothing changes for a model; Cargo.lock loses 54 crates of HTTP and TLS code.
- **`forge compare` fits its table to the terminal, and takes `--format table|csv|json|markdown`.** With eight scenarios and long names, the header ran the names together (`rent_1500_prosupport_not_recognized`) and a long scalar name such as `bankruptcy.monthly_surplus_payment` pushed its values out of their columns: every column was 20 or 15 characters, padded with the colour codes counted in. Each column now takes its width from what it holds, with two spaces between columns. A table wider than the terminal (`COLUMNS`, or the terminal's width; a file or a pipe is not wrapped) is printed in blocks of columns that fit, one row per scalar, or one row per scenario when that needs fewer blocks. The numbers follow `forge report`'s rule (`1,450.00`, whole numbers whole, an input as written, so `27637.935076` is `27,637.94`), and the scalars come in the model's order, as in the MCP tool, instead of alphabetical. `csv` and `json` print the numbers at full precision and nothing else; `json` is the MCP tool's result. The formats share `forge report`'s code.
- **A scenario with `scalars` and no `probability` was refused with an error about something else, and `forge compare --help` showed one form only.** Removing `probability` from a scenario with `description` and `scalars` gave `'scenarios.rent_1100.description' must be a number, not the text ...`, because the file was then read as the flat form. `forge compare` weighs nothing and needs no probability, so the full form is now accepted without it (the schemas of 5.0.0, 5.1.0 and 5.2.0 are widened; the model format does not change, and a misspelt `probabilty` is still caught). `forge scenarios` does weigh by it and now says `scenario 'optimistic' has no probability` and that they must sum to 1, where it said `missing field`. `forge compare --help` shows the flat and the full form (one of them for all the scenarios of a file), and an output copied from a run.
- **Numbers in the output of `forge goal-seek` and `forge tornado` follow `forge report`'s rule, and `forge compare` prints the scenarios' descriptions.** The solution of a goal-seek printed as `living.rent = 1148.273882`, a tornado bar as `swing 0.18` They now print two decimals and thousands separators (`397.04`, `80,000.00`, `-1,125,000.00`) and a number under 1 keeps four significant digits (`swing 0.063`, not `0.06`). JSON and files keep the raw values. The `description` of a scenario (the full form) was read and dropped: `forge compare` prints it in a legend below the table (a list in Markdown), and the JSON result, which is the MCP tool's, has a `descriptions` field when a scenario has one.

### Fixed
- **Forge Studio no longer shows an exchange rate of 1.38 as 138%.** It decided a number was a percentage from its name alone (rate, growth, margin, share, pct, percent) for any value up to 5, so `exchange_rate 1.38` showed 138%, `hourly_rate 4.5` 450% and `margin 3` 300%, while `renewal_increase 0.04` showed 0.04. The numbers underneath were Forge's and right; the display was not. Now the model's unit decides: `%`, `percent`, `percentage` or `pct` shows the value (a fraction, as `VARIANCE_PCT` gives it) as a percentage, and any other unit never does. With no unit, the name decides by whole words (so `corporate_tax` is no longer read as a rate), now also `increase`, `inflation` and `wacc`, and only for values of 1 or less. One function, `isPercent` in `studio/format.js`, drives every card, hint, slider, table, report and analysis. On the examples nothing that was a percentage stops being one; the rent increases of the rentals example and the DCF's WACC now show as percentages. A percentage typed into a field ("4.5 %") is read as hundredths, 0.045, instead of being written as 4.5.
- **Forge Studio can change an input written with a negative exponent or in hex.** An input such as `1e-5`, `1.5E+3` or `0x10` gave "Not changed": the card's pattern did not have every number Forge reads. It now has the ones Forge accepts (a sign, a point, an exponent, 0x, 0o and 0b integers), in all three ways an input is written. `1_000`, `.inf` and `1,000` are not numbers to Forge, so they stay out.
- **The bagel example's `payback_months` is `first_year_return`.** In the base case `year.payback_months` was 1,449.79, about 120 years, and negative when the year lost money, so `forge scenarios` reported a range of -67.28 to 1,449.79 with an expected value of 717.79: a negative payback and an average of paybacks mean nothing. A payback that says "never" has to be an error or text, and `forge scenarios` and `compare` cannot carry either (the first one fails with `result is not a number`). The scalar is now the first year's profit divided by the fit-out (`year.first_year_return`, 0.83% in the base case, -17.8% under the flour shock), which is defined whatever the profit and can be weighed by probability. `bagel.yaml`, `actual.yaml` and `bagel_simulate.yaml` change; the engine does not. The tornado output in the README is the real output, with the separators of the last release.

## [10.0.0-rc.13] - 2026-11-10

One more wrong answer in `forge export` fixed, so the two-week stable clock of docs/STABILITY.md restarts here (no earlier than 2026-11-24).

### Fixed
- **`forge export` wrote formulas over text that looks like a number as they were, and Excel reads such text its own way.** With `s: ["5", "apple", "2", "x"]`: `SUM(t.s)` was 7 in Forge and 0 in Gnumeric and LibreOffice, `COUNTIF(t.s, ">3")` 1 and 0, and `IFERROR(IF(s > 3, 1, 0), -1)` 1, -1, 0, -1 in Forge and 1 in every row in both (Excel puts any text above every number). The same held for a column of months (`2026-01`) compared with a date. A formula that reads a number from text with a digit in it, that orders text with `<`, `>`, `<=` or `>=`, or that reads a number from text made in the formula (`LEFT(code, 2) * 1`), is now written as its values, with a warning; one that reads text as text stays a formula, and so does a column of words read as numbers (nothing, in both). `forge import` applies the same rule to a workbook's own formulas. Forge's own results do not change. A wrong answer under docs/STABILITY.md: the next release candidate restarts the two-week clock (ADR-054, decision 6, amended; roadmap T1-TEXT-NUMBERS).
- **`forge functions` listed six names a formula cannot call.** `Y`, `M`, `D`, `YD`, `YM` and `MD` are the units of `DATEDIF(a, b, "YD")`, not functions: `=YD(a, b)` was an unknown function. The list, the MCP `forge_functions` tool and every count are now 169, of which six are FP&A-native. A test calls every listed function by name. It also found that `=IRR()` with no arguments panicked; it now says `IRR requires cash flows` (roadmap T1-FUNCTION-LIST).
- **`forge import` said "Import Complete" and said nothing about two things it could not keep.** A column of numbers with text in it (`n/a`, a note) came in with 0 in those cells; the warning now names them (`orders.amount: ... A3 (n/a), A5 (see note) ... imported as 0`). A column that reads its own total, a share of the total `=A2/SUM($A$2:$A$5)`, came in as a column formula that `forge calculate` refuses (`aggregations should be in scalars section`); it is now imported as the values the workbook shows, with a warning, and the model calculates to those values (roadmap T1-IMPORT-MIXED, T1-IMPORT-AGGREGATE).
- **`forge export` of a model that does not calculate said "Export Complete" and nothing else.** The formulas were then written without results, and what a formula column holds is known only from the results (ADR-054): `next: =EDATE(d, 1)` and `y4: =LEFT(next, 4)` were written as `=LEFT(B2, 4)`, 4608 in Excel where Forge, once the model calculates, holds 2026. The export still writes the file and exits 0, now with a warning that names the calculation's error (`Exported, but not checked` in the command; the first entry of `warnings` in the MCP tool, which has a new `warnings` field on the in-memory export too) (roadmap T1-EXPORT-UNCALCULATED).

## [10.0.0-rc.12] - 2026-11-10

Twelfth release candidate, for wrong answers in `forge export` and `forge import` found while making dates real dates in Excel (ADR-054, below). Under docs/STABILITY.md that restarts the two-week clock: the stable release is no earlier than 2026-11-24. It is also the first release with `forge report`, the starter model, dates as real dates in Excel, and installation with Homebrew or cargo-binstall; the README described the first three before they were published. Known and not fixed: text that is a number is read as a number where Excel reads text (roadmap T1-TEXT-NUMBERS), which restarts the clock again when it is fixed.

### Fixed
Found while making dates real dates in Excel (ADR-054). Each was a wrong answer under docs/STABILITY.md, so the next release candidate restarts the two-week clock.
- **Formulas over a date column gave other answers in Excel than in Forge.** `forge export` wrote a date as text, and Excel does not read text as Forge reads a date. With four dates in `t.d`: `=IF(d > DATE(2026, 6, 1), 1, 0)` was 0, 0, 1, 0 in Forge and 1, 1, 1, 1 in Gnumeric and LibreOffice (text is greater than any number there); `=MIN(t.d)`, `=MAX(t.d)` and `=SUM(t.d)` were the day numbers in Forge and 0 in both; `=COUNT(t.d)` 4 and 0; `=YEAR(MAX(t.d))` 2026 and 1899; `=SUMPRODUCT((t.d > DATE(2026, 2, 1)) * t.v)` 90 and 100; `=IF(EDATE(d, 0) = d, 1, 0)` 1 and 0. Dates are now written as dates, and all of these agree.
- **`+` joined text**: `"5" + 1` was `"51"` (Excel: 6), a date plus a day was `"2026-01-311"` (Excel: the next day), and `forge export` wrote both as `+`. `+` now adds, as in Excel: text that is a number or a date is that number, and other text is `#VALUE!` with a message that names `&` and `CONCAT`.
- **A date was not always a date in Forge's own calculation.** `MAX(d, DATE(2026, 6, 1))` in a row ignored the date `d` (so did `MIN`, `SUM` and `AVERAGE`), and `COUNT(d, e, n)` counted only the number, though the same functions over a column counted dates. `COUNTIF(t.d, ">2026-02-01")` and the other criteria with `>`, `<`, `>=`, `<=` matched no date. `d = DATE(2026, 1, 31)` was FALSE for that date. A date is now its day number wherever a number is read, and equal to it.
- **`TEXT` with a format it did not know gave a wrong string**: `TEXT(d, "yyyy")` was `"46053"`, `TEXT(1234.5, "#,##0.00")` was `"1234.50"`, `TEXT(1.2345, "0.000")` was `"1.234"` (Excel: `1.235`). `TEXT` now reads a defined part of Excel's format codes (digits `0` and `#`, a point, `,` between digits, `%`, `E+00`, literals, and dates with `yyyy`, `mmm`, `dddd`...), checked on 51 formats against Gnumeric and LibreOffice; any other code is an error that says which part is not read.
- **`forge import` read a date cell as text of its day number** (`"46053"`), dropped a date in a scalar's cell, and wrote 0 for a date in a column of numbers. A date cell is a date now, and a date-time or a time is Excel's number.
- **`forge import` read a formula over dates held as text as Forge reads dates.** In a workbook with `2026-01-31` in text cells, `=IF(A2 > DATE(2026, 6, 1), 1, 0)` is 1 in every row in Gnumeric and LibreOffice (text is greater than any number, which is Excel's rule too). It was imported as `=IF(d > DATE(2026, 6, 1), 1, 0)`, which Forge calculates as 0, 1, 1 for January, July and December. It is now imported as the values the workbook holds, with a warning.

### Added
- **Dates are dates in Excel** (T3-EXPORT-DATES, ADR-054). A column in which every value is a date from 1900-03-01 on, typed in or calculated (`EDATE(d, 1)`, a period column, `TODAY()`), is written as date cells: charts, filters and pivots see dates. Each formula reads a date the way Forge reads it there: the cell where a number is read; the date's text where text is read (`=LEFT(d, 4)` is written `=LEFT(TEXT(YEAR(A2) * 10000 + MONTH(A2) * 100 + DAY(A2), "0000-00-00"), 4)`, which gives `2026` in any language of Excel, as `TEXT(A2, "yyyy-mm-dd")` does not); `DATE(2026, 6, 1)` for a date written in the formula, also in a criterion (`">" & DATE(2026, 2, 1)`). A date read in a way that has no rule is written as values with a warning, never guessed. Months (`2026-01`), dates before 1900-03-01 and columns that mix dates with other text (`n/a` in some rows) stay text; a formula that reads a number from such a column (`YEAR(maturity)`, `maturity > DATE(2026, 6, 1)`, `MAX(loans.maturity)`) is written as its values with a warning, because Excel reads text there, and one that reads its text (`maturity = "n/a"`, `LEFT(maturity, 4)`) stays a formula. 68 new forge-e2e-gnumeric cases (`edge_real_dates.yaml`), with expected values from the same calculation in Python, are recalculated by Gnumeric (66) and LibreOffice (2). Not checked in Excel.
- **`&` joins text**, as in Excel: `=name & "-" & d`. It binds less than `+` and more than a comparison.
- **`forge import` reads a workbook's dates and the formulas over them.** A date cell comes in as `2026-01-31`, whatever format Excel showed it in. A formula is read only if it is the same calculation in Forge: Forge's own exported forms come back as the formulas they were (`=LEFT(d, 4)`, `=d > "2026-06-01"`, `=COUNTIF(t.d, ">2026-02-01")`), `=A2 + 30` shown as a date comes in as the number it is, and a workbook's own `=LEFT(A2, 4)` on a date cell (`4605` in Excel, `2026` in Forge) is imported as its values, with a warning. So is a formula that reads a number from dates held as text (`2026-01-31` in a text cell, as every Forge before this one wrote them): `=IF(A2 > DATE(2026, 6, 1), 1, 0)` is 1 in every row in a spreadsheet, where text is greater than any number, and was imported as a comparison of dates. A column that only reads the row above comes back as `PREV(value, first)`, as it was written (part of T3-IMPORT-PREV).
- **Install with Homebrew or cargo-binstall** (T3-DIST). This repository is a Homebrew tap: `brew tap mollendorff-ai/forge https://github.com/mollendorff-ai/forge` then `brew install mollendorff-ai/forge/forge` installs the prebuilt binary of the release on macOS and Linux. `Formula/forge.rb` is written by `scripts/homebrew-formula.sh` from the release's checksums, and the Release workflow rewrites and commits it after each release. `cargo binstall mollendorff-forge` downloads the release's archive instead of building, from the first release after rc.11 (crates.io carries the metadata from then on). A new Install workflow runs `brew install` (macOS, Linux) and `cargo binstall` (macOS, Linux, Windows) and checks that `forge --version` names the version: on a pull request that changes the packaging, from the formula and metadata of the pull request; after each release, as a user would, from the tap and from crates.io.
- **`forge report`** (T2-REPORT): a calculated model's scalars side by side, one row per group and one column per key, so comparing options needs no script. Groups with the same keys share a table, others get their own, in file order; inputs come before formulas. `--group`, `--keys`, `--no-inputs`, and `--format table|csv|json|markdown`: Markdown is a pipe table to paste into a document, CSV and JSON keep full precision. In a table, numbers have two decimals and thousands separators, and a number under 1 keeps four significant digits (a rate of 0.0479 is not shown as 0.05). `report` reads the values `forge calculate` wrote: it never calculates or writes the file, and a formula without a value is shown as `—` and named in a note on standard error. docs/REPORT.md. It is one of the commands docs/STABILITY.md keeps within 10.x.
- **`examples/options/compare-options.yaml`** (T2-COMPARE-EXAMPLE): five invented rental units with the same keys, measured against a take-home pay, for the flow `forge calculate` then `forge report --format markdown`. Its numbers go through `calculate` and `report` in `tests/demo_examples.rs` and equal the same calculation in Python.
- **A first model to start from** (T2-FIRST-MODEL): `forge examples starter` (and the MCP `forge_examples` tool, name `starter`) is a model with a group of scalars written `{formula: "=..."}` and a table with bare `"=..."` column formulas, each with a one-line comment saying why; it calculates as it is. docs/EXAMPLES.md opens with "Your first model": the two rules every first model breaks (`_forge_version` is the format version, not Forge's; a formula in a group of scalars is a mapping), then the starter, whose output the doc test checks. The MCP server's instructions say the same in one sentence.

### Changed
- **`forge report` shows whole numbers without decimals.** A column whose values are all whole prints `1,700`, `3`, `0` in `table` and `markdown`; one fractional value in a column keeps the decimals for every cell of it, so a column never mixes `3` and `3.50`. An input is shown as written where the usual rules would round it (`0.14975`, not `0.1498`). `csv` and `json` are unchanged. `--no-inputs` and `--keys` say in their help that they exclude each other, and `--help` and docs/REPORT.md say that `--keys` narrows a wide table. No hint is printed for a table wider than the terminal: the width is not known without a new dependency.
- **The starter says that a column cannot reduce a column** (T2-FIRST-MODEL). A fresh agent given only `forge examples starter` and asked for a five-plan comparison failed its first run: it put `MIN(plans.total_cost)` in a table column ("aggregations should be in scalars section"). The starter now has a column that reads a scalar total (`share_of_revenue: "=revenue / totals.revenue"`) and a comment on the rule, and "Your first model" has it as a third rule. Three fresh agents given the new starter and three different tasks (five car loans, a six-product shop, four job offers) each wrote a model that calculated on the first run.
- An unsupported `_forge_version` now ends with the line to write: `` write `_forge_version: "5.1.0"` for a new model (`"5.2.0"` if a table has a period column), or start from `forge examples starter` ``.

## [10.0.0-rc.11] - 2026-11-10

Eleventh release candidate, for two wrong answers in `forge import` (a running balance built in Excel came in as another formula; Excel's `ROW()` and `COLUMN()` were read as Forge's). Under docs/STABILITY.md that restarts the two-week clock: the stable release is no earlier than 2026-11-24. It also adds `PREV`, period columns and model format 5.2.0 (ADR-053).

### Fixed
- **`forge import` took the formula of the first data row for the whole column.** A running balance built in Excel (`=A2`, then `=B2+A3`, `=B3+A4`: 100, 150, 175) was imported as `balance: =amount` and calculated 100, 50, 25, without a warning. Import now reads every row. A column is a row formula only if every row holds the same formula; a column whose first row holds one formula and every other row another, which may read the row above, comes in as `=IF(ROW() = 1, amount, PREV(balance, 0) + amount)`; any other column of differing formulas is imported as its values, with a warning. This is a wrong answer under docs/STABILITY.md, so the next release candidate restarts the two-week clock.
- **`forge import` read Excel's `ROW()` and `COLUMN()` as Forge's**, which count differently: `=ROW()*A2` in a table gave 40, 90, 160 in Excel and 20, 60, 120 after import. Excel's `ROW()` in a table is now read as `(ROW() + 1)`, and a formula that uses a function Forge defines differently from Excel (`COLUMN`, `ROW` of a reference, `VLOOKUP`, `HLOOKUP`, `OFFSET`, `INDIRECT`, `NOW`, `UNIQUE`, `SORT`, `FILTER`, `SEQUENCE`, `RANDARRAY`) is imported as its value, with a warning. `forge export` already refused to write those; import did not refuse to read them.

### Added
- **Periods and the row above** (T2-TIME, ADR-053). Three things a table over time needed:
  - **`PREV(value, first, [back])`** reads the row above in a row formula: `opening: "=PREV(closing, loan.principal)"`, a running total `=PREV(total, 0) + amount`, growth `=PREV(sales * 1.01, 100)`. `first` is what the first row gets and is required; `back` reads further up. Columns that read each other through `PREV` (an opening and a closing balance) are calculated together, row by row; within a row a cycle is still an error, and so is a cycle through a scalar or another table. A model without `PREV` is calculated in the order it always was: 56 of the 62 outputs of the comparison corpus are byte for byte those of rc.10, the other six being the three examples that changed and three error messages that now list format 5.2.0.
  - **A period column**, `month: {start: 2026-12, count: 300, unit: month}`, is the column of the dates the periods start on (`day`, `week`, `month`, `quarter`, `year`), and gives the table its rows. It is an ordinary date column for `YEAR`, `MONTH` and `INDEX`.
  - **`ROW()`**, the number of the row, is now exported as `(ROW() - 1)` instead of as a value.
- **Model format 5.2.0**: 5.1.0 plus the period column, and nothing else. 5.1.0 and 5.0.0 files are unchanged and stay valid; a file needs 5.2.0 only if a table has a period column, and one that has it under an older version is told so. `forge schema v5.2`, `forge upgrade --to 5.2.0` (a 5.1.0 file only changes its version line). `schema/forge.schema.json` is one schema for editors that checks a file against the schema of the format it names; the VS Code extension (4.1.0) bundles it, and its test validates the examples of both formats and a mistaken period column with yaml-language-server.
- **`forge export` writes `PREV` as cells that read the row above** (`=('loan'!$B$2)` in the first row, `=(G2)` in the next), the way a schedule is built by hand, so it needs nothing Excel 2007 lacks.
- **Three examples**, each checked against the same calculation in Python (`tests/demo_examples.rs`): `examples/mortgage` now holds the loan month by month for 300 months (payment 247 is the last, in June 2047; its interest equals the closed form's, and its balances match a Python loop in months 1, 12, 60, 120, 246 and 247), `examples/periods/monthly_plan.yaml` (growth, seasons, running cash) and `examples/periods/dcf.yaml` (a five-year valuation). Gnumeric and LibreOffice recalculate the three exported workbooks to the same numbers. 14 new forge-e2e-gnumeric cases on `PREV`, `ROW()` and a period column, with expected values worked out by hand, are recalculated by Gnumeric. 175 functions.

### Changed
- The DCF model in the README uses a period column and `PREV`; it calculates to the same numbers as before, in the base case, the `bull` scenario and the sensitivity table.

## [10.0.0-rc.10] - 2026-11-09

Tenth release candidate, for one wrong answer: `forge export` gave a column shorter than its table the table's rows, so `ROWS` and `INDEX` over it gave other results in Excel than in Forge. Under docs/STABILITY.md that restarts the two-week clock: the stable release is no earlier than 2026-11-23. It also writes `LET`, `LAMBDA` called at once, `COUNTUNIQUE` and `TODAY` as Excel formulas, and forge-e2e-gnumeric now recalculates in LibreOffice the 68 cases Gnumeric cannot read.

### Fixed
- **`forge export` gave a column shorter than its table the table's rows.** In `d: {long: [1, 2, 3, 4, 5], short: [7, 8]}`, `ROWS(d.short)` is 2 in Forge and was written as `ROWS('d'!$B$2:$B$6)`, which is 5 in Excel; `INDEX(d.short, ROWS(d.short))` read an empty cell (0 in Gnumeric, 8 in Forge), and the new `COUNTUNIQUE` form counted the empty cells. A column's range now ends at its own last value. Found when LibreOffice counted 8 distinct values in a column of 8 with a repeat (forge-e2e-gnumeric `countunique_small_set`); a new test checks the formula written. This is a wrong answer under docs/STABILITY.md, so the next release candidate restarts the two-week clock.

### Added
- **`forge export` writes `LET`, a `LAMBDA` called where it is written, `COUNTUNIQUE` and `TODAY` as Excel formulas** (T2-EXPORT-LET); they were values, so those cells did not follow their inputs in Excel. `LET` keeps its names, stored as Excel stores them (`_xlpm.x`). `LAMBDA(p, r, n, p * (1 + r)^n)(1000, 0.05, 10)` is written as the `LET` that computes the same (the values first, then the body), which Excel 2021 can also read; a name Excel refuses (`r`, `c`, `x1`), one used twice in the formula, or one that is also a value the formula reads gets `_` added (`r_`), and a `LET` that is a whole value or calculation of another is merged into it. `COUNTUNIQUE` of a column in a scalar is `ROWS(UNIQUE(range))` for numbers and an `EXACT` count for text, since Excel's `UNIQUE` ignores case and Forge does not. `TODAY()` is written where its result is a number; a table column of `TODAY()`, which is text in Forge, stays values. `forge import` reads all of them back (a `LAMBDA` as its `LET`, the two `COUNTUNIQUE` forms as `COUNTUNIQUE`).
- **forge-e2e-gnumeric recalculates in LibreOffice what Gnumeric cannot read.** A case with a `libreoffice` key (the reason) is recalculated by LibreOffice 24.8 or later, run with a profile that recalculates every formula when the file is loaded. 68 cases moved there and pass: the 32 `LET`, `LAMBDA` and `COUNTUNIQUE` cases, 24 `DATEDIF`, 4 `XLOOKUP`, 5 `ISFORMULA`, a comparison at 15 digits and two financial results where Gnumeric differs from Excel. The 4 `TODAY` cases are now recalculated by Gnumeric. Of the 40 cases checked against Forge only, 4 remain: `PRICEDISC` and `ACCRINT` results checked by hand in Excel, where LibreOffice 26.2 agrees with Gnumeric and not with Excel. `test_today_year` expected 2026, so it would have failed from 2027; it checks a range of years now. The Validation workflow installs LibreOffice from noble-backports (Ubuntu 24.04 has 24.2, without `LET`).

## [10.0.0-rc.9] - 2026-11-09

Ninth release candidate: new functions, commands and editor support since rc.8, and no wrong answer, so the two-week clock in docs/STABILITY.md keeps running from rc.8 (stable no earlier than 2026-11-23). Highlights: `SUMPRODUCT`, table columns read from included files (`@prices.list.per_kg`), error messages that say what is wrong and where, a schema that reads a model the way Forge does, a VS Code extension that works without a server, and worked examples (mortgage, household budget) checked against independent calculations.

### Added
- **A security policy** (`SECURITY.md`): how to report a vulnerability privately (security@mollendorff.ai or GitHub's private reporting), which versions get fixes (the latest release only), what to expect (a reply within 14 days, best effort) and what is in scope. GitHub now shows it under the repository's Security tab.
- **`SUMPRODUCT`** (T2-SUMPRODUCT): `=SUMPRODUCT(orders.qty, orders.price)` multiplies 1 to 255 arrays element by element and adds up, the way Excel does: text in an array counts as 0, and arrays of different lengths are an error that names both lengths (`#VALUE!` in Excel). It also takes one array, or a computed one: `SUMPRODUCT((orders.region = "N") * orders.qty * orders.price)`. `forge export` writes it as `SUMPRODUCT` over the columns' ranges, so it follows its inputs in Excel; Gnumeric recalculates the five new forge-e2e-gnumeric cases (two, one and three arrays, a text column, a condition times columns) and they match. 174 functions. A unit test that expected it to be unknown is now one that checks its result.
- **Table columns from included files** (T2-INCLUDE-COLUMNS): `@prices.list.per_kg` reads the column `per_kg` of the table `list` of the file included `as: prices`, with its formula columns calculated, so a price list or a rate curve can live in a file of its own.
